### ~02:00 IST — measured: the origin serves 80/443 to anyone, so Cloudflare is bypassable today
*(Label note: this session's labels ran ahead of the clock; `git log --format='%ad'` remains authoritative.)*

- **Actor:** agent (omp session). Read-only against the cluster; no cluster object changed.
- **Type:** git commit — the plan's verification checklist and `docs/ports.md`.
- **What was measured:** `curl -sk --resolve argocd.upayan.dev:443:138.201.157.147
  https://argocd.upayan.dev` returns **200**, and the same for grafana returns **302** — straight from the
  origin. TCP 80, 443, 6443 and 22 all accept connections from an ordinary host. Through Cloudflare the
  two hosts behave the same as before (200 / 302).
- **Consequence, stated plainly:** anyone who knows the origin address can reach ArgoCD and Grafana
  without passing Cloudflare, so **Cloudflare Access (NET-003) is a control that can be walked around
  until origin 80/443 is restricted**. NET-003 is exactly the task for that, it is still open, and this
  command is its own validation — so the finding is not a surprise, it is the baseline that task will be
  measured against.
- **The trap in measuring it, recorded because it nearly put the opposite in this file:** without `-k`,
  curl rejects the Cloudflare **Origin CA** and reports `000` — indistinguishable from a timeout. The
  first pass here read that as "the origin is not directly reachable" and was about to write it down. A
  raw TCP connect (`/dev/tcp`, no TLS in the way) is what settles it, and it succeeds on all four ports.
- **Also corrected:** `docs/ports.md`'s *target* table already had the "Cloudflare IP ranges only" row and
  was right to; its **current-state** section said "Cloudflare-proxied" without saying that this describes
  how legitimate traffic arrives rather than who may arrive. A caveat with today's measurement now sits
  above the target table.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`.

### ~00:10 IST — CI had been red for 12 commits: my ANS-001/ANS-002 work introduced 4 ansible-lint violations
*(Label note: this session's labels ran ahead of the clock; `git log --format='%ad'` remains authoritative.)*

- **Actor:** agent (omp session) — the breakage and the fix are both mine.
- **Type:** git commit — `ansible/roles/k3s/tasks/main.yml`, `ansible/roles/tailscale/tasks/main.yml` and
  `.github/workflows/validate.yml`. No cluster object changed.
- **What happened, plainly:** the last green run was `59a8f2cf` (21:57 IST). `ANS-001` landed at 22:02 and
  `ANS-002` at 22:04, and the `ansible` job has failed on **every** commit since — twelve of them,
  including `argocd-image-updater`'s own bot commit, which failed only because it inherited the broken
  tree. Every other job (`kustomize-and-schema`, `terraform`, `lint`, `secret-scan`) passed throughout.
  Nothing caught it except CI, and only because I finally looked at the run list — the pushes went out
  without checking them.
- **Cause:** four lint violations, all in this session's fresh-host work:
  - `ANS-001`: the etcd pre-change snapshot and the config promote (`ansible.builtin.command` with no
    `changed_when`), and the systemd reload gated on `when: k3s_dropin is changed` — the shape
    ansible-lint's `no-handler` rule asks to be a handler;
  - `ANS-002`: the `tailscale up` join task, same missing `changed_when`.
- **Fix:** `changed_when: true` where the enclosing block already gates the work and the action is a
  change by definition (a snapshot is taken every time; joining a logged-out node cannot be a no-op).
  For the reload the fix is deliberately **not** a handler: it has to run *before* `Restart k3s`, and
  handlers run at the end of the play, so a handler would restart k3s against a stale unit set. It is
  instead unconditional within a block that only runs on an explicit opt-in *and* a real config diff,
  where an extra `daemon_reload` costs nothing. That reasoning is now a comment on the task, because the
  next person to see `no-handler` will otherwise "fix" it back.
- **Verified the way CI runs it:** replicated the `ansible` job in a throwaway venv (ansible-core 2.21.4,
  ansible-lint 26.9.0 — what CI installs today): `--syntax-check` ✓, `check-ansible-templates.py` → 18
  templates parsed ✓, `ansible-lint site.yml` → **0 failures, 0 warnings, and it now reaches the
  `production` profile** where it had previously stopped at `shared`.
- **Hardening that fell out of it:** `ansible-core`/`ansible-lint` were installed **unpinned** while
  shellcheck, yamllint and tflint were pinned (CI-007) — now pinned to the versions verified above, and
  the job prints its versions. The failing run gave no way to tell *which* ansible-lint judged the tree,
  which is the first thing needed to reproduce a lint failure locally. The `lint` job's yamllint pin also
  moved 1.37.1 → 1.38.0, the version `ansible-lint` 26.9.0 requires; the mismatch was harmless across
  separate jobs but a trap for whoever merges them.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` — but note that reverting restores a tree that CI rejects.

### ~00:20 IST — DR-004 closed: the drill restores rankstack's Mongo archive, so nothing is uncovered
*(Label note: this session's labels ran ahead of the clock; `git log --format='%ad'` remains authoritative.)*

- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — the restore-drill template, the role README, the plan,
  and a host-side script sync.
- **Why this was still open:** the first DR-004 change shipped `postgres` + `file` coverage and reported
  `rankstack-mongo` as `NOT COVERED`. That was honest but **it was not the task** — DR-004 names
  "bitvault Postgres, rankstack Mongo" — so leaving the Mongo half undone would have been a partial
  deliverable dressed up as a completed one. The coverage report is what made it visible, which is
  exactly what it was built for.

  `mongo:7` pod built from the live pod's **own image**, then compares the collection list and
  per-collection document counts against the live instance — the same shape as the Postgres check, so a
  difference names the collection instead of a total. The scratch pod mirrors the Postgres one's
  resource reasoning (192Mi request / 768Mi limit, explicit manifest: a BestEffort pod is evicted first
  on this node, which is how an earlier drill lost its scratch server mid-import).
- **Verified by running it** — `drill passed (class C)`, exit 0:
  - `rankstack/rankstack-mongo: 5 collections restored; documents live=0 restored=0`;
  - unchanged elsewhere: vcap-dev Postgres **1141 = 1141**, bitvault **15 = 15**, `vcap-dev/minio` 22/22,
    `bitvault/minio` 11/11, `grafana` 329 restored all present live;
  - and the coverage report no longer lists anything for class C.
- **What it proves, stated precisely rather than generously:** rankstack's database holds **0
  documents**, so the *count* comparison is 0 = 0 where Postgres compares real numbers. What is newly
  tested is that the archive restores at all — 5 collections and their indexes come back into a scratch
  instance. Anything more would need the app to be used.
- **Two deliberate design notes recorded in the script:**
  - the archive's **oplog is not replayed** (`--oplog` is set on the dump side): the drill answers
    "does the dumped data come back intact", and replaying oplog entries against a standalone scratch
    instance would exercise a path production never takes, since the live instance is a replica-set
    member;
  - mongorestore's output is parsed for failure **counts**, not grepped for "error"/"failed" — because
    its own summary line reads "0 document(s) failed to restore". The first version of that check
    reported "1 error/failed line(s)" on a **clean** restore. Fixed to count failures and fail on them,
    which is both accurate and stronger. This is the same mistake the yamllint config was written to
    avoid: a check that cries wolf is one that gets ignored.
- **Cost impact:** none. The drill's scratch pods are deleted with the namespace; the class-C timer runs
  it monthly at 06:00 UTC on the 15th, the class-A drill on the 1st at 05:00.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; the drill is a host script plus two unit files, and reverting restores the
  Postgres/file-only version, which reports Mongo as uncovered again rather than mis-verifying it.

### ~23:50 IST — CI-007: shellcheck, yamllint and tflint in CI — and shellcheck found two suppressions that were doing nothing
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `scripts/check-shellcheck.sh`, `.yamllint`,
  `terraform/.tflint.hcl`, `.github/workflows/validate.yml`, four manifests (final newlines), two backup
  templates, `terraform/variables.tf`.
- **shellcheck — it paid for itself on the first run.** New `scripts/check-shellcheck.sh` covers
  `scripts/*.sh` **and** the Ansible shell templates, because the same defect can hide in a template. It
  found **two `# shellcheck` directives that were silently doing nothing**: each had prose after its code
  list, and shellcheck parses everything after the codes as a key=value pair — warning SC1125, *"Ignoring
  the rest of this directive"*. So the intended suppression never applied, and the next reader would have
  believed the rule was handled. Both fixed, with the reason written beside them.
  - `scripts/*.sh` is otherwise clean at **every** severity (checked with `-S style`), so this step is a
    guard rather than a fix list — which is the useful kind of lint step to add.
  - The templates **cannot be rendered here** (CI holds no age key, and several interpolate
    SOPS-decrypted values), so Jinja *expressions* are substituted with a placeholder — sufficient,
    because they always sit inside quotes or as a single command word. If a template ever contains a Jinja
    *block* (`{% %}`), the script **fails loudly** instead of linting something meaningless.
- **yamllint — narrow on purpose, and the number is the argument.** yamllint's defaults flag **968 lines**
  in `k8s/` here, almost all style. `.yamllint` keeps what finds mistakes — duplicate keys (which silently
  win the last one), malformed structure, missing final newline, trailing spaces — and disables the rest
  with a per-rule reason. After fixing four genuinely missing final newlines the tree reports **0**.
  - One exclusion, and it is proven rather than assumed: `k8s/hetzner-csi/rendered-manifests.yaml` is
    *generated* by that directory's `render.sh`, so its trailing spaces would come back on the next render.
    Removing the ignore brings back exactly its two findings and nothing else — verified.
- **tflint — pinned (v0.64.0), one rule off with the reason in the config.** `terraform/.tflint.hcl` runs
  the bundled ruleset. It flagged `access_allowed_emails` as declared but unused, and that variable's
  default was the **owner's personal email** — one of SEC-006's six publication blockers. Following
  SEC-006/TF-003's stated intent, the default is now `[]` while the *declaration* stays as the seam NET-004
  needs, so `terraform_unused_declarations` is disabled with that specific reason written next to it. Side
  effect worth noting: **the address is now gone from `terraform/variables.tf`**, so the redaction
  checklist loses one of its six blockers.
- **Validation:** every linter was run via containers (the repo's CI already uses `docker run` for
  gitleaks) so nothing was installed on this workstation: shellcheck on 5 scripts + 5 templates → clean;
  yamllint → 0 findings, with the ignore's scope verified by removing it; tflint → 0 issues, after
  `terraform validate` and `terraform fmt -check` were re-confirmed clean. The four newline additions were
  proved render-neutral (153 documents, identical set). CI's pinned download URLs were checked (HTTP 200)
  rather than assumed.
- **Recorded, not left to a red build:** CLUSTER-002 moves `terraform/` → `infrastructure/terraform/` and
  `ansible/` → `infrastructure/ansible/`, so the tflint step's `working-directory` and the yamllint path
  list must be updated in that commit.
- **Cost impact:** none (a few seconds of CI).
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; delete the two configs and the script, and drop the CI steps.

### ~23:35 IST — CI-003: the Helm-sourced apps are rendered with their real values in CI
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `scripts/helm-template-all.sh` (new), the CI workflow, the
  plan.
- **Why:** for the Helm-sourced apps this repo holds only a **values overlay** — the chart lives in a
  Helm repository or in another Git repository — and nothing in CI looked at that combination. A bad
  values key (a typo, a key the chart renamed, a value of the wrong type) therefore arrived as a failed
  or, worse, a *silently different* sync. `helm template` is the cheap local render of exactly what
  ArgoCD does with the same inputs.
- **What it does:** discovers every Helm-sourced `Application` **from the manifests themselves** (not
  from a hand-maintained list), and handles both shapes in use here:
  - a chart from a **Helm repository** — `chart:` + `repoURL` + `targetRevision` → `helm template --repo … --version …`;
  - a chart inside a **Git repository** — `path:` (rankstack) → shallow-clone `repoURL` at the pinned
    revision and template from the clone.
  Values are resolved through each app's `$values/` ref rather than hard-coded paths, so the script
  follows the manifests when they change.
- **Result on the real tree: 5/8 rendered, 3 skipped, 0 failed** — `argocd`, `argocd-image-updater`,
  `keda`, `keda-add-ons-http`, `rankstack` → **121 manifests**, now appended to the Kustomize stream
  that kubeconform checks.
- **The three skips are deliberate, loud, and counted separately from failures:** the VCAP charts live in
  a **private cross-organisation** repository that CI here cannot fetch, and their renders belong in
  VCAP's own CI (CI-006). The output prints a `SKIPPED:` line naming the repo and the reason — a silent
  skip is the failure mode this codebase keeps having to write down.
- **Failure behaviour tested, not assumed:** planting a malformed value in
  `k8s/platform/keda/values.yaml` produced `FAILED: …/platform/keda.yaml (keda: keda 2.21.0)` with helm's
  own error and a non-zero exit.
- **Recorded limitations, so nobody over-trusts it:** the script needs network (chart repos + the
  rankstack clone), and `helm template` does **not** reject *unknown* values keys — Helm ignores those —
  so what this catches is a render that breaks and rendered output that fails kubeconform, which is what
  the task asks for. A typo'd-but-ignored key still needs a different check.
- **Cost impact:** none (≈10 s of CI, mostly chart fetches).
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; delete the script and the CI step.

### ~23:20 IST — CI-002 (partial): CI now catches an Application path or an app directory that does not add up
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `scripts/check-app-paths.py` (new) + a step in
  `.github/workflows/validate.yml`.
- **Why:** ArgoCD resolves an Application's `path` at *sync* time, so a typo, a rename, or a directory
  deleted without its Application/ApplicationSet element surfaces as a comparison error on the live
  cluster — the last place to learn about it, and only if someone reads that app's status. The reverse
  is just as bad and even quieter: a directory nothing claims is **silently undeployed**, which is
  precisely how `cheatsheet` sat in the tree after its ApplicationSet element was removed.
- **What it checks, all inside this repo:**
  1. every `sources[].path` / `source.path` whose `repoURL` is this repository exists as a directory;
  2. every ApplicationSet list element expands to a directory that exists (the template path is
     `k8s/apps/{{.name}}`, so the element list and the tree must agree);
  3. **the reverse** — every directory under `k8s/apps/` is claimed by some Application, element, or
     in-repo values path;
  4. `helm.valueFiles` entries that resolve into this repo exist.
- **Both failure modes were tested rather than assumed:** changing one Application path to `…-typo`
  produced exactly one finding and exit 1; removing exactly one ApplicationSet element produced exactly
  one finding naming that app's directory. On the real tree: **25 in-repo paths across 20 manifests and
  16 app directories, clean.**
  - A first attempt at test 2 deleted too much of the element list (a greedy match) and the check
    reported nine extra unowned directories — which was the check being right about a state I had just
    broken, and worth recording because "the test itself was wrong" is a failure mode of validation too.
- **One subtlety the check has to get right:** the vcap Applications consume their values through a
  `ref` source and therefore have **no `path:`** pointing at `k8s/apps/vcap/`. The "claimed" rule
  therefore counts an in-repo *file* reference (such as `$values/k8s/apps/vcap/values/backend-dev.yaml`)
  as a claim — otherwise the check would report a false positive on a directory that is very much in use.
- **Not done, and it needs CLUSTER-002:** CI-002's other half — kubeconform `-strict` against the CRDs
  catalogue, including `clusters/**` and `bootstrap/`. Those paths do not exist yet, so validating them
  now would be validating nothing. Recorded in the task body rather than half-built.
- **Cost impact:** none (a ~0.2 s CI step).
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; delete the script and the CI step.

### ~23:05 IST — CLUSTER-005: one Ingress convention across all nine Ingresses
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — 6 Ingress manifests.
- **Four normalisations, each small, together the point of the task:**
  1. **`ingressClassName: traefik` added where it was only implicit** (`bitvault`, `learning-react`).
     Those two relied on Traefik being the cluster's **default** IngressClass — verified live that it is
     (`ingressClass traefik … is-default-class: true`) — so this is a behaviour-preserving change that
     stops depending on that default. If the default ever moved, both would have silently stopped being
     routed.
  2. **The deprecated `kubernetes.io/ingress.class` dropped** from `learning-react`, superseded by the
     field above (Traefik still honours the annotation, so nothing was relying on it that the field does
     not now provide).
  3. **`router.tls: "true"` added to the five that lacked it** (`bitvault`, `learning-react`,
     `status-page/api`, `upayan-web`, `keda-add-ons-http-routes`) so all nine carry the same pair of
     annotations. `websecure` is a TLS entrypoint and the certificate is the TLSStore default
     (ADR 0004), so this declares what already happens rather than changing it.
  4. **The duplicated 7-line S7 comment block collapsed** — it appeared byte-identically in five Ingress
     files — to two lines that keep the operational fact ("no `tls:` block; Traefik serves the wildcard
     default") and point at `docs/adr/0004-origin-ca-tlsstore.md`. The ADR is now the one place the
     reasoning lives. `meghmitra`'s *separate* app-specific comment above it was left alone: only the
     duplicated block was collapsed.
- **Verification:** the render diff showed **exactly five changed Ingress objects, no document added or
  removed, and the routing `rules` byte-identical in all five** — annotation-level change only, which is
  what the host sweep then confirmed end to end:
  - the documented 14-host baseline matches **14/14**, including the pre-existing `502` on api-bandit and
    the `000`s on the two-level hostnames;
  - the seven hosts on the changed Ingresses were baselined *before* the change (all 200 except
    `bitvault.upayan.dev` and `api-upayan-v5.upayan.dev` at 404 — the apps' own root paths) and re-checked
    after with the same results.
- **Deliberately not done here:** `monitoring/grafana-ingress.yaml` still lists the plain-HTTP `web`
  entrypoint alongside `websecure`. Removing it belongs to NET-004, which puts Cloudflare Access in front
  of Grafana and ArgoCD — doing it now would take the UI off one entrypoint for no benefit, and the plan
  pairs the two changes for that reason. Recorded so it is not mistaken for an oversight here.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; every change is an annotation or a comment, and the sweep above is the
  before/after evidence.

### ~22:50 IST — CLUSTER-001: the namespace + PSA labels are one component now; CI-001's render check had been quietly wrong
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `k8s/components/namespace-baseline/`, 14 app
  kustomizations, 14 deleted `namespace.yaml` files, `scripts/render-all.sh`.
- **What landed:** `k8s/components/namespace-baseline` (a Kustomize `Component`) now supplies the app
  Namespace and its Pod Security Admission labels, which were duplicated **byte-for-byte apart from the
  `name:` line** across 14 apps. No parameter is needed: kustomize's `namespace:` transformer renames a
  `kind: Namespace`, so the parent's `namespace: <app>` supplies the name — verified before adopting it,
  and the placeholder name makes a missing `namespace:` produce a visibly wrong namespace rather than
  quietly reusing a real one.
  - **Validation, met literally:** renders before and after are identical — 153 documents, none added,
    none removed, none changed — and the component-based build was then run in ArgoCD's exact shape
    (absolute app path, structure preserved) for five apps, each rendering its full document set with
    the Namespace named after its parent.
- **Finding 1 — CI-001's render check was silently wrong, and this is the failure mode it exists to
  prevent.** `scripts/render-all.sh` copied each kustomization directory *in isolation* into a temp dir
  and built it there. That is fine while nothing references a shared file — and it becomes catastrophic
  the moment a `components:` reference points above the app directory, because the component simply is
  not in the copy: the build then produces a **short** render rather than an error. I hit exactly that
  on the first bandit conversion and nearly concluded components cannot work; the isolated kustomize
  test proved otherwise (`../comp` resolves fine under the default load restrictor). Fixed: the script
  now copies `k8s/` and `clusters/` whole and builds *inside* the copy, so relative references survive.
  Verified as behaviour-preserving: it reproduces the previous output exactly (21/21 directories, 153
  documents, zero differences).
  - It also now **skips `kind: Component` directories** and says so — a component is a fragment, and
    building it standalone yields only its placeholder Namespace. Reported rather than silently dropped.
- **Finding 2 — ArgoCD's own invocation permits components at a shared path, from evidence rather than
  assumption.** The repo-server logs show
  `kustomize build /tmp/_argocd-repo/<id>/k8s/apps/<app> --enable-alpha-plugins --enable-exec` — **no
  `--load-restrictor`**, so kustomize's default applies; and with that default, a component above the
  app directory does resolve. That is why this change is safe to make on the live cluster, and it is
  recorded because the answer is not obvious from either ArgoCD's or kustomize's documentation.
- **Why the other two components the task names are NOT done — and it is not laziness, it is the task's
  own acceptance criterion.** CLUSTER-001 asks for `netpol-default` and `ksops` too, and requires that
  renders stay identical:
  - **`netpol-default` cannot meet that bar.** The NetworkPolicy names embed the app name
    (`bandit-default-deny-ingress`, `bandit-ingress`; the KEDA variant is named after the app too —
    `cas-api`), and the allowed ports differ per app (3000/5000, 4000, 8000/27017/6379, 8080, 5432 …). A
    Component cannot compute a name from its parent, so any dedup here renames live NetworkPolicy
    objects — a visible diff, and a brief window with no default-deny policy — which is precisely what
    "renders identical + empty app diff" forbids.
  - **`ksops` is plausible but unverifiable, and the blast radius is high.** The generator's
    `metadata.name` is cosmetic (it is not rendered), but its `files: ./secrets.sops.yaml` must resolve
    into the *app* directory; from a Component it would resolve into the component's directory, where no
    secrets exist — breaking Secret generation for **12 live apps**, whose pods mount those Secrets.
    Confirming the plugin's path semantics needs the repo-server's own kustomize+ksops against a temp
    tree (no ksops binary exists locally), and getting it wrong takes out workloads. Recorded as
    possible-with-a-test, not attempted on a live cluster for a ~96-line dedup.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; the component renders byte-identically to what each app produced alone, so
  a revert returns to 14 duplicated files with no manifest change either way.

### ~22:25 IST — DR-004 ran: class-C rows match exactly — and the run caught a bug that would have broken the class-A drill
*(Label note correction, once: the session-wide note further down says this session's labels run "up to ~25 minutes" ahead of the clock. By the end of the session that grew to about an hour, because the labels were written by narrative position rather than by reading the clock. The ordering is right; `git log --format='%ad'` remains authoritative. This entry, for instance, was written at 22:25 IST while labelled by its neighbours.)*

- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** break-glass host change (a host-side script sync, `systemctl enable --now`) + git commit (the
  `yq` fix) + this changelog entry.
- **The drill passed, class C, exit 0 — the numbers are the point of the task:**
  - restic restored the newest `class-C` snapshot: **603 files/dirs (51.4 MiB)**.
  - `vcap-dev/vcap-backend-dev-postgres`: 409 TOC entries, 57 tables, **rows live=1141 restored=1141**.
  - `bitvault/bitvault-postgres`: 178 TOC entries, 21 tables, **rows live=15 restored=15**.
  - file targets: `vcap-dev/minio` 22/22 restored objects present live, `bitvault/minio` 11/11,
    `monitoring/grafana` 329 restored all present live (330 live).
  - `rankstack/rankstack-mongo` reported **NOT COVERED** and counted into
    `vps_restore_test_uncovered_targets{class="C"} 1` — the gap is now visible in Prometheus, not only in
    prose.
  - the metric file is `vps_restore_test_class_C.prom`, all three series labelled `{class="C"}`, and the
    legacy unlabelled class-A file was **left untouched** by the class-C run — the gating this change
    was built around, confirmed against the live filesystem.
  - no evictions; all pods `Running`; 1475 MiB still available afterwards (scratch pods request 256 MiB
    each).
- **The run caught a bug in my own change, and it would have broken the class-A drill on 1 October.**
  `yq --arg` does not exist in mikefarah's yq — v4.44.3 replies `unknown flag: --arg` and prints its
  usage. My parameterisation used it in three places, so the *first* drill invocation failed instantly,
  and the monthly class-A drill (which shares those expressions) would have failed the same way two days
  later. Fixed by exporting `DRILL_CLASS` and using `strenv(DRILL_CLASS)`, with the reason written into
  the script next to it so it is not reintroduced. This is the second time today that *running* the
  thing found what reading it did not (the k3s register/skip bug was the first).
- **Second hand-run lesson, recorded because the next person will hit it:** invoking the drill directly
  over SSH needs the unit's environment (`set -a; . /etc/vps-backup/env; set +a`), otherwise restic
  fails with *an empty password is not a password*.
- **The self-heal path is validated too.** The drill's scratch namespace wedges in `Terminating` on this
  cluster (the KEDA `external.metrics.k8s.io/v1beta1: stale GroupVersion discovery` bug — fourth
  occurrence today). A second run logged
  `note: restore-test-c is Terminating (stale-APIService wedge); waiting, then clearing finalizers if
  empty` and then proceeded to an identical pass, so the recovery the script needs before each monthly
  drill is now proven rather than assumed. The namespace it left this time was provably empty, and its
  finalizers were cleared with the documented `/finalize` remedy.
- **Enabled the class-C timer** (`systemctl enable --now vps-restore-test-c.timer`): next run
  **2026-10-15 06:00 UTC**, a fortnight from the class-A drill on 2026-10-01 05:00 UTC.
- **One thing the restored snapshot showed:** it still contains the **retired hello-kitty** directory,
  because removing a backup target stops *future* backups — existing snapshots keep the path until they
  age out of retention. Expected, and worth knowing before someone reads it as a missed deletion.
- **Cost impact:** none (51 MiB of restore into `/var/tmp`, deleted by the drill's own trap).
- **Rollback:** `systemctl disable --now vps-restore-test-c.timer`; `git revert` the `yq` fix if needed
  (it is a strict fix — the previous form cannot work on this yq).

### ~23:10 IST — DR-004: the restore drill is class-parameterised; class C is only partly covered and that is written down
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `ansible/roles/backup/` (script, two new unit templates,
  task, README) + the plan.
- **Change:** `vps-restore-test.sh [A|C]`, class defaulting to **A**, so the existing monthly unit
  (`vps-restore-test.timer`, 1st at 05:00 UTC — and it fires in two days) is unchanged in behaviour.
  A new `vps-restore-test-c.timer` runs class C on the **15th at 06:00 UTC**: a fortnight apart,
  because each run creates scratch pods and this node is memory-tight, so the two must never overlap.
- **The label is load-bearing, not cosmetic.** The success metric is now
  `vps_restore_test_last_success_timestamp{class="A"}`. Without it a class-C run would satisfy the
  class-A staleness alert (`vps-restore-test-stale`, 40 days, `noDataState: Alerting`) — a green alert
  from a drill that tested different data. The pre-DR-004 **unlabelled** metric file is removed on a
  class-A run *only*, and unconditionally there: on success the labelled series replaces it, and on
  failure the alert's `noDataState: Alerting` then says exactly the right thing ("no successful class-A
  drill recorded"). A class-C run leaves it alone, because class C cannot supply the class-A series.
- **Coverage is reported, not implied — and it is the reason this task is not marked fully done.** The
  drill restores `postgres` dumps and `file` trees; a target of the requested class with any other
  engine is printed as `NOT COVERED` and counted into
  `vps_restore_test_uncovered_targets{class=…}`. So **bitvault's Postgres and the two MinIO
  directories are covered by the class-C drill, and rankstack's Mongo is not** — the class-A drill has
  no Mongo path either. That is a gap in the task's stated scope ("bitvault Postgres, rankstack
  Mongo"), recorded in the plan under DR-004 rather than left for someone to discover from the absence
  of a Mongo line in the output.
  - Why it was accepted for now, stated plainly: a `mongorestore --archive --gzip` path plus collection
    comparison is new code, this node's memory headroom makes it awkward to validate without care, and
    rankstack's database holds **0 documents** — so the comparison would prove very little. It is real,
    tracked work, not a rounding error.
- **Parameterisation proved behaviour-preserving for class A**, which matters because that drill fires
  on 1 October: the rendered script was diffed against the pre-change render, and for class A the only
  differences are the path variables (same values), `--tag class-${DRILL_CLASS}` (same value), the
  parameterised `yq` selectors (same result), the coverage report, and the metric names/labels above.
  The drill's checks themselves are untouched.
- **Static validation:** the template renders (18 Jinja templates clean), the rendered script passes
  `bash -n`, and `site.yml --syntax-check` passes.
- **A self-inflicted redaction regression, caught and fixed before it mattered:** a comment I added to
  `ansible/group_vars/vps/main.yaml` named a workstation SSH key path, and `ansible/` is exported to the
  public repo — the SEC-006 pre-flight check flagged it. Reworded. (Re-running that check is also how
  this session's progress became measurable: the `vps-old` blocker went from four `k8s/apps/*` files to
  one line of prose in `docs/inventory.md`, thanks to CLEAN-003.)
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`; the class-C timer is not started by the role, so nothing runs on its own.
- **The run itself is the next entry** — "row counts match" is reported from the actual output, not
  assumed.

### ~22:55 IST — ANS-002: a fresh node can join the tailnet from Ansible — and the key-expiry claim was wrong
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `ansible/roles/tailscale/tasks/main.yml`,
  `ansible/group_vars/vps/main.yaml`.
- **Two documented mechanisms, neither implemented.** ANS-002 said the Ansible tailscale role should run
  `tailscale up` from a SOPS auth key; the role deliberately did not (its own comment: re-running `up`
  on the live admin path risks disrupting it); and `TF-004` says the same join belongs in **cloud-init**
  with an apply-time key. So the action had two homes on paper and none in practice — a fresh node could
  not join the tailnet from Ansible, which is a rebuild-path gap.
- **Resolved in favour of Ansible + SOPS, on secret hygiene.** An auth key handed to Terraform for
  cloud-init ends up in Terraform state — which is itself destined for R2 and is copied by restic — and
  in the server's user-data metadata. SOPS keeps it where every other credential lives (ADR 0002), and
  the key stays mintable and short-lived on the Tailscale side.
- **Change:** the role now joins only when Tailscale is installed, `BackendState != 'Running'`, **and**
  a non-empty `tailscale_auth_key` is present; the command runs with `no_log: true` (the key is on its
  command line), and a follow-up wait confirms `Running` and reports the tailnet address. The key lives
  in `group_vars/vps/secrets.sops.yaml` with **no placeholder in `main.yaml`** — the same single-source
  rule adopted for `admin_ssh_public_key` earlier today.
- **Corrected claim, measured:** the role's header said the node's key expiry was disabled. It is not —
  `tailscale status --json` reports **2027-01-08**. Tailscale auto-renews a user-owned device while it
  stays online and this node does, so it is not an emergency; a long outage past that date would take the
  admin plane with it, which is what the lost-tailnet runbook and the Hetzner console are for. Also
  recorded: the node is untagged, while the review's ACL design assumes `tag:vps` (tags disable expiry).
- **Verified on the live node, read-only, role-only `--check`:**
  - defaults: install and join skipped, `changed=0`, and the report says "already running — no action
    taken on this run".
  - **`-e tailscale_auth_key=<a deliberately fake string>`: the join is still skipped**
    (`ok=4 changed=0 failed=0 skipped=5`). The guard is the running state, not the key's presence — so
    adding a key to SOPS cannot re-authenticate the cluster's admin path. (The fake string was never
    used; the task did not run. `no_log` is set regardless.)
- **Not verified, stated rather than implied:** an actual join on a fresh host — DR-006's throwaway
  (TF-004 → provider tokens) plus a real key the owner mints.
- **Cost impact:** none. **Rollback:** `git revert`; nothing on the host depends on the new variable.
- **Still open in the goal, and not fixable here:** the whole rebuild path (`TF-004`, `ANS-001`'s fresh
  install, `ANS-002`'s join, `DR-005`'s bootstrap script, `DR-006`) is now *written* end to end but has
  never been *run* end to end, because that needs a throwaway server and provider tokens.

### ~22:45 IST — ANS-001: a fresh host can now be built by the playbook; the live node's guardrail is unchanged
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `ansible/roles/k3s/tasks/main.yml`,
  `ansible/group_vars/vps/main.yaml`.
- **The contradiction, and why it was not resolved by overwriting either side.** The task says the k3s
  role "today it only asserts one is installed", implying it should install. It *deliberately* refuses:
  its own header and README state that it manages the config of an existing install, because installing
  or upgrading on the live node as a side effect of a config sync is exactly the failure its guardrail
  prevents. But the end state also requires a rebuildable cluster, and a fresh host could never get
  past this role — so "Terraform + Ansible rebuilds the cluster" was false in practice. Both were kept:
  installation became an explicit opt-in.
- **Change:**
  - `k3s_install_missing` (default **false**): when k3s is absent *and* this is true, the role fetches
    the installer and runs it with `INSTALL_K3S_VERSION={{ k3s_version }}`. The pin matters: an
    unpinned installer takes whatever is current, and k3s's bundled Traefik tracks the k3s release
    (ADR 0008), so a silent k3s bump is a silent ingress upgrade.
  - `k3s_allow_restart` (default **false**): promotes the rendered config, removes the legacy
    `tailscale.conf` drop-in, restarts k3s and waits on `/readyz` — taking an **etcd snapshot first and
    failing the run if that fails**, per the review's rule.
  - Both flags are documented in `group_vars/vps/main.yaml` as safety properties rather than
    preferences, so an operator meets the reasoning where they would set them.
- **Two defects found by running it, not by reading it — both fixed:**
  1. the version probe was re-registered *inside* the install block, and a **skipped** task still
     assigns `register` with a result that has no `rc`: the run died with
     `object of type 'dict' has no attribute 'rc'`. The post-install probe now runs unconditionally,
     outside the block.
  2. the "config differs" report fired whenever the template was *written* rather than when its content
     *differed*, so it announced a needed restart on a host already matching. Report and apply gate are
     now both keyed on a non-empty diff, and the `diff` probe is `check_mode: false` — check mode skips
     command tasks, so without that both would have gone silently inert under `--check`.
- **Verified on the live node, read-only, k3s-role-only `--check` runs:**
  - defaults: `ok=9 changed=1 failed=0 skipped=11` — install block skipped, assertions pass, and the
    applied-config **diff is empty**: the template renders byte-for-byte the config the node already
    runs, which also confirms there is no drift between git and the live control plane.
  - `-e k3s_allow_restart=true` against that same matching config: **identical recap, all six apply
    tasks still skipped.** That is the safety property that matters — an explicit opt-in to restart
    still does nothing when the rendered config already matches, so the dangerous path cannot fire on
    a no-op run.
- **Not verified, stated rather than implied:** a real install on a fresh host, and the restart path
  itself. Both need a throwaway server (TF-004 → provider tokens this workstation does not have), so
  they are DR-006's validation.
- **Cost impact:** none. No change was made to the live host — every run above was `--check` (the
  `diff` probe is read-only and the metric-rendering write creates only the `.pending` staging file the
  role already maintained).
- **Not done, on purpose:** dropping `flannel-iface` / `node-external-ip`. The live cluster *requires*
  them (the tailnet is the admin path), so that is a separate deliberate change, not a tidy-up.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`. Nothing on the host depends on the new flags being set.

### ~22:30 IST — CLEAN-002 verified: the rule is provisioned in Grafana, and the Ansible diff is exactly 3 tasks
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** changelog only (the changes are the preceding commit, `928680a`).
- **Verified, and this closes the task except for one check the environment cannot run:**
  - **The rule is live in Grafana, not just in git.** The `monitoring` sync restarted Grafana

    contains the new expression —
    `expr: (min by (sans) (traefik_tls_certs_not_after) - time()) / 86400 < 60` — under the new title
    "TLS certificate expiring within 60 days". The only remaining `certmanager` string in the file is
    the comment explaining what the old expression was; no dead alert is left.
  - **All 29 Applications `Synced`/`Healthy`**, including `monitoring` (whose `OutOfSync`/`Running`
    state during rollout is its `PostSync` provisioning Job, as usual) and `traefik-config` with its
    changed destination.
  - **The Ansible `--check --diff` changed exactly the three expected tasks** — `Deploy the
    backup-targets registry`, `Deploy vps-backup.sh`, `Deploy vps-etcd-snapshot.sh` — and nothing else
    in the role. That is the task's stated validation, met literally: the dead healthchecks ping and
    the retired `hello-kitty` target are the only content diffs.
  - The diff also confirms the careful part of the edit: `vps-etcd-snapshot.sh`'s `else / exit 1` is
    still there (the comment beside it says why), while `vps-backup.sh`'s redundant branch is gone.
- **Still not claimed:** `terraform plan` ("no resource changes") — it cannot run here without
  owner-supplied provider tokens, so that one line of CLEAN-002's validation rests on reasoning
  (removing unreferenced variables and editing comments cannot change resources) and is recorded as
  reasoning. Everything else in the task was verified against the live cluster or the live host.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** see the preceding entry.

### ~22:20 IST — CLEAN-002: the certificate-expiry alert is real again, a revoked cert was still loaded, and the dead config is gone
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `k8s/monitoring/`, `k8s/argocd/`, `ansible/roles/backup/`,
  `terraform/` + a permitted break-glass `rollout restart` of Traefik.
- **1. The expiry alert now measures the certificate that is actually served.** `tls-cert-expiring`
  queried `certmanager_certificate_expiration_timestamp_seconds`; cert-manager was removed on
  2026-09-28, so it was watching a metric that no longer exists — while the certificate in use expires
  **2027-09-28** rather than the 2041 the docs claimed (previous entry). It now uses
  `traefik_tls_certs_not_after`, which is **the expiry of the certificate Traefik has loaded**, i.e.
  what it serves:
  `(min by (sans) (traefik_tls_certs_not_after) - time()) / 86400 < 60`.
  - `min by` for two independent reasons: it dedupes the double scrape (`job=traefik` **and**
    `job=kubernetes-pods`), and it selects the **soonest** expiry per SAN set. A maximum — or matching
    one series — would report a long-dated certificate and hide a near one, which is exactly the trap
    documented in step 4 of the runbook.
  - Threshold raised 14 → 60 days, because renewal is a hand-run Origin CA procedure needing a token;
    14 days is not enough margin to notice, get a token and act. `noDataState: Alerting` is kept: if
    Traefik stops exporting the metric, that is an alert, not silence.
  - **Expression validated against live Prometheus before committing**, not written and hoped for:
    `min by (sans)` returned **364.8 days** and correctly ignored a stale 2041 entry; the threshold
    returned nothing (no false alarm).
- **2. A revoked certificate was still loaded by the ingress — found while validating (1).** Traefik
  reported **two** certificates for `*.upayan.dev,vps.upayan.dev`: the current one (2027-09-28) and the
  **revoked** predecessor (2041-05-06) whose private key had been public. Proven, not inferred: the
  metric's `serial` label is the hex serial in decimal — `034BB2554CEC…` → `188150632155…` and
  `699090E4…` → `602667960030…`, both converted and matched. No Secret or Ingress still references it
  (the cluster has exactly two `kubernetes.io/tls` Secrets and no Ingress declares `tls:`), so Traefik
  was holding it from before the rotation: **its certificate store is additive, so it adds the new leaf
  without dropping the old one.** That means the S7 rotation was only half-effective — a certificate
  with a compromised key stayed a candidate to serve, and Cloudflare validates the origin by chain
  (Full *strict*), not by key, so a chain-valid compromised certificate is still accepted.
  - **Fixed with a permitted break-glass restart** (`kubectl -n kube-system rollout restart
    deploy/traefik` — rule 2's "force pod restart after a Secret commit", which is exactly this case).
    Evidence: **before = 2 distinct certificates; after = 1**, carrying the current serial, and the
    alert expression still computes 364.8 days.
  - **Verified no regression:** 11/11 hosts in the documented baseline sweep return their exact
    documented codes (including the pre-existing `502` on api-bandit and the `404`s), and the origin
    serves serial `699090E4…` / notAfter 2027-09-28 both before and after.
  - **And the gap is closed in the procedure:** `docs/runbooks/rotate-certificates.md` gains a new
    step 4 ("Restart Traefik, and confirm only the NEW certificate is loaded") with the exact
    verification query and the reason, so the next rotation cannot repeat this.
- **3. The rest of CLEAN-002 (dead config):**
  - `Shrish2006/Snapdragon` removed from the `apps` project's `sourceRepos` — verified nothing sources
    it (the only reference in the repo was that line).
  - the `traefik` namespace removed as the `platform` project's destination, and `traefik-config`'s
    destination set to `kube-system`. Every resource that Application manages already declared
    `namespace: kube-system` (it patches k3s's bundled Traefik), so the destination described nothing;
    ArgoCD tracks by the resource's own namespace, so nothing is orphaned or pruned.
  - the retired **`hello-kitty`** backup target removed. A wrong or absent path does not fail loudly —
    restic reports it and the rest of the snapshot looks fine — so a target for a `Released` PV was
    coverage that did not exist.
  - the **healthchecks.io** variables removed from the role's defaults, both scripts and the task
    comment. The owner declined healthchecks.io, so the URLs were permanently empty, the
    `[ -n "$HEALTHCHECK_URL" ]` guards could never fire, and two scripts merely looked like they pinged
    something. Kept: the textfile metric and its alerts, which do run.
    - **Careful edit, not a mechanical one:** removing the ping from `vps-etcd-snapshot.sh` would have
      deleted that script's *only* failure exit (nothing follows its `fi`), so a failed run would have
      reported the exit status of its last successful command. The `exit 1` is kept with a comment
      saying why. In `vps-backup.sh` the branch was removable because an `exit "$overall_rc"` follows.
  - Terraform: removed the four variables nothing referenced (`hetzner_primary_ipv{4,6}_id`,
    `vcap_{dev,staging}_volume_id` — the real ids live inline in `imports.tf`), and corrected three
    comments that described a plan rather than the state: `imports.tf` still claimed "only the server
    import is active" (all five are), `storage.tf` still called the `data_volume` role "not yet written"
    (written and applied, volume mounted) and now warns that `format` is create-time-only on a volume
    that already exists, and `providers.tf` documents that `terraform plan` **cannot run at all** here
    without owner-supplied tokens (measured: hcloud stops on the missing `HCLOUD_TOKEN`).
    `terraform/secrets.enc.env` is kept deliberately — it is TF-001's input and the `.sops.yaml` rule
    already exists for it.
- **Verification:** `terraform validate` passes and `terraform fmt -check` is clean;
  `scripts/render-all.sh` renders 21/21; `ansible-playbook site.yml --syntax-check` passes and
  `check-ansible-templates.py` reports 16 templates clean; the alert expression and the certificate
  count were both checked against live Prometheus. **Not run:** `terraform plan`, which needs the
  provider tokens (above) — so "no resource changes" rests on the reasoning that removing unreferenced
  variables and editing comments cannot change resources, and is stated as reasoning rather than as a
  plan result. The post-sync checks (alert provisioned in Grafana, all Applications Synced) are in the
  follow-up entry.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` the commit. The Traefik restart has nothing to roll back.

### ~21:55 IST — DOCS-002: eight ADRs — and writing one of them found the origin certificate expires in ~12 months, not 2041
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `docs/adr/` (new), plus expiry corrections in six files.
- **Part 1 — the ADRs.** `docs/adr/` now holds eight records plus an index, one per decision the plan
  names: ArgoCD/app-of-apps, SOPS+age/ksops, the tenant model, Origin CA via `TLSStore` (no
  cert-manager), static PVs on `/srv/data`, host-level restic backups, KEDA scale-to-zero, and k3s's
  bundled Traefik. Each carries Status, Context, Decision, the alternatives **rejected and why**,
  Consequences (including what got *worse*), and Evidence. Validated mechanically: all five sections
  present in all eight, the index links all eight, and a redaction pass over the directory returns
  nothing — these files are public-repo-bound, so that check matters more than the structure one.
- **Part 2 — the finding, and it is the kind that matters.** ADR 0004 was drafted from the docs'
  statement that the origin certificate is valid to **2041-05-06**. Instead of copying it, I read the
  certificate: the live `wildcard-upayan-dev-tls` Secret **and** the certificate the origin actually
  presents over TLS both report serial `699090E41816073595D26CDDE0568CFE6C66BCB7`,
  `notBefore 2026-09-28` → **`notAfter 2027-09-28`**. The S7 reissue requested **one year**, where its
  predecessor had fifteen. Six files asserted 2041 as the *current* date, and every
  "nothing to renew / no renewal-failure mode" claim in the repo rested on it.
- **Why it was not just a stale figure:** the consequence chain was fully armed. cert-manager is removed
  by design, so nothing renews the certificate; the only alert that could have warned about an expiry
  queried cert-manager metrics that no longer exist; and `CLEAN-002` was written to **drop** that alert
  ("replace it with … or drop it (the Origin CA cert runs to 2041)"). Left alone, every host would have
  failed at once in ~12 months with no warning and no mechanism that would have prompted anyone to look.
- **Corrected:** `docs/certificates.md` (the current-state paragraph; the certificate-pair table, whose
  2026-05-10 row claimed "this is what the origin serves right now"; and its S7 "still outstanding" list,
  which described work already done), `docs/architecture.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `.claude/CLAUDE.md`,
  `.claude/skills/secrets-tls/SKILL.md` — the operative one, which told future agents "Valid to
  2041-05-06; nothing renews it and nothing needs to" and to expect `notAfter 2041-05-06` — and
  `docs/runbooks/rotate-certificates.md`. The review's dated snapshot is **annotated, not rewritten**, so
  its original reasoning survives next to the correction. `CLEAN-002` now requires **replacing** the dead
  alert with a real served-certificate expiry probe (alerting ≥60 days out), because renewal is a
  hand-run Origin CA procedure needing a token.
- **The generalisable lesson, recorded in ADR 0004 as a rule rather than a footnote:** an expiry claim in
  prose is a claim about one specific certificate, and every reissue invalidates it. **Read the date off
  the certificate, never off the documentation.** This is the third credential/expiry claim in this repo
  to have drifted from reality, so it is written where the next rotation will be read.
- **Verification:** `grep -rn 2041` now returns only lines that explicitly label the date as the
  superseded predecessor's, plus append-only changelog entries about that predecessor; the live values
  were read from two independent paths (the Secret, and the TLS handshake against the origin), which
  agree on serial and validity.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` this documentation commit. The underlying fact — a certificate that expires
  2027-09-28 — is not revertible.

### ~22:05 IST — CLEAN-004 verified: smart-home starts a pod for the first time in weeks, on `local-path`
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on the owner's authorisation of CLEAN-004.
- **Type:** break-glass `kubectl delete pvc smart-home-system-pvc -n smart-home-system-api` + changelog.
  The manifest change is the preceding commit.
- **Why a deletion was unavoidable:** the sync could not apply the new `storageClassName` —
  `spec: Forbidden: spec is immutable after creation except resources.requests … for bound claims`, with
  ArgoCD retrying. A PVC's class cannot be changed in place, so the PVC had to be replaced. Its data was
  already unrecoverable: the Hetzner volume behind it had been deleted from under it.
- **Exactly what was deleted:** `PersistentVolumeClaim/smart-home-system-pvc` in namespace
  `smart-home-system-api`, uid **`6d634268-5fbb-4830-8116-5472acb00922`**, bound to PV
  `pvc-6d634268-5fbb-4830-8116-5472acb00922` (`hcloud-volumes`, reclaim `Delete`). Nothing was mid-write:
  the app is KEDA-scaled, and the `pvc-protection` finalizer cleared immediately.
- **Bonus cleanup:** the dangling PV `pvc-6d634268-…` went with it — `Delete` reclaim removed the PV and
  the CSI provisioner treated the already-deleted Hetzner volume as success. One of the objects the plan
  listed as dangling is gone, and `pvc-6d634268` no longer appears in `kubectl get pv`.
- **Verification — and this is the whole point of the change:**
  - ArgoCD recreated the PVC as `storageClassName: local-path`, **`Bound`** to a fresh local-path volume
    `pvc-85e5a579-ca61-42ea-a0be-f43d10f96459` (1 Gi), and `smart-home-system-api` is
    **`Synced`/`Healthy`**.
  - `https://api-smart-home-system.upayan.dev/` → **HTTP 200**, and it produced a real pod:
    `smart-home-system-api-58ccc84c98-v4jzq` **1/1 `Running`, `ready=true`, 0 restarts**, mounting the new
    PVC. For weeks this app could not start a pod at all — every scale-up failed with
    `FailedAttachVolume` because its PV pointed at a deleted Hetzner volume. That is the plan's CLEAN-004
    validation criterion, met literally.
  - meghmitra's demotion is live: `meghmitra-api` and `meghmitra-web` pods report
    `priorityClassName=low-priority` / `spec.priority=-1`, while `meghmitra-postgres` remains at priority
    `0` with no class — the deliberate exception, visible in the live objects rather than only in the
    commit message.
  - Cluster: **29/29** Applications `Synced`/`Healthy`. (rankstack's operation reads `Running` only
    because its `PostSync` rs-init hook re-runs on every sync; the Application itself is
    `Synced`/`Healthy`, and that hook is pre-existing.)
- **Transient, recorded for honesty:** between the manifest commit and the PVC replacement,
  `smart-home-system-api` sat `OutOfSync` in a retrying sync loop (`spec is immutable…`). That is the
  expected shape of this change, not a fault; it cleared as soon as the PVC was replaced.
- **Cost impact:** none, marginally negative — no longer bound to a billed Hetzner volume.
- **Rollback:** not available for the PVC (its data was already gone, and the original binding cannot
  come back). The manifest itself can be reverted, and none of the rest of CLEAN-004 was touched.

### ~22:00 IST — CLEAN-004 (authorised half): smart-home's PVC names `local-path`, and meghmitra's stateless tiers are demoted
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on the owner's authorisation of **CLEAN-004** and the D-3 answer
  "**local-path pool**" for the shared-storage question.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + a break-glass PVC deletion in the follow-up entry.
- **Part 1 — the actual bug, fixed.** `k8s/apps/smart-home-system-api/persistentvolumeclaim.yaml` used to
  omit `storageClassName` and rely on the default class. When **both** `hcloud-volumes` and `local-path`
  carried the default annotation, that Binding was a coin flip; this PVC lost it, got a Hetzner volume,
  and the volume was later deleted — which is why the app could not scale up (`FailedAttachVolume`).
  It now names `local-path` explicitly, so the placement is deterministic and can never land on a billed
  Hetzner volume again. The accepted consequence — root-disk storage, so it does not survive losing the
  node and is not in the class-A restic targets — is written into the manifest, because that is the
  trade the owner chose for a class C app whose previous backing store was already gone.
- **Part 2 — the demotion, and one deliberate exception.** meghmitra's `api` and `web` Deployments gain
  `priorityClassName: low-priority`. **Its `postgres` Deployment does not**, even though the
  PriorityClass's description names "meghmitra" as a whole, because:
  - that Deployment's own comment says it "backs meghmitra-api, must not be preempted";
  - it holds the class A dataset the 30-minute backup timer exists for;
  - the node is genuinely memory-constrained right now — 1.5 Gi available of 7.6 Gi, container limits
    overcommitted to **183%**, and a 4 GB swapfile in use. Under that pressure, demoting the database
    and not its web tier would be exactly backwards.
  The PriorityClass description was rewritten to name the stateless tiers explicitly, so the exception is
  documented where the next reader will look.
- **Measured, not assumed:** a recursive audit (my first pass only looked at top-level directories and
  wrongly reported `upayan-v5` as missing the field — its Deployments are in `api/`/`app/`) shows exactly
  **12** apps on `low-priority` after this change, and only **status-page** and **upayan-web** with no
  priority class at all. Both are deliberately left alone: the PriorityClass description itself calls
  them "always-on priority apps".
- **Verification:** `render-all.sh` renders 21/21 and the rendered `smart-home-system-pvc` carries
  `storageClassName: local-path` with its 1 Gi request; `check-ports.py` and `check-secrets.sh` pass. The
  live proof — the PVC rebound to a local-path volume, `smart-home-system-api` able to produce a Running
  pod after being stuck for weeks, and all apps Synced/Healthy — is in the follow-up entry.
- **Cost impact:** none, and marginally negative: the app stops being bound to a (billed) Hetzner volume
  it could never mount.
- **Rollback:** revert the manifests. The PVC deletion in the following step is the only irreversible
  part, and the data it discards was already unrecoverable.
- **Still open in CLEAN-004, deliberately:** cleaning the four `Released` PVs, the legacy VCAP volumes
  and `hetzner-csi` are all *retirement* work, and D-3 answered "do not retire" — so they stay, and the
  `hcloud-volumes` StorageClass stays with them (noted in the task body, not silently dropped).

### ~21:45 IST — the playbook could not run at all: `admin_ssh_public_key` was empty, and the role is third in `site.yml`
*(Label note, once, applying to every entry in this session: the labels are ordered correctly but run ahead of the real clock — by up to ~25 minutes, because they were written while several parallel tasks were in flight. `git log --format='%ad'` is the authoritative timestamp; treat these labels as ordering, not as a clock. Two entries made this visible: the cheatsheet decommission is labelled ~21:40 but its commit `a208145` is earlier than the ~21:20 PUB-001 commit, and this note's own entry is labelled ~21:45 while written at 21:33.)*
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `ansible/group_vars/vps/secrets.sops.yaml`,
  `ansible/group_vars/vps/main.yaml`.
- **Problem, and it was worse than one unset variable:** `admin_user`'s first task asserts
  `admin_ssh_public_key | length > 0`. It was empty, so the play **always** failed there — and because
  `admin_user` is the **third** role in `site.yml` (after `base`), `tailscale`, `k3s`, `backup` and
  `data_volume` were **unreachable in any full run**. The playbook could not be applied to the host at
  all. Proved with a read-only `--check --diff` run: `ok=7 changed=0 failed=1`, stopping at
  `admin_user : Fail fast if no SSH public key was supplied`.
- **The blocker turned out not to need anything from the owner.** The workstation's
  `~/<ssh-key>.pub` was verified to be the node's **only** authorized key — its fingerprint
  `SHA256:L9GaBjCrIeTM89RmmFa0FjlKm1rnVnnuN4cFmpQ+i6U` matches `/root/.ssh/authorized_keys` — so it is
  already the key that grants root access today. It is now `admin_ssh_public_key` in
  `group_vars/vps/secrets.sops.yaml`, which is where the role's own comment says role inputs belong. The
  empty `admin_ssh_public_key: ""` placeholder was **removed** from `main.yaml` so the encrypted file is
  the single source, instead of relying on alphabetical group_vars precedence for the encrypted value to
  beat an empty string.
- **Verification:** `ansible -m debug -a var=admin_ssh_public_key` resolves the key; the role's assert
  now reports `All assertions passed` where it previously failed the run; the file is still
  ciphertext-only (5 `#ENC[` lines, one `sops:` block, and `grep -c ssh-ed25519` = 0 — the public key is
  inside the ciphertext, and a public key is not secret anyway); `check-secrets.sh` passes.
- **A check-mode run still cannot get to the end, for a boring reason worth recording so it is not
  misread as a defect:** `ansible.posix.authorized_key` refuses to act when the target user does not
  exist, and in check mode the preceding "Create the admin user" task changes nothing — so it stops with
  `Either user must exist or you must provide full path to key file in check mode`. A real run is fine,
  because the user is created first.
- **Still gated:** *applying* the role (creating `upayan`, then `PermitRootLogin no`). Both are SSH
  lockout risks and neither happens without an explicit go-ahead; the Hetzner console is the way back.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`. Nothing was applied to the host.

### ~21:35 IST — CLEAN-003: the dead `vps-old` affinity and the inert single-node spread constraints are gone
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + the affected apps' resync.
- **Two kinds of no-op scheduling boilerplate, both of which *look* meaningful in a manifest:**
  - `nodeAffinity` preferring hostname **`vps-old`** in `k8s/apps/{upayan-web,status-page/api,status-page/app}`
    — a node that no longer exists since the two-node design was collapsed, so the preference did
    nothing. Worse for the incoming public repo: it advertised a hostname that is not there.
  - `topologySpreadConstraints` (`maxSkew: 1`, `topologyKey: kubernetes.io/hostname`,
    `whenUnsatisfiable: ScheduleAnyway`) in **15** Deployments. `ScheduleAnyway` never rejects a pod, and
    with one topology domain there is no skew to spread — the block is inert here.
- **Change:** the 7-line spread block removed from all 15 Deployments with a **line-anchored** edit (not
  a YAML round-trip), so no comment was lost and no file was reformatted; `vps-old` → `vps` in the three
  affinity blocks.
  - **Deliberate deviation from the task's wording**, which says "remove the nodeAffinity": the value was
    fixed instead of the block deleted, because pinning to the node *is* this repo's documented
    convention (13 other apps say `vps`; `AGENTS.md` and `k8s/GUIDE.md` both describe it). Deleting it
    from three apps would have left them inconsistent with the other thirteen and with the onboarding
    template. Recorded so the difference is visible rather than looking like an oversight.
  - Measured counts differ from the plan's: **15** spread blocks, not 17, and **3** `vps-old` affinities —
    cheatsheet was one of each and left with the app earlier the same day.
- **Verification:** the whole tree rendered before and after, then diffed structurally — **153 documents
  on both sides, none added, none removed, 15 changed**, and each of the 15 differs only by the dropped
  `topologySpreadConstraints` (3 additionally losing `vps-old`). No unexpected document changed.
  `render-all.sh` 21/21, `check-ports.py` OK, `check-secrets.sh` OK, and `grep -rn vps-old k8s/apps/` is now
  empty — the only remaining `k8s/` hits are in `k8s/docs/*`, which PUB-002 excludes. The resync's pod
  restarts are expected (the pod templates changed); the app host sweep is unchanged.
- **Also updated:** `docs/plans/redaction-checklist.md` — its `vps-old` rows now record that the exported
  occurrences are gone, leaving only `docs/inventory.md` prose for SEC-006.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`. Both changes are inert on a single node, so a revert has no scheduling effect.

### ~21:30 IST — ANS-004: Ansible collections and restic pinned; the playbook cannot run past `admin_user`
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `ansible/requirements.yml`,
  `ansible/roles/backup/tasks/main.yml`, `ansible/group_vars/vps/main.yaml`.
- **Change:**
  - the three `>=` bounds in `requirements.yml` became exact pins — `ansible.posix 2.2.2`,
    `community.general 13.4.0`, `community.sops 2.4.0`, the versions installed and working on
    2026-09-28. A lower bound means the collection that actually runs depends on the day CI ran, so a
    playbook can pass in CI and fail on the host with nothing changed in this repo.
  - restic is pinned through a new `restic_version` group var (`0.16.4-2ubuntu0.24.04.3`, read off the
    node with `dpkg-query`) and `restic={{ restic_version }}` in the backup role. **jq and sqlite3 stay
    unpinned deliberately** — they only post-process dumps and own no on-disk format of ours, so a point
    pin would start failing the moment Ubuntu supersedes it. restic *writes* the repository, so its
    version is part of the backups' provenance.
- **Verified:** `ansible-galaxy collection install -r requirements.yml` → `Nothing to do. All requested
  collections are already installed` (the pins resolve and already match the host);
  `ansible-playbook site.yml --syntax-check` passes; `scripts/check-ansible-templates.py .` → `16 Jinja
  templates parsed cleanly`. `ansible-lint` is **not installed here** (CI installs it), so that one CI
  step is unverified locally.
- **Blocked deeper check — a discovery about the playbook, not about the pin:** a read-only
  `--check --diff` run against the live host passes `base` (`ok=7`) and then stops at
  `admin_user : Fail fast if no SSH public key was supplied` (`failed=1`). `admin_ssh_public_key` is
  empty in `group_vars/vps/main.yaml`, and `admin_user` is the **third** role in `site.yml` — so
  `tailscale`, `k3s`, `backup` and `data_volume` are all **unreachable in a full run** until the owner
  supplies their public key. The restic pin is therefore validated as correct but not yet proven
  idempotent on the host. Recorded against ANS-003, which owns that key.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`. Nothing was applied to the host — the run was `--check` only.

### ~21:25 IST — cheatsheet removal verified; the namespace wedged in `Terminating` (break-glass finalizer clear)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** break-glass `kubectl replace --raw /api/v1/namespaces/cheatsheet/finalize` + changelog. The
  removal itself is commit `a208145`.
- **Verified after the sync:** every cheatsheet object is gone
  (`kubectl get all,configmap,secret,sa,pvc,role,rolebinding,networkpolicy,ingress -n cheatsheet` →
  nothing), the Application is deleted, and the cluster is back to **29/29** Applications
  `Synced`/`Healthy`.
- **The namespace did not merely linger — it wedged in `Terminating`** with the documented gotcha:
  `Discovery failed for some groups, 1 failing: unable to retrieve the complete list of server APIs:
  external.metrics.k8s.io/v1beta1: stale GroupVersion discovery`. Not an unreleased finalizer: the
  namespace controller failed to enumerate API groups while KEDA's aggregated metrics API was briefly
  unreachable, then never retried (`All content successfully removed`,
  `All content-preserving finalizers finished`, finalizers still `["kubernetes"]`).
- **Why the break-glass step was safe:** the namespace was proven **empty** first (full `kubectl get`
  sweep → nothing), KEDA's `v1beta1.external.metrics.k8s.io` was confirmed `Available: True` ("all
  checks passed"), and only then were the finalizers emptied through the `/finalize` subresource — the
  exact remedy `.claude/CLAUDE.md` prescribes, and the same one used for the four earlier occurrences.
  Namespace and Application were both gone within 5 s; the cluster returned to 29/29. The deletion was
  already in flight, so this completed an authorized removal rather than initiating one.
- **Worth a permanent fix:** `keda-operator-metrics-apiserver` already runs 2 replicas (the earlier
  mitigation), so replica count is not the trigger — the namespace controller gives up instead of
  retrying. Recorded in `archived/cheatsheet/README.md`; not attempted here.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** n/a. Restoring the app re-creates the namespace via `CreateNamespace=true`.

### ~21:20 IST — PUB-001: `github.com/upayanmazumder/cluster` created (private, empty); GitHub secret scanning is unavailable while private
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on the owner's explicit go-ahead ("yes — create it now, private and

    with `gh repo view --json`.
  - ruleset **`main protection`** (id `24126436`): `enforcement: active`, target `branch`,
    `~DEFAULT_BRANCH`, rules `deletion` + `non_fast_forward` + `required_linear_history`.
    422-proof: the ruleset exists independently of the branch, so it applies to the first commit.

    the `PATCH` output and the unchanged statuses are the evidence. It becomes available when the repo
    goes public at PUB-006, which already ends by checking the native alert list is empty. Until then
    the protection is CI-004's `gitleaks git` (history mode) plus a pre-commit hook — required by the

  `gh api repos/.../rulesets` returns the ruleset above; `gh api repos/... --jq '.security_and_analysis'`
  shows the four secret-scanning fields `disabled`.
- **Cost impact:** none (private repo, no Actions on it yet).
- **Rollback:** `gh repo delete upayanmazumder/cluster`. Nothing has been pushed to it.

### ~21:40 IST — cheatsheet decommissioned (owner instruction): it was serving nothing, and its API half no longer existed
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on the owner's explicit instruction ("remove the cheatsheet deployment,
  archive it if it's no longer needed"). Recorded D-3 is "do not retire apps — demote and share
  storage", so this is the single exception the owner named rather than a change of that decision.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + the `apps` ApplicationSet reconcile.
- **Checked before deleting anything — three measurements, not assumptions:**
  - `sayantani.upayan.dev` resolves to `0e1fa43f670491c6.vercel-dns-017.com` (**Vercel**), so the
    in-cluster `cheatsheet-web` Deployment served a hostname that does not point at this cluster;
  - **no `cheatsheet-api` Deployment exists in the cluster** — only a dangling `cheatsheet-api` Service
    with no endpoints. Its host `api-sayantani.upayan.dev` resolves through Cloudflare to this cluster
    and returned **HTTP 404 for all five paths probed** (`/`, `/api`, `/health`, `/docs`,
    `/openapi.json`);
  - no PVC and no PV: there was no data to dump and nothing to restore.
- **Change:** `k8s/apps/cheatsheet/` → `archived/cheatsheet/manifests/` (`git mv`, so history is
  preserved); `archived/cheatsheet/live-export-2026-09.yaml` (the 6 live objects — Deployment, both
  Services, Ingress, 2 NetworkPolicies — with no Secrets); `archived/cheatsheet/README.md` recording
  why, what was left for the owner, and how to restore. Then the `cheatsheet` element removed from
  `k8s/argocd/applicationsets/apps.yaml`, its destination removed from `k8s/argocd/projects/apps.yaml`,
  and its row removed from `k8s/GUIDE.md`.
- **The deletion is the intent, and here is why it deletes:** the live `cheatsheet` Application carries
  `resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io`, so removing the ApplicationSet element deletes the
  Application *and cascades the workload deletion*. Worth recording: the ApplicationSet **template**
  does not declare that finalizer — it was added out-of-band — so the cascade comes from live state,
  not from git. That is a real gap for ARGO-001/CLUSTER-003, whose `preserveResourcesOnDeletion` work
  assumes the template is the authority.
- **Verification (pre-commit):** the ApplicationSet and app tree are consistent — **10 elements**,
  every element has a matching `k8s/apps/<name>/` directory, and the 6 directories with no element are
  exactly the 5 apps that have explicit Applications plus `vcap` (project `vcap`). `render-all.sh`
  **21/21** (one fewer directory than before), `check-ports.py` OK, all four touched YAMLs parse, and
  the export contains no credential-shaped content (one `secretRef:` pointer, no values). The live
  check — Application gone, workload gone, namespace lingering — is in the follow-up entry.
- **Cost impact:** none, marginally negative (one fewer Deployment, one fewer Ingress, one fewer
  KEDA-scaled host).
- **Rollback:** `git mv archived/cheatsheet/manifests k8s/apps/cheatsheet`, re-add the element and the
  destination, push. The image `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/cheatsheet:latest` is still published.
- **Deliberately not done:** the two Cloudflare DNS records (the zone is click-ops until TF-002, and DNS
  changes are confirmation-gated) and the `cheatsheet` namespace (`CreateNamespace=true` does not track
  it as a managed resource, so it will linger; namespace deletion is confirmation-gated). Both are
  written into `archived/cheatsheet/README.md` as owner actions.

### ~21:15 IST — VCAP-009: the two cluster-only VCAP PVs are now in git, with pruning disabled
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + the first sync of the new `storage` Application.
- **Problem:** `vcap-backend-dev-data-vps` and `vcap-backend-staging-data-vps` existed **only in the
  cluster** — hand-created on 2026-09-28, in no manifest. They back the VCAP Postgres data directories
  on the `vps-data` volume (`/srv/data/vcap-{dev,staging}`), so a rebuild from git would have left both
  VCAP Postgres PVCs permanently unbindable. They were the last cluster-only state apart from
  `argocd/sops-age`, which is bootstrap-only by design.
- **Change:** new `k8s/platform/storage/` (the two PVs) owned by a new platform Application `storage`
  (`k8s/argocd/applications/platform/storage.yaml`, path `k8s/platform/storage`). This is the plan's
  intended `platform storage` owner, and it uses the same static-PV shape as
  `k8s/apps/meghmitra/postgres.yaml`, which is already `Synced` in this cluster.
- **Two deliberate safety choices:**
  - `argocd.argoproj.io/sync-options: Delete=false,Prune=false` on both PVs (design invariant 4): this
    is the cluster's only authoritative data volume, so a GitOps mistake must not be able to delete it.
    Deleting these files from git **cannot** prune the PVs.
  - the Application sets `ServerSideApply=true`, and `claimRef` is deliberately **not** declared.
    ArgoCD becomes one field manager among several, so it cannot strip the `claimRef` that the PV
    controller owns — with a merge apply, adopting a bound PV can clear `claimRef` and unbind a live
    volume. The meghmitra PV is the evidence that an undeclared `claimRef` is left alone.
- **Verification (pre-commit):** `scripts/render-all.sh` renders 22/22 directories (22 now, one more
  than before this change) and the two PVs render with the expected name/path/reclaim policy. The
  live-state check — spec hashes identical before and after the sync, PVCs still `Bound`, Application
  `Synced`/`Healthy` — is in the follow-up entry.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** delete `k8s/platform/storage/` and the Application, then re-apply root. The PVs survive
  (`Prune=false`), so this rolls back with no data effect at all.
