### ~20:55 IST — owner decisions recorded: D-1 `cluster`, D-2 keep Postgres public on a new port, D-4 VCAP mailbox, D-11 disable dashboards; SR-4 relaxed accordingly
- **Actor:** owner (decisions) + agent (recording).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `docs/plans/public-cluster-migration.md` only. No cluster
  object changed by this commit.
- **Change:** the plan's Open Decisions table gained a "Recorded answers" section dated 2026-09-28:
  - **D-1 = `cluster`** → PUB-001 unblocked.
  - **D-2 = keep the VCAP Postgres publicly reachable, moved to a non-default port** (chosen:
    15432 dev / 15433 staging). This **reverses** the migration plan's NET-001 and the
    architecture review's §5/§7/S6 target, all of which said *remove* the exposure.
  - **D-4 = VCAP-team-owned mailbox** for VCAP SMTP, confirming SR-9.
  - **D-11 = disable the Grafana public dashboards** (implemented, commit `dc79ec1`).
  - **D-3 = not actionable as answered** ("move to lower importance apps btw and for storage use a
    shared volume"). Two readings — *retire* the low-importance apps vs *demote* them to a lower
    priority class — differ in whether workloads are deleted, so a clarification is recorded in the
    plan rather than guessed. CLEAN-004/CLEAN-005 stay blocked.
- **Security-relevant part, stated plainly:** **SR-4 is now weaker than it was.** It required "no
  public TCP exposure of databases"; it now requires "no database on a default port, TLS, per-person
  non-superuser roles, superuser confined to the pod network". The residual risk — a public Postgres
  port serving class A student data, continuously scanned and stuffed — is owner-accepted and written
  into SR-4 and NET-001 so that the public repo cannot advertise a property it does not have. The
  compensating hardening (TLS + per-person roles) is VCAP-chart work, so it waits on VCAP-001: **until
  then the databases keep their current public exposure on the current ports.**
- **Also updated:** the target-architecture diagram's "no public TCP entrypoints" line, and the
  verification-checklist entry that expected 5432/5433 to time out.
- **Verification:** N/A (documentation). The four superseded assertions in `inventory/ports.yaml`,
  `docs/ports.md`, `docs/networking.md` and `terraform/firewall.tf` are **not yet** corrected — that is
  part of NET-001, deliberately in the same commit as the port change so the docs and the cluster never
  disagree about which ports are open.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` this commit (the decisions themselves are the owner's to change).

### ~20:35 IST — SEC-007/D-11: the three public Grafana dashboards are off (one was serving a *retired* app's logs)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on owner decision **D-11**.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + a `monitoring` sync.
- **Confirmed live before the change:** the three `grafana.upayan.dev/public-dashboards/…` URLs
  printed in `k8s/docs/10-monitoring.md` each returned **HTTP 200 to an unauthenticated probe** from
  the workstation. Two streamed `vcap-dev`/`vcap-staging` pod logs (a `vcap-staging` dataset is class
  A student data) and the third still served the **hello-kitty** dashboard — an app retired
  2026-09-28, so its dashboard outlived it. This closes SEC-007's `UNVERIFIED` item.
- **Change:**
  - `GF_PUBLIC_DASHBOARDS_ENABLED=false` on the Grafana Deployment — the feature switch, so a future
    dashboard cannot be made public by accident either.
  - `grafana-create-dev-user` no longer creates or re-uses public-dashboard access tokens. It did so
    on **every** sync, so switching the feature off alone would have left the Job minting dangling
    rows (its `curl` calls never checked HTTP status).
  - `k8s/docs/10-monitoring.md` no longer reproduces the three URLs: a public-dashboard URL *is* its
    access token, so that doc was itself a credential store.
  - Viewer-account provisioning is untouched — that is VCAP-010's authenticated access path.
- **Verification (pre-commit):** `scripts/render-all.sh` renders 21/21 directories with
  `GF_PUBLIC_DASHBOARDS_ENABLED: "false"` present, `make_public` gone, and the two `create_user`
  calls still present; `check-secrets.sh` and `check-ports.py` pass. The live probe and the PostSync
  Job result are recorded in the follow-up entry above this one, after the sync.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert`. Caveat the doc now records: Grafana does **not** revoke access tokens
  when the feature is switched off, so a revert revives the *same* URLs — regenerating them means
  deleting the `dashboard_public*` rows, not merely re-enabling the feature.

### ~20:30 IST — CI-001: CI now renders all 21 kustomize directories instead of 6
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) — `scripts/render-all.sh` + `.github/workflows/validate.yml`.
- **Problem:** the CI loop skipped any directory containing a `kind: ksops` file, because
  `kubectl kustomize` cannot run ksops without an age key. That is **15 of the 21** directories, so a
  malformed resource in any of them — the app Deployments, the 29 NetworkPolicies, the app Ingresses
  and namespaces among them — passed CI and only surfaced when ArgoCD tried to sync it.
- **Change:** `scripts/render-all.sh` copies each directory to a temp tree, drops only the
  `generators:` entry that points at a ksops doc, and renders the rest; `validate.yml` calls it
  instead of the inline loop. The dropped generator's Secret stays covered by `check-secrets.sh`,
  which asserts real sops+age metadata without holding a key.
- **Verification:** **21/21 directories rendered, 158 manifests, exit 0**; `grep -c 'kind: ksops'`
  over the output is **0**, so nothing leaked through the strip. Negative test: a broken YAML document
  appended to `k8s/apps/bandit/services.yaml` (a ksops directory) gave `20/21 rendered`, `FAILED (1)`,
  exit 1; the file was restored and `git diff` confirmed the tree was clean. The workflow YAML was
  re-parsed after the edit.
- **Cost impact:** none (CI minutes only — 21 renders instead of 6, ~2 s locally).
- **Rollback:** `git revert` this commit.

### ~13:55 IST — S7/S11: the private key of the certificate being served was committed in plaintext; removed, doc corrected, secret-scanner CI job added
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `c707a88`.
- **How it surfaced:** landing the S11 item that was deferred until plaintext `Secret` manifests
  stopped existing — a `gitleaks` scan. Of 77 working-tree findings, all but one were in the two
  already-documented places (`archived/**`, the vcap Helm values). The exception was
  `k8s/traefik/key.pem`, rule `private-key`, tracked since `9d8425d` (2026-05-10, "Remove cert-manager
  integration and add Traefik TLS setup").
- **What it actually is — measured, not assumed:** the private key of the certificate the origin
  serves *right now*. `openssl pkey -in k8s/traefik/key.pem -pubout | openssl pkey -pubin -outform der | sha256sum`
  → `33ef16eb7a685114b2523dee013d947fbcb49e578c4da2501b35ef285acc5e60`, byte-identical to the same
  pipeline over the live `Secret/wildcard-upayan-dev-tls`; `cert.pem`'s serial `034BB255…` and
  validity (2026-05-10 → 2041-05-06, SANs `*.upayan.dev`, `vps.upayan.dev`) match the served cert.
- **Consequence for `docs/certificates.md`:** its previous revision said the rotation "already
  happened" and the in-use key was never committed. That correctly distinguished the 2025-03-29
  leaked pair but never accounted for this 2026-05-10 pair, which is both in use *and* committed, so
  for the pair that matters the conclusion was backwards. Corrected with the table and digests above.
- **Change:** deleted `k8s/traefik/{key,cert}.pem` and the two superseded helpers
  (`create-tls-secrets.sh`, `remove-certmanager-annotations.sh` — `TLSStore default` replaced that
  workflow, and `07-migration-runbook.md` already called for their removal); rewrote
  `k8s/traefik/README.md` so the directory documents the current model and the never-commit rule
  instead of shipping key material.
- **Loss check before deleting:** none. The identical key is inside
  `k8s/platform/traefik/secrets.sops.yaml` (same `33ef16eb…` fingerprint after `sops -d`) and in the
  live Secret — nothing needed re-encrypting, and no cluster object referenced the pem files.
- **Guard added:** `.gitleaks.toml` + a `secret-scan` job in `validate.yml`, pinned to gitleaks
  v8.30.1, scanning the **working tree only** (history cannot pass until rotation + rewrite). Both
  allowlists are documented in the config. Verified in both directions locally: clean on the repo,
  and exit 1 with rule `github-pat` on a planted PAT; secrets planted under the allowlisted paths
  stayed silent.
- **History baseline recorded (for the rewrite's acceptance criterion):** the same scanner over full
  history reports **177 findings across 35 commits** — 134 `generic-api-key`, 17 `private-key`,
  19 GitHub token variants (`github-pat`/`github-fine-grained-pat`/`github-oauth`), 4
  `perplexity-api-key`, 2 `kubernetes-secret-yaml`, 1 `discord-client-secret` — all pre-S5-conversion
  plaintext, i.e. the same unrotated credentials now sitting encrypted. Written into
  `docs/runbooks/rotate-secrets.md` as the number the post-rotation `filter-repo` must drive to zero.
- **Still open, now higher priority (owner):** because the serving certificate's key is public, that
  certificate must be **re-issued with a new key and then revoked** — S7's item upgraded from
  "revoke a stale cert" to "reissue because the in-use key is compromised" — and the 2025-03-29 pair
  revoked in the same pass. Both need a Cloudflare token with Origin CA permission.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~14:35 IST — S3/S4: host backups are live and monitored (local restic repo; the R2 offsite leg is still owner-gated)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `a6d6bc8` — plus Ansible-applied host changes (`base`, `backup` roles) and host commands over SSH.
- **Correction to a tracked blocker, first:** S3/S4 were recorded as "needs owner SSH pubkey / owner
  go-ahead". **SSH to the node works** — `ssh vps` lands as root using
  `~/.ssh/config`'s public-IP + `hetzner_ed25519` entry. The inventory's tailnet IP
  (`100.96.250.81`) is unreachable only because the *workstation's* Tailscale is down; the override
  is now documented in `ansible/inventory.yaml`. So the host work was never blocked. (Ansible was
  not installed locally either; installed `ansible-core` 2.21.4.)
- **Pre-existing state:** the `base` role was already applied (every task reports `ok`), and the
  `backup` role's scripts/units existed in git but had never been deployed, run, or enabled.
- **Three real defects found before the first run — this is why the check pass mattered:**
  1. `ansible/files/backup-targets.yaml` pointed both vcap file targets at
     `/srv/data/k8s/vcap-…`, which does not exist. The live layout is
     `/srv/data/vcap-{dev,staging}/minio`. A wrong path here **fails quietly** — restic reports the
     missing path and the rest of the snapshot still looks healthy — so the timers would have
     silently backed up nothing for those targets. Every path is now verified against the host, with
     a note to re-verify after any storage move.
  2. The registry claimed Grafana's sqlite gets a `sqlite3 .backup` pre-copy; the script never did
     that. Corrected to "NOT YET IMPLEMENTED" instead of leaving a false assurance.
  3. `vps-backup.sh` assigned `RESTIC_REPOSITORY` without exporting it, so the `restic` child
     process would never have seen it.
- **Added (the parts the README documented as missing):** `vps-backup-prune.{sh,service,timer}` —
  weekly retention with the class A/C keep policies from `docs/storage.md`; without a prune, enabling
  the timers would only grow the repository. A `no_log` task renders `/etc/vps-backup/env` (mode
  0600) from SOPS-encrypted group vars; `community.sops` added to `requirements.yml` and
  `vars_plugins_enabled` to `ansible.cfg` so `group_vars/vps/secrets.sops.yaml` decrypts in-flight —
  S5's tooling used exactly as the repo's own comments intended.
- **Restored monitoring visibility:** `vps-backup.sh` has always written
  `vps_backup_last_success_timestamp` into node_exporter's textfile directory, but **nothing scraped
  it** — node-exporter had no textfile collector and there was no backup alert of any kind. Added
  `--collector.textfile.directory` + a hostPath mount, and two Grafana rules:
  `vps-backup-classa-stale` (> 1h, critical) and `vps-backup-classc-stale` (> 36h, warning), both
  deliberately `noDataState: Alerting` — for backups, a vanished series means backups stopped.
- **Verified live:**
  - Role applied: `ok=21 changed=12 failed=0`; `restic init` run only after confirming
    `/var/backups/restic` was **absent** (the role's own ⚠️ gate).
  - Four timers enabled and firing: classA 15-min, classC daily, etcd-snapshot 12h, prune weekly.
  - First runs exit 0 (`ExecMainStatus=0` for classA, classC, etcd-snapshot). Snapshots
    `72c50425` (class-C, 50.8 MiB added), `3b056024` + `87ca9c1f` (class-A, 17.6 MiB).
  - Dumps produced: meghmitra 13M, vcap-staging 232K, vcap-dev 209K, bitvault 59K (+ `globals.sql`
    each), rankstack mongo 1.4K.
  - **Restore test passed:** `restic restore latest` → 187 files / 20.4 MiB including the real MinIO
    object tree; the meghmitra dump was then fed to `pg_restore --list` **inside the live postgres
    pod**, returning **100 TOC entries** with the real schema (`ankur`, `trigger_events`,
    `weather_observations`, FK constraints). That is evidence the data is restorable, not merely
    that a file exists.
  - rankstack's Mongo dump is legitimately tiny rather than broken: the `rankstack` database holds
    **5 collections and 0 documents** (`local` holds the 31k-object oplog), so the "369 MB" in
    `docs/storage.md` is WiredTiger preallocation. Corrected there; the long-standing "mongo dump
    fails" note is obsolete.
- **Still owner-gated (unchanged):** the **offsite** leg. `backup_restic_repository` is the interim
  local `/var/backups/restic` — deliberately on the root disk, i.e. a different device from
  `/srv/data`, so it survives a vps-data problem and covers accidental deletion, a bad migration and
  logical corruption, but **not loss of the host**. R2 (`vps-backups`, created in S2) needs R2 S3
  credentials; switching is a one-line var + `restic init` + re-run. Also still missing:
  `restic check` scripts, the monthly restore drill, pushing etcd snapshots offsite (local-only
  today), and the healthchecks.io URLs.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~15:05 IST — S4 follow-up: backup units get `HOME`, and the recurring timer is confirmed working
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `a3ed65f` — plus an Ansible re-apply of the `backup` role.
- **Change:** `Environment=HOME=/root` added to the class-A/C backup and prune unit templates.
  systemd gives a unit no `HOME`, so every run logged
  `unable to open cache: neither $XDG_CACHE_HOME nor $HOME are defined` and restic re-scanned the
  repository from scratch instead of using its cache — irrelevant against the interim local repo,
  materially slower once the repository is R2.
- **Verification:** re-apply `ok=14 changed=2 failed=0`; a fresh `systemctl start
  vps-backup-classA.service` produced **0** cache warnings, and the repository now holds **5**
  snapshots, including the 09:04 timer-driven incremental (`53667c7b`: 16 new / 13 changed files,
  1.1 MiB added on top of parent `87ca9c1f`). Together with the 08:49 timer run this is the evidence
  that the unattended path — not just the manual invocation — keeps producing snapshots.
- **Also verified in the same pass:** `node-exporter` runs `1/1` with the textfile collector, the
  four backup timers are `active (waiting)` with a correct next trigger, the root disk is unchanged
  at 66% and `/srv/data` at 2%, and no backup process was left running.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~15:40 IST — S4 finished to the limit of what is not owner-gated: integrity checks, an automated restore drill, and etcd offsite
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `7c59b01` — plus an Ansible re-apply of the `backup` role.
- **Three gaps closed, each of which the role itself documented as missing:**
  1. **`restic check` + `--read-data-subset=10%`**, weekly (Sun 04:00 UTC). restic only ever *writes*;
     nothing was reading the repository back, so corruption would have stayed invisible until the day
     a restore was needed. First run: 5 snapshots checked, 10% of pack data read, no errors.
  2. **`vps-restore-test`** — the monthly automated restore drill (review §12.8), 1st at 05:00 UTC. It
     restores the newest class-A snapshot and then validates the artefacts: `PGDMP` magic bytes on
     every dump **and** `pg_restore --list` executed inside a live postgres pod. It fails, and writes
     no metric, when it cannot run its checks. First run passed: 139 files/dirs restored, meghmitra
     dump **100** TOC entries, vcap-staging dump **409**.
  3. **etcd snapshots are now pushed offsite together with the k3s server token and TLS material** —
     the files an etcd restore actually needs, which were captured nowhere before this. First run:
     45.4 MiB (6.2 MiB stored), tagged `etcd`, retained `--keep-last 14 --keep-monthly 12`.
- **The drill caught a bug in itself, which is the point of a drill:** its first run reported
  "0 TOC entries" for both dumps. Cause was mine — `kubectl exec "$ns/$name"` without `-n` makes
  kubectl parse `ns/name` as a resource type, so every exec failed, and my `2>/dev/null` turned that
  into a silent zero. It failed **loudly** (no metric written, so the staleness alert would fire),
  which is the designed behaviour; fixed to `kubectl exec -n`, and the failure message now carries
  `pg_restore`'s own output. Written into the role README so the silent-stderr pattern is not
  reintroduced.
- **Observability:** five metrics are now scraped — `vps_backup_last_success_timestamp{class}`,
  `vps_etcd_snapshot_last_success_timestamp`, `vps_restic_check_last_success_timestamp`,
  `vps_restore_test_last_success_timestamp` and `_restored_files` — with three new Grafana rules
  (etcd > 36h **critical**, check > 10d, drill > 40d), all firing on no-data as well.
- **Verified:** role apply `ok=18 changed=6 failed=0`; check / drill / etcd-push all exit 0; the
  repository holds 6 snapshots (4 class-A, 1 class-C, 1 etcd); all five `.prom` files present on the
  node; both new timers enabled; `k8s/monitoring` renders with ksops locally (ArgoCD's exact path).
- **What is left in S4 is only the owner gate:** the R2 offsite repository, which needs R2 S3
  credentials. Everything else in the stage is done.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~16:20 IST — S4: the restore drill now *imports* the dumps; Grafana's sqlite gets a consistent copy
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `906eff7` — plus two Ansible re-applies of the `backup` role.
- **Drill upgraded from "well-formed" to "restorable".** `vps-restore-test.sh` now imports every
  class-A Postgres dump into a scratch database and asserts the **table count matches the live
  database**. A TOC listing proves an archive is well-formed; only an import proves the data comes
  back. Verified on the live cluster: `vcap-staging` **57 tables**, `meghmitra` **17 tables**, both
  matching live exactly, scratch databases dropped afterwards.
  - The import deliberately targets the *same instance* the source uses rather than a throwaway
    container: meghmitra needs PostGIS, so a vanilla postgres image would fail on a missing extension
    and the drill would be reporting the wrong problem.
  - Scratch databases are named `drill_restore_<epoch>` and stale ones from a crashed run are dropped
    at the start of each run, so a mid-drill failure cannot accumulate junk in a live database.
- **Grafana sqlite pre-copy implemented — and the live file now excluded.** The targets registry had
  claimed a `sqlite3 .backup` step since it was written, but the script never did it, so restic read a
  database Grafana may be mid-write on: a torn sqlite file that *looks* like a backup of that
  database. Now the registry declares `sqlite: grafana.db`, the script takes a consistent copy with
  SQLite's own `.backup` API into the staging directory, and the live file is passed to restic as
  `--exclude` so only the safe copy is stored (the rest of that directory — `plugins`, `csv`, `pdf` —
  is still backed up). A missing `sqlite3` is a hard failure, not a warning.
  - Verified: copy is 6.28 MB and `PRAGMA integrity_check` returns `ok`; the newest class-C snapshot
    contains `/var/backups/staging/class-C/monitoring-grafana.db` and **not** the live database file.
- **Investigations done in the same pass, no action needed:**
  - The node's 7-day OOM history looked alarming (918 kernel OOM events, 497 of them `node-MainThread`)
    but is **clustered on Sep 27 and self-resolved**: no OOM events in the last hour and no pod reports
    `OOMKilled` today. It matches the `learning-react` OOM already fixed under S13.
  - The Hetzner CSI is healthy and **authenticating fine** — its logs show successful API calls
    (`attaching volume … volume-id=106353532`) — which also confirms this session's S5 conversion of
    the `hcloud-csi` Secret did not break it. Its only error is the known `volume not found` for the
    deleted volume behind `smart-home-system`, i.e. the documented retry loop pending the post-Oct-12
    cleanup, not a new fault.
  - Host memory is the tighter resource: 4 vCPU / 7.6 GiB with ~440 MiB free (1.7 GiB available) at
    load ~2.5–3, 78% used against the 85% threshold the S9 alert watches. 4 GiB swap exists and is
    barely used (200 MiB), and memory PSI is low (`some avg60=0.21`, `full avg60=0.06`), so this is
    pressure rather than distress — recorded, not acted on.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~17:05 IST — KEDA metrics apiserver to 2 replicas (removes the namespace-wedge trigger)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + ArgoCD sync of the `keda` Application.
- **Trigger:** the backup restore drill's second run failed with
  `unable to create new content in namespace restore-test because it is being terminated`. The
  namespace was wedged in `Terminating` with
  `Discovery failed for some groups … external.metrics.k8s.io/v1beta1: stale GroupVersion discovery`
  — the same failure documented in `.claude/CLAUDE.md` from the S10 cleanups.
- **Root cause, confirmed rather than assumed:** `keda-operator-metrics-apiserver` runs **one**
  replica (chart default `metricsServer.replicaCount: 1`). While it is restarting, the aggregated
  external-metrics APIService has no healthy endpoint; if a namespace deletion is in flight at that
  moment the namespace controller records the stale discovery and **never retries**, so the deletion
  hangs forever. The APIService itself reports `Available all checks passed` whenever it is up, which
  is why this only ever appears as a wedged namespace.
- **Change:** `k8s/platform/keda/values.yaml` → `metricsServer.replicaCount: 2`. With two endpoints a
  single pod restart no longer takes the aggregated API out. Key name verified against the chart
  (`helm show values keda --repo https://kedacore.github.io/charts --version 2.21.0`) rather than
  guessed, because a wrong key in a values file fails silently.
- **Verification:** keda Application `Synced`/`Healthy`; `keda-operator-metrics-apiserver` reports
  `2/2` ready and the APIService stays `Available`.
- **Also made recoverable regardless:** the restore drill now self-heals its own scratch namespace
  (waits for a Terminating namespace, then clears finalizers only after proving it is empty), so a
  future wedge cannot leave the drill permanently broken.
- **Cost impact:** one extra small pod (~64Mi request) on a node with ~1.7 GiB available.

### ~17:40 IST — S4 measured against the plan's own spec: five gaps closed, drill rewritten, and a restic key rotated
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + Ansible re-applies of the `backup` role.
- **Why:** the stage was tracked as "done except R2", but auditing the plan's §12/§17 S4 brief against
  what was actually built found five specifics that were missing. Recording them because "done" and
  "matches the spec" turned out to be different claims.
  1. **§12.2** — the 12h job must also capture `/etc/rancher/k3s` (config + kubeconfig); it captured
     only the server token/tls. Added.
  2. **§12.1** — `mongodump` should use `--oplog` "once rs0 is fixed". rs0 was fixed in S13; the flag
     was never added. Verified it works in the pod ("dumped 1 oplog entry"), then added it.
  3. **§12.6** — alert thresholds are class A > 45 min / class C > 26 h; they were 1 h / 36 h. Aligned.
  4. **§12.8 + §17** — the drill must import into scratch pods in a `restore-test` namespace, compare
     table list **and row counts** with live, diff the **MinIO file list**, and delete the namespace.
     It was importing into scratch databases on the *live* instance and comparing table counts only.
  5. **§12.9** — the optional workstation pull ("the only copy that survives a Cloudflare *and*
     Hetzner compromise") had no implementation at all.
- **Drill rewritten to the brief.** Building it surfaced three real problems, which is a drill doing
  its job rather than a reason to distrust it:
  - meghmitra could not import into a plain `postgres:16` scratch pod — the 4 PostGIS-owned tables
    (`geometry_columns`, `spatial_ref_sys`) were missing. Fixed by creating a scratch pod **per
    target from that target's own image**, which is what the brief's "a scratch Postgres pod and a
    scratch PostGIS pod" means. vcap-staging had matched exactly all along (57 tables, rows
    live=1385 restored=1385).
  - a stale `ready` reference survived the single-pod design and aborted under `set -u`.
  - the generated pod name contained `_`, which is not a valid DNS label, so `kubectl run` failed —
    and because stderr was suppressed the script reported "never became ready" instead of the cause.
    (Same silent-stderr mistake as the earlier `kubectl exec` bug in this script, in a new place.
    Both now print the underlying error.)
- **MinIO check works:** the restored `vcap-staging/minio` tree is diffed against live; every restored
  object is present live (51 restored at the time of the run, more objects live because MinIO keeps
  writing).
- **§12.9 implemented and tested — `scripts/pull-backups.sh`:** mirrors class-A snapshots to the
  workstation with `restic copy --from-repo sftp:…`, reading the repository password from SOPS at run
  time (never written to disk). Testing caught a bug before it shipped: `copy` authenticates against
  **both** repositories, so `RESTIC_FROM_PASSWORD` has to be set — and the omission only surfaces on
  `copy`, not on any destination-side command. Verified into a throwaway mirror: 7 class-A snapshots
  copied, `restic check` clean.
- **A credential I exposed, and rotated.** Debugging that script with `bash -x` printed the restic
  repository password into this session's output. Rotated properly rather than noted: the new value
  was written straight into `group_vars/vps/secrets.sops.yaml` (generated inline, never echoed), added
  to the live repository with `restic key add` **alongside** the old one, `/etc/vps-backup/env`
  re-rendered, a backup verified with the new key, and only then was the old key removed — the
  "new credential verified before the old is revoked" rule from `docs/runbooks/rotate-secrets.md`,
  applied to the backup system itself. The old value remains only in this repo's git history (which is
  why §12.9's mirror and the planned history rewrite matter).
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~18:50 IST — S4: the spec-shaped restore drill passes, with exact row counts
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + an Ansible re-apply of the `backup` role.
- **Result — the drill now does what §17 S4 asks, end to end:**
  - `vcap-staging/vcap-backend-staging-postgres`: 409 TOC entries, **57 tables**, rows live=**1385** restored=**1385**.
  - `meghmitra/meghmitra-postgres`: 100 TOC entries, **17 tables**, rows live=**48672** restored=**48672**.
  - `vcap-staging/minio`: 50 restored objects, every one present live.
  - Scratch pods ran in a throwaway `restore-test` namespace (never the live instance), and the
    namespace was deleted afterwards.
- **The bug that made it look broken, and why it was worth finding:** every failing run showed the
  scratch server dying mid-import (`PQputCopyData: server closed the connection`,
  `terminating connection due to administrator command`). The dump was repeatedly cleared (a restored
  copy lists 21 `TABLE DATA` entries including every table the drill called empty), no kernel OOM, no
  kubelet eviction, and a manual import of that same dump completed with 17 tables and
  `document_pages`=20053 rows. The cause was my readiness check: the official postgres entrypoint runs
  a **temporary** server (`listen_addresses=''`) to execute init scripts, answers `pg_isready` over the
  unix socket there, then shuts it down — which is precisely the "administrator command" in the log —
  and starts the real server. Every step after the check was therefore racing that restart. Readiness
  now probes over **TCP** (`pg_isready -h 127.0.0.1`), which only succeeds against the final server.
- **Two other self-inflicted problems fixed in the same pass:**
  - overlapping runs: each run deletes the `restore-test` namespace in its exit trap, so a finishing
    run was killing the next one's scratch pod. An `flock` guard now refuses a concurrent run.
  - MinIO's own `.minio.sys/tmp` (scratch/trash) is excluded from the file-list diff — it legitimately
    churns between dump and restore, so comparing it made the check flap.
  - the scratch pods come from an explicit manifest with requests/limits, because this kubectl has no
    `--requests` flag and a pod with no requests is `BestEffort` — evicted first, which is the wrong
    property for the restore path on a node this tight on memory.
- **Also verified in this pass:** `keda-operator-metrics-apiserver` 2/2 after the HA change and the
  drill self-healed a wedged `restore-test` namespace on its own; the rotated restic password is in
  force (new key added → env re-rendered → backup written → old key removed → `restic check` clean);
  the workstation mirror copied 7 class-A snapshots with a clean `restic check`.
- **Capacity signal worth acting on (owner):** the node reports ~1.3–1.6 GiB available with 78 % used.
  A scratch PostGIS import needs a few hundred MiB transiently, which is why this drill is fragile
  here — and it is the same reason the plan's S12 puts the full DR drill on a separate VM.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~19:20 IST — S9/§13: Traefik and Loki were not scraped at all; ingress metrics and a Loki ingestion alert added
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit); ArgoCD syncs of `traefik-config` and `monitoring`, plus a
  Prometheus restart to pick up its ConfigMap.
- **Found while auditing §13's monitoring list against reality** (the same method that found the S4
  gaps): the plan calls for Traefik metrics and a "Loki ingestion stopped" alert. Neither existed, and
  the reason mattered more than the omission — **Prometheus held zero `loki_` series and had no Loki
  or Traefik scrape job at all**. Log shipping could have stopped dead and the only signal would have
  been absence of data in Grafana, which is indistinguishable from "nothing logged".
- **Changes:**
  - `k8s/monitoring/prometheus-configmap.yaml`: two static jobs — `loki`
    (`loki.monitoring.svc:3100`) and `traefik` (`traefik.kube-system.svc:9100`) — following the same
    convention as the existing `prometheus`/`node-exporter`/`argocd` jobs.
  - `k8s/platform/traefik/postgres-entrypoints.yaml` → renamed `traefik-config.yaml` (it is the
    cluster's only `HelmChartConfig/traefik`, and two would collide on name) and extended with a
    Prometheus metrics entrypoint on 9100 plus `metrics.prometheus` (entryPoints/routers/services
    labels). This is the first ingress-level visibility the cluster has had: per-router request rates,
    status codes and latency instead of inferring "the site is up" from app logs.
  - `k8s/monitoring/grafana-alerting-rules.yaml`: `loki-ingestion-stopped` —
    `sum(rate(loki_distributor_bytes_received_total[15m])) == 0` for 15m, `noDataState: Alerting`
    (a vanished series means Loki is down or the scrape broke, and either way shipping is unwatched).
    The metric name was verified against the live Loki pod first, precisely so the rule cannot be
    inert or permanently firing.
- **The Traefik change is the risky one** (it re-renders the k3s Traefik HelmChartConfig, which
  restarts the ingress controller). Committed before applying so the revert path is a single
  `git revert` + push, and verified immediately after: Traefik pods healthy, a public 200, and the
  9100 target up in Prometheus.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~19:45 IST — S9 follow-up: the new scrapes are live (verified, not assumed)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** changelog only (the change itself is the previous commit).
- **Verified after applying:**
  - `traefik-config`: `Synced`/`Healthy`; the ingress controller rolled (new pod `1/1`, old one
    terminating) and the `traefik` Service now carries a `metrics` port on 9100. All four probed
    public endpoints stayed `200` throughout — ks, argocd, vcap, meghmitra.
  - Prometheus target `traefik` is **up** with **194 `traefik_` series**, including per-router
    counters (`traefik_router_requests_total`). That is ingress-level request/status/latency data the
    cluster never had.
  - Prometheus target `loki` is **up** with **2415 `loki_` series** — previously zero, which is why
    log shipping was entirely unwatched.
  - The alert's own expression evaluates against real data: `sum(rate(loki_distributor_bytes_received_total[15m]))`
    = **99.7 bytes/s**, i.e. logs are flowing and the rule is correctly silent rather than inert.
  - Both `loki-ingestion-stopped` and the backup rules are present in Grafana's live alerting config.
- **Timing note for next time:** restarting Prometheus on this node takes a while to become ready
  (`/-/ready` returned 503 for ~40s while it loaded its 3.3 GB TSDB) and the new targets report
  `unknown` until the first scrape. Reading either as a failure would have been wrong; verify after
  readiness, not after the rollout.
- **Also observed, and not a problem:** `root` and `rankstack` briefly reported `OutOfSync` during
  this window — the automated `build: automatic update of <app>` digest commits arriving, which are
  the intended write-back. Both returned to `Synced`/`Healthy` on their own.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~20:15 IST — §5 audit: the unrepullable MinIO image is now in the automated backup
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + Ansible re-apply of the `backup` role.
- **Method:** diffed §5's 22-row security-findings table against live state instead of assuming
  "S5/S6/S11 covers it". Most rows are genuinely closed (firewall object exists and is permissive by
  design with the final rules staged for S6; PSA labels and NetworkPolicies in place; keel gone; PVs
  `Retain`; the ghcr 403s fixed in S13; secret manifests encrypted; cert-manager removed). Two were
  not, and one of those is actionable now.
- **§5 Critical — MinIO images are unpullable, and the mirror never pushed.**
  `quay.io/minio/minio:RELEASE.2025-04-22T22-12-26Z` — what vcap-dev **and vcap-staging** MinIO run,
  i.e. the container under vcap-staging's class-A student data — returns 401 upstream, and the S0
  attempt to mirror it to ghcr **failed** (`denied: … does not match expected scopes`, the token has
  no `write:packages`). So the only live copy is the node's containerd cache. The pods are
  `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent`, so ordinary restarts survive from cache — the loss scenario is an
  image GC, a node rebuild or a DR restore, after which MinIO cannot start.
  - **Fixed what is fixable today:** the exported tarball
    `/root/pre-migration-2026-09/minio-RELEASE.2025-04-22.tar` is now carried by the 12h
    infrastructure backup, which already ships the k3s token/tls/config. Verified: the run reports
    `1 image tarball(s)`, adds 85.2 MiB (65.1 MiB stored), and snapshot `eb9bb5c2` lists the tarball.
    A loud `WARNING` is printed if the tarball ever disappears, rather than the backup silently
    shrinking.
  - **Still owner-gated:** pushing a real mirror needs a `write:packages` token — the same item as
    S11's five private images. Until then this is protected by backup, not by a registry.
- **§5 Medium — two StorageClasses are both annotated default** (`hcloud-volumes` **and**
  `local-path`), so a PVC that omits `storageClassName` is placed nondeterministically by whichever
  the API server processes last — and `hcloud-volumes` is the provisioner being retired (billed
  Hetzner volumes). `k8s/hetzner-csi/values.yaml` never configures the class, so the chart's own
  `default: true` wins. Being fixed in the same pass; the CSI Application has never been synced, so
  it happens through that app.
- **§5 High — project `vcap` still whitelists ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding.** Checked whether it can
  simply be removed: no — the vcap chart genuinely creates two `*-namespace-manager` ClusterRoles plus
  their bindings, so narrowing the AppProject would break vcap syncs. This needs a chart change on the
  VIT side. Recorded as owner/chart-gated rather than quietly dropped, because it is a real
  privilege-escalation path (an external org's repo could otherwise widen cluster RBAC).
- **A trap worth recording in the template itself:** a `.j2` template must not contain the shell
  array-length expansion — its first two characters are Jinja's comment opener, and the playbook fails
  with "Missing end of comment tag". It took two attempts because the first version of the warning
  comment contained the very sequence it was warning about.
- **Cost impact:** ~65 MB once in the restic repository (deduplicated).

### ~20:40 IST — §5 Medium closed: `hcloud-volumes` is no longer a cluster default StorageClass
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + the first sync of the `hetzner-csi` Application.
- **Problem:** both StorageClasses carried `storageclass.kubernetes.io/is-default-class: "true"`, so a
  PVC that omits `storageClassName` was placed nondeterministically — and one of the two candidates
  provisions billed Hetzner volumes through the provisioner S8 is retiring.
- **Fix:** `k8s/hetzner-csi/values.yaml` gains `storageClasses: [{name: hcloud-volumes,
  defaultStorageClass: false, reclaimPolicy: Delete}]` and `rendered-manifests.yaml` is re-rendered
  from the chart (this app vendors its render, with `render.sh` reproducing it from chart 2.20.0).
  - The key is `defaultStorageClass`, **not** `default` — taken from the chart's own values rather than
    guessed, since a wrong key here is a silent no-op that would leave the bug in place.
  - The re-render was diffed against the vendored file first: **only** that one annotation changed
    (`"true"` → `"false"`) plus a trailing newline, so nothing else about the CSI deployment moves.
  - Pre-flight check before syncing an app that had never been synced: the vendored render contains
    **no** `Secret`, so the sync cannot overwrite the live `hcloud-csi` token Secret (which the one
    remaining CSI-backed volume depends on). Confirmed by listing the rendered kinds.
- **Verification:** the `hetzner-csi` Application is now `Synced`, and `kubectl get sc` shows
  `local-path` as the only class carrying the default annotation.
- **Cost impact:** none (arguably negative — it becomes much harder to accidentally provision a
  billed Hetzner volume by omission).

### ~21:30 IST — §13's Grafana fixes (never implemented) plus a real admin-password rotation
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + a `monitoring` sync.
- **Found by auditing §13 against reality**, not by trusting the commit that claimed S9 was done.
  Three prescribed fixes were absent, and one of them mattered a lot:
  1. **§13 fix (a) — no `reset-admin-password` initContainer.** `k8s/monitoring/secrets.sops.yaml`
     still held the literal pair **`admin` / `admin`**, and Grafana reads
     `GF_SECURITY_ADMIN_PASSWORD` *only* when it creates its sqlite database — afterwards the password
     lives in the database. Git was therefore not the source of truth: a UI change diverges silently,
     which is what happened twice and broke the user-provisioning Job with 401s. Added the
     initContainer that resets the password from the Secret on every start, which is what makes git
     authoritative ("change it in git, never in the UI").
     - **And rotated the credential itself.** A default login on an internet-facing admin UI
       (`grafana.upayan.dev`; anonymous auth is off, but the credential was trivial) is not hygiene:
       Grafana admin grants datasource configuration, which is an SSRF-shaped foothold inside the
       cluster network. The new value was generated inline into SOPS (never echoed), so the file is
       now both authoritative and not a default. The old value is dead from this commit on.
     - Verified before touching the live pod, against a **copy** of the live database: the binary is on
       PATH at `/usr/share/grafana/bin/grafana`, the reset exits 0 and modifies the database, and the
       no-database branch is a no-op so a fresh install still takes its first password from the env
       var. A failing reset aborts the start loudly instead of leaving a wrong password.
  2. **§13 fix (c) — `strategy` was `RollingUpdate`** on a Deployment whose state is a sqlite file on
     an RWO volume. On a single node, RWO is not enforced between two pods on the same node, so a
     rolling update can run two Grafana processes against one database file. Now `Recreate`.
  3. **§13 fix (b) — the provisioning Job was still a `PostSync` hook with `backoffLimit: 10`**,
     `restartPolicy: OnFailure` and a floating `alpine:3.20`. The retry count is what turned a wrong
     password into a hung sync. Now `backoffLimit: 2`, `restartPolicy: Never`, image pinned by digest.
     It deliberately stays a hook — a plain Job's pod template is immutable, so spec changes would fail
     to apply — but with a working password, two retries and a pinned image, §13's actual complaint (a
     sync hanging behind auth retries) is addressed. Recorded as a deliberate deviation.
  4. **§13: "all `noDataState: Alerting` for critical ones"** — 12 of the 14 pre-existing rules had
     `noDataState: OK`, *including every critical one* (`node-not-ready`, `disk-root-high`,
     `pod-crashloopbackoff`, `tls-cert-expiring`, …). That is the dead-man problem: if node-exporter or
     a scrape target disappears, those rules go **silent** and the absence of alerts reads as
     all-clear. 12 flipped; the two left as `OK` are the `info` KEDA rule and `prometheus-target-down`
     (whose condition is already covered by the critical rules firing on no-data). **18 of 20** rules
     now fire when their data vanishes.
  5. **Docs stopped publishing the credential.** `docs/monitoring.md` named `admin`/`admin`; it now
     points at the SOPS file. The dated review keeps its original finding, with a correction.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~22:20 IST — the Grafana rotation is verified live (and the sync needed two fixes to get there)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** changelog only (the changes are in the preceding commits).
- **Verified:**
  - **New credential (from SOPS) → HTTP `200`** on `https://grafana.upayan.dev/api/user`.
  - **Old `admin` → HTTP `401`.** The default credential is retired; unauthenticated is also `401`
    (anonymous access stays off).
  - The `PostSync` provisioning Job **Completed** in 39s — it authenticates with the same Secret, so a
    pass is direct evidence that git, the database and the automation now agree. §13's failure mode (a
    sync hanging behind auth retries) is gone.
  - Grafana rolled onto `strategy=Recreate` (new pod, initContainer log:
    `admin password reset from the Secret`), `monitoring` is `Synced`/`Healthy`, operation `Succeeded`.
  - 18 of 20 alert rules now carry `noDataState: Alerting` (loaded in the live Grafana config).
- **Two things the sync taught, both worth keeping:**
  1. **A merge patch never removes a field.** The Deployment still carried `strategy.rollingUpdate`
     from its RollingUpdate days, so `type: Recreate` was rejected
     (`may not be specified when strategy type is 'Recreate'`) and the apply failed **five times
     without rolling anything** — ArgoCD correctly refused rather than applying something invalid.
  2. **`rollingUpdate: null` does not work through ArgoCD**: null fields are stripped during
     rendering, so the stale field survived and the failure repeated at the next revision. The scoped
     fix is `argocd.argoproj.io/sync-options: Replace=true` *on that one Deployment*, which replaces
     the whole object and therefore does drop the field. Safe there specifically because it is one
     replica with no HPA and no controller-added spec fields; it is not a pattern to apply app-wide.
- **Owner action, small but real:** the Grafana admin password has changed. It lives in
  `k8s/monitoring/secrets.sops.yaml` (`grafana-admin-credentials`) and is retrievable with
  `sops -d k8s/monitoring/secrets.sops.yaml`. **Do not change it in the UI** — the initContainer
  resets it from the Secret on every start, by design.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~23:00 IST — the disaster-recovery documentation was describing a world that no longer exists
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** docs only (no cluster change).
- **Why this mattered more than documentation usually does:** `docs/runbooks/recover-vm.md` told an
  incident responder, in the present tense, "there is no Terraform or Ansible in this repo yet, and no
  `vps-data` volume", and instructed them to re-attach two hcloud CSI volumes that S8 had already
  released. `docs/disaster-recovery.md` said "there is no automated, refreshed backup yet" and "RTO:
  unknown, never exercised end-to-end". Both were false after today's S4 work — and a DR page that is
  wrong is worse than one that is missing, because it is read under pressure and believed.
- **Rewritten against the system that now exists:**
  - `disaster-recovery.md`: what to reach for in order (the automated repository → the S0 bundle → the
    workstation mirror), **measured** RPO/RTO instead of estimates (class A RPO ≤ 15 min from the timer;
    RTO for a class-A restore **< 5 min, measured in the drill**, which imports both databases and
    matches row counts exactly), a what-survives-what matrix, and the caveat stated plainly: the
    repository is on the node's root disk, so it does not survive losing the machine.
  - `recover-vm.md`: the Terraform → Ansible → etcd → ArgoCD sequence as the current procedure, keeping
    the two traps that were genuinely valuable (the private-repo bootstrap order that really bit on
    2026-09-28, and the `data_volume` role's refusal to `mkfs`), plus a step the old version could not
    have known: **vcap's MinIO image cannot be pulled from any registry**, so a rebuilt node must
    `k3s ctr images import` the tarball before MinIO starts. Also records that both primary IPs still
    have `auto_delete=true`, so losing the server loses the IPs and DNS must be re-pointed.
  - `docs/README.md`: "has no automated backups" → what actually exists, plus the caveat, without
    losing the "never delete casually" caution (which remains correct for a different reason).
- **Owner-gated items this surfaced but did not fix** (both are Terraform, and applying them touches
  live infrastructure): `hcloud_primary_ip.auto_delete = false` so the IPs survive a server loss, and
  `prevent_destroy` on the `vps-data` volume — the volume holds the class-A data and currently has no
  deletion guard. Both are one-line changes with an exact diff; neither should be applied without the
  owner looking at the plan output.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~23:20 IST — correction: the Hetzner protections were already live (verified, not assumed)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** docs + a stale Terraform comment (no infrastructure change).
- **What happened:** the entry above said `hcloud_primary_ip.auto_delete = false` and `prevent_destroy`
  on the volume still needed applying, quoting §5's findings table. Rather than trust that, I ran a
  scoped `terraform plan` against the live Hetzner API for the server, both primary IPs and the
  volume: **"found no differences, so no changes are needed"** (exit 0).
- **So the real state is:** the server has `delete_protection` **and** `rebuild_protection`; both
  primary IPs have `auto_delete=false` **and** delete protection; the `vps-data` volume is
  delete-protected. All applied and live, all already declared in `terraform/hcloud.tf`.
- **Why this matters beyond the correction:** it means **losing the server does not lose the IPs, so
  DNS survives a rebuild** — a materially better DR story than §5 recorded, and one I had just written
  the opposite of into two brand-new DR pages. Fixed: `terraform/hcloud.tf`'s comments (they claimed
  the live values were the old ones), `disaster-recovery.md`, `recover-vm.md` step 8, `docs/README.md`,
  and a stale-marker on the plan's own findings row so nobody re-does this work.
- **The pattern, again:** this session has repeatedly found "documented as broken, actually fine" and
  "documented as fine, actually broken". Checking the live system took eight seconds and avoided
  propagating a three-week-old finding into documentation written today.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~23:50 IST — the restore runbooks pointed at the wrong source, and CI could not see a broken template
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** docs + CI (no cluster change).
- **The runbooks.** `restore-postgres.md`, `restore-minio.md` and `restore-mongo.md` all described S0 as
  the source of dumps — true when they were written, wrong since S4 landed. Fixing that surfaced two
  real defects:
  - **`restore-postgres.md`'s Variant A did not work.** It stood the scratch pod up with
    `kubectl run … -- sleep infinity`, which overrides the entrypoint, so Postgres never starts and the
    `createdb` that follows fails for a confusing reason. It also hardcoded `postgres:16`, which has no
    PostGIS — a meghmitra restore would have silently lost the extension-owned tables
    (`geometry_columns`, `spatial_ref_sys`), the exact failure the drill taught me about. Now: an
    explicit manifest, the *source app's* image, and the TCP readiness check that only succeeds against
    the final server rather than the entrypoint's temporary one.
  - **`restore-mongo.md` declared a gap that no longer exists** ("no verified backup exists today") and
    framed the replica-set fix as future work. Verified live: `rs.status()` is `rs0` / `PRIMARY` /
    `ok:1`, the `--oplog` dump runs daily, and the database holds **0 documents** (the 369 MB in
    `storage.md` is WiredTiger preallocation) — so the 1.4 KB archive is correct, not a symptom. It now
    also notes the restore needs `--oplogReplay`, because the dump is taken with `--oplog`.
  - `restore-minio.md` claimed a restore-and-diff "has not been verified by an actual test yet" — the
    monthly drill does exactly that (50 restored objects, all present live, measured 2026-09-28). Also
    documents skipping MinIO's churning `.minio.sys/tmp`, and separates the two image-availability
    cases: the containerd cache on a *live* node (still fragile) versus a *rebuild*, which is already
    covered now that the tarball travels in every restic `etcd` snapshot.
- **CI could not see a broken template.** `ansible-playbook --syntax-check` and `ansible-lint` both pass
  a `.j2` file that fails to *parse*, which is how the `${#…}` array-expansion trap reached a live
  playbook (two failed applies, "Missing end of comment tag"). Added
  `scripts/check-ansible-templates.py`, which Jinja-parses every template and names the usual cause when
  a comment marker is the problem, wired into the `ansible` job. Verified in both directions: the 16
  real templates parse cleanly, and a planted trap fails with the hint.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~00:30 IST — the certificate runbook named resources that do not exist
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** docs only (no cluster change).
- **The last two runbooks audited**, finishing the set. `rotate-certificates.md` — the procedure for the
  **top owner-gated item** — was written when S7 had not landed, and had not been updated since:
  - it said "Target procedure (S7, not yet implemented)" while the wildcard is Traefik's live default;
  - **it named a SOPS file and Secret that do not exist** (`k8s/platform/traefik/origin-cert.sops.yaml`,
    Secret `origin-wildcard-tls`). Following it would have written a Secret nothing reads and left the
    real one — `k8s/platform/traefik/secrets.sops.yaml`, Secret `wildcard-upayan-dev-tls` — untouched;
  - it described the private-CA Postgres TLS leaf, and a `vcap_db_cert_expiry_timestamp` metric with a
    60-day alert "see monitoring.md". Postgres runs with **`ssl = off`** (verified) and **that metric
    does not exist anywhere in this repo** — one more "documented but absent".
  Rewritten against verified state: the real resource names and ksops path, both issuance routes
  (dashboard, and the API with **Origin CA: Edit** — the permission that is missing locally, which is
  why this is an owner action), the origin-side verification probe with the expected serial, **`526`**
  named as the failure signal, revocation only after verification, and a recommendation to issue a
  **1-year** certificate rather than 15 — the entire reason this rotation exists is that a long-lived
  key leaked. The Postgres section is now explicitly labelled not-implemented, with a note that whoever
  lands S6's Postgres TLS must create that metric and alert in the same change.
- `add-port.md`: step 3 still said "pre-S2, there is no Hetzner Cloud Firewall". It exists and is
  deliberately permissive; the step now lists exactly what it allows and what a new port therefore
  implies.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~01:15 IST — image-pullability audit: the private images were never protected, and one is already missing
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + Ansible re-apply + a monitoring sync + host-side exports.
- **Why:** §5 called the unpullable MinIO image Critical. That is a *class* of failure — S13 fixed one
  instance, S4 backed up another — so I audited all 52 images the cluster runs against their registries.
- **The audit first corrected itself.** My probe reported 404 for a dozen images, including
  `kedacore/keda`, which this cluster demonstrably pulled. The probe was wrong: GHCR returns 404 for a
  manifest request whose `Accept` header omits the OCI **index** media type. Re-tested with curl and the
  full list, `xarhaanshx/meghmitra/web:edge` and its digest both return **200**. The public images are
  fine; those 404s were artifacts of the instrument, not findings. (Third time this session that the
  lesson "verify the instrument before believing the reading" has paid.)
- **The real finding is worse than §5 recorded.** The five private images — vcap `api`, `auth`,
  `worker`, frontend `web`, and `bitvaultd` — run purely from the node's containerd cache, because
  **every registry credential in this cluster is dead**: the tokens inside `vcap-{dev,staging}/pull-secret`
  and `bitvault/pull-secret` are classic `ghp_` PATs that return **403** against GHCR's token endpoint
  (verified directly, not inferred). S11's outstanding "needs an owner `read:packages` token" is
  therefore not a tracking nicety — it is the difference between a rebuild that works and one that does
  not, for the two apps that hold real data.
  - **`vcap-staging-backend/worker`'s image is not in the node cache at all** (`image not found`) and
    cannot be pulled. It is a KEDA scale-from-zero component, so the next time a job is submitted it
    will fail with `ImagePullBackOff` — a latent, user-visible outage for the class-A staging app.
  - **`bitvaultd`'s manifest is cached but its blobs were GC-pruned** (`content digest … not found` on
    export). The running container is unaffected; the image can be neither exported nor pulled.
- **Done anyway:** exported every at-risk image that can be exported — vcap `api` (113 MB), `auth`
  (7.7 MB), frontend `web` (106 MB) plus the S0 MinIO tarball — into `/var/backups/images`, and
  repointed the 12h infrastructure backup at that directory (it previously globbed only `minio-*.tar`
  inside the S0 bundle). Verified: the run reports **4 image tarball(s)**, adds 239.9 MiB (218.5 MiB
  stored) and snapshot `24148426` lists all four.
  - Two things cost a wasted attempt and are worth recording: exports must use the `repo@sha256:…` form
    — ArgoCD writes `repo:tag@sha256:…`, which `ctr` rejects — and the candidate list must come from
    *workload templates*, not running pods, or KEDA scale-to-zero workloads are invisible.
- **A second gap closed:** nothing alerted on `ImagePullBackOff` or `CreateContainerConfigError`, which
  is exactly how the worker's state would have stayed unnoticed until a user hit it. Added
  `pod-image-pull-failing` (warning, 10m) over kube-state-metrics' waiting-reason series — 21 rules now,
  19 of them firing on no-data.
- **Owner-gated, and now precisely stated:** a working `read:packages` token restores pullability for
  all five images and unblocks the MinIO mirror. Until then, two of the five are documented rather than
  protected, and the vcap worker is a live latent failure.
- **Cost impact:** ~218 MB stored in the restic repository, once (deduplicated afterwards).

### ~03:05 IST — correction: my bitvault edit was wrong twice, and how each caught itself
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** changelog only (the fixes are in the two commits before this entry).
- **Mistake 1 — the value never applied.** I updated `bitvault-ghcr-pull` in-cluster first, and ArgoCD
  reverted it: the Secret *is* git-managed (SOPS). Correct — that is rule 1 doing its job, and it is
  why the fix belongs in the encrypted file.
- **Mistake 2 — I wrote base64 into `stringData`.** That Secret stores its payload as **`stringData`**
  (plaintext), but I wrote a base64-encoded dockerconfigjson, which would have shipped a *corrupted*
  credential. It did not reach the cluster: ArgoCD refused it
  (`Secret "bitvault-ghcr-pull" is invalid: data[.dockerconfigjson]: Invalid value: invalid character
  'e' looking for beginning of value` — the `e` of `eyJ…`, i.e. base64 where JSON was expected), and
  my own parse of the file failed for the same reason. Rewrote it as a plain JSON block scalar, then
  verified in three layers before trusting it: the decrypted file parses as JSON, `kustomize` + ksops
  renders `stringData` with valid JSON (199 bytes), and the applied Secret's token authenticates
  (`auth 200`, private manifest `200`).
  - **Lesson worth keeping:** `data` values are base64, `stringData` values are not, and a Secret may
    use either. Check which one the specific file uses rather than assuming; the failure mode is a
    credential that looks present and works for nobody.
- **Addendum to the entry above:** its claim that bitvaultd "was re-pulled" was written before that pull
  had actually succeeded — it failed on the stale secret. With the credential now correct, the image
  has been pulled again and exported into `/var/backups/images`, which the earlier GC-pruned blobs had
  made impossible.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~02:40 IST — a working registry token landed, and it closed four things at once
- **Actor:** owner (created the token) + agent (used it) — omp session.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + in-cluster Secret updates + a registry push.
- **What the owner did:** refreshed the `gh` CLI token with **`write:packages`**. That single action
  resolved the item I had ranked first, because every registry credential in this cluster was dead.
- **What it fixed, each verified:**
  1. **The live latent failure.** `vcap-staging-backend/worker`'s image was absent from the node and
     unpullable, so the next KEDA scale-up would have failed. The image is now in containerd's cache
     (420 MB) — pulled with a throwaway pod that carried the pull-secret.
  2. **`argocd-image-updater` is at `errors=0`** (from 17 → 6 → 0) with `images_updated=5`, and the
     private packages appear as tracked rather than denied. That closes the last open S11 item.
  3. **The MinIO mirror exists at last.** `quay.io/minio/minio:RELEASE.2025-04-22` was pushed to
     `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/mirror/minio` (61 MB, `--platform linux/amd64`, verified pullable:
     manifest HTTP 200), and vcap-dev, vcap-staging and bitvault all now **run from the mirror** —
     each `Running`, ready, zero restarts. The containerd-cache dependency that S0 could not remove is
     gone.
  4. **bitvaultd was re-pulled**, restoring the blobs image GC had pruned.
- **The repo's guardrails caught me, correctly.** I first updated `bitvault-ghcr-pull` with `kubectl`
  — and ArgoCD **reverted it within seconds**, because that Secret *is* git-managed (SOPS, in
  `k8s/apps/bitvault/secrets.sops.yaml`). That is rule 1 working exactly as documented; the fix went
  into the encrypted file instead. Worth remembering: the live-only secrets in this cluster are the
  vcap pull-secrets, not bitvault's.
- **Verification subtlety worth recording:** `gh auth token` returns a *different* string on later
  invocations, which made fingerprint comparison look like the copies had gone stale. They had not —
  the copies in `vcap-{dev,staging}/pull-secret` and `argocd/ghcr-creds` were each tested functionally
  (GHCR token endpoint 200 **and** a private manifest fetch 200). **Do not verify these by comparing
  fingerprints to `gh auth token`; test the auth flow.** Rotating again: refresh the CLI token, re-copy
  into the four Secrets (bitvault's in SOPS), delete the image-updater pod.
- **Still open, and it is the last S7 item:** the Cloudflare Origin CA certificate. The replacement
  keypair and CSR are generated and parked at `~/.config/vps/cf-origin-2026-09/` with instructions;
  issuing the certificate is behind Cloudflare's MFA, so it needs the owner. Until then the origin
  keeps serving the certificate whose private key was committed.
- **Cost impact:** +61 MB in GHCR (free tier), no cluster cost.

### ~03:40 IST — S7 closed: the origin certificate now has a key that was never committed, and the old one is revoked
- **Actor:** owner (minted a Cloudflare API token) + agent (did the rotation) — omp session.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + a `traefik-config` sync + a Cloudflare API revocation.
- **Why this was the top security item:** the certificate the origin had served since 2026-05-10 had
  its private key committed in plaintext (`k8s/traefik/key.pem`, deleted from the tree on 2026-09-28
  but still in git history), and it remained valid until **2041**. Deleting the file changed nothing;
  a reissue was the only fix.
- **What happened:**
  1. Generated a fresh keypair + CSR locally (public key `30b06d23…`, deliberately different from the
     committed `33ef16eb…`), SANs `*.upayan.dev` and `vps.upayan.dev`.
  2. Issued the certificate through the **Cloudflare Origin CA API** — `POST /certificates` — for one
     year (2026-09-28 → 2027-09-28), serial `699090E41816073595D26CDDE0568CFE6C66BCB7`.
  3. Put cert + key into `k8s/platform/traefik/secrets.sops.yaml` (Secret `wildcard-upayan-dev-tls`,
     `data` field, base64 — checked rather than assumed, because the bitvault Secret earlier that day
     used `stringData` and the two are not interchangeable).
  4. Synced and **verified the origin serves it** on three separate hostnames, with all public
     endpoints `200` (no `526`, i.e. Cloudflare's Full (strict) validation passes).
  5. **Then** revoked the old certificate (id `18815063215507537879465766570076134496689012063`),
     after confirming nothing served it. Active certificates in the zone went 14 → 13.
  6. Shredded the parked plaintext private key, after confirming the SOPS copy matched it byte for
     byte by public-key fingerprint.
- **API lessons recorded in the runbook** (each cost a failed attempt):
  - the dashboard is behind MFA; the API is not — which is the whole reason a token was better here;
  - `csr` must be the **PEM text**, not base64 DER — base64 returns `1007 CSR parsed as empty`, which
    reads like a malformed CSR rather than an encoding mismatch;
  - list and revoke are **zone-scoped** (`?zone_id=…`) and need the **full 47-character id**; a
    truncated id fails with `1101 Failed to read certificate from Database` (I hit that by printing
    ids through a `[:24]` slice, then quoted them as if complete).
- **Useful finding for the plan:** the "leaked 2025-03-29 certificate" that §17 S7 lists as an
  outstanding revocation is **not among the zone's active certificates** — either already revoked or
  never activated — so there is nothing to revoke for that item. Eleven unused certificates from past
  rotations remain (four from the 2025 era, seven multi-host ones from early 2026); they are inert and
  were left alone, listed in the report for the owner to decide on.
- **Owner-visible change:** none, by design — same hostnames, same Cloudflare edge certificate, no
  endpoint changed. Only the origin's certificate, and the private key behind it, are different.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~04:10 IST — R2 checked with the token: both intended buckets exist and are empty; the TF backend is local
- **Actor:** agent (omp session) — owner supplied a Cloudflare token with R2 scope.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit); no cluster or infrastructure change.
- **R2 inventory (read via the API, `usage` endpoint):**
  | bucket | created | region | class | objects | size |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | `vps-backups` | 2026-09-27 | WEUR | Standard | **0** | **0 B** |
  | `vps-tfstate` | 2026-09-27 | WEUR | Standard | **0** | **0 B** |
  | `bitvault-opennext-cache` | 2026-05-01 | APAC | Standard | 217 | 17.0 MiB |
  | `memory-book-dev` | 2026-06-22 | APAC | Standard | 16 | 6.9 MiB |
  So the offsite target exists and has never been written to — consistent with S4's offsite leg still
  being switched off. The other two buckets belong to other projects; untouched.
- **Finding worth acting on: `vps-tfstate` is unused.** `terraform/backend.tf` still declares
  `backend "local"` with the S3 block commented out, so the authoritative state is a **19 KB
  gitignored file on the operator's workstation**. Losing it loses Terraform's view of the
  infrastructure — recoverable by re-importing, but avoidable. Wiring R2 as the backend needs the same
  S3 credentials as the backup leg, so both wait on the same key pair.
- **The API cannot mint long-lived R2 S3 keys.** `POST …/r2/api_tokens` and `…/r2/tokens` both return
  `10015 No route matches this url`; the only credential route that exists is
  `…/r2/temp-access-credentials` (`400 The JSON you provided was not well formed`, i.e. the route is
  real) and those are short-lived by design — unusable as a restic repository credential. So the last
  step needs one dashboard action: **R2 → Manage R2 API Tokens → Create API Token**, scoped to
  `vps-backups` with Object Read & Write.
- **healthchecks.io is declined** by the owner (2026-09-28) — staleness alerting stays on the Grafana
  rules over the metrics the backup scripts already write. Removed from the outstanding lists.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~13:00 IST — class-A cadence to 30 min, then a self-inflicted backup outage (found and fixed in ~10 min)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session) — a change the owner asked for, plus an incident the agent caused.
- **Type:** git commit (this entry's commit) + two Ansible runs against the host + a break-glass timer
  restart, all logged here because none of them are visible to ArgoCD.
- **Cadence change (requested):** class A moves from `OnUnitActiveSec=15min` to `30min`. Reason: the
  R2 free tier allows 1M Class A operations/month, and restic lists the repository on every run, so
  the timer interval is the multiplier. Owner approved on the grounds that storage is not the risk.
  **This lengthens the class-A RPO from 15 to 30 minutes** — a documented guarantee, so every doc
  claiming 15 min was updated rather than left stale: `docs/backups.md`, `docs/README.md`,
  `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/disaster-recovery.md` (including the RPO table, which now records the
  change and why), `docs/runbooks/restore-postgres.md`, `docs/runbooks/restore-minio.md`, and the
  role README. The plan's own RPO table still says ≤15 min — that is the historical proposal, left
  as-is. Whether the cadence can return to 15 min depends on the measured operation counts once R2 is
  wired; the docs say so.
- **Incident, and it was the agent's fault.** The role's `Render the restic environment file` task was
  run from the repo root instead of `ansible/`. Ansible reads a cwd `ansible.cfg` only, so
  `ansible/ansible.cfg` — the file that enables `community.sops`' vars plugin — was never loaded, the
  `restic_password` var stayed as `ENC[AES256_GCM,…]` ciphertext, and the role dutifully rendered
  **that string** as `/etc/vps-backup/env`'s `RESTIC_PASSWORD`. Every class-A backup then failed with
  `Fatal: wrong password or no key found`, starting 12:57, roughly two minutes after the render.
  - **Diagnosis:** the env file's size had grown 308 → 431 bytes; `grep -c "ENC\["` on it returned 1
    (i.e. the value was still ciphertext, not a password); and the SOPS value, piped straight into a
    `restic snapshots` on the node, **opened the repository** — proving the secret was correct and
    only the render was wrong.
  - **Blast radius:** none to data. The repository was never at risk and the password was never lost;
    the only cost was ~4 minutes of class-A snapshots not running, and one further failed run during
    verification.
  - **Fix:** re-ran the playbook from `ansible/`, so `community.sops` decrypted normally. Verified:
    the env is 308 bytes with zero `ENC[` lines, `systemctl start vps-backup-classA.service` returns
    `Result=success`, restic reports `processed 71 files, 20.390 MiB` and saves snapshot `e8dae142`.
  - **Guard added** so it cannot fail silently again: the role now asserts before rendering that
    `restic_password` is non-empty and does not begin with `ENC[`, failing loudly with a message
    naming the cause. The README's apply instructions now show the `cd ansible` step explicitly.
- **Also corrected:** the service unit's description still advertised "15-min", and its comment
  claimed the env file carries the R2 credentials (it carries `RESTIC_PASSWORD` alone until R2 is
  wired). Both fixed and applied.
- **R2 state (checked, not changed):** bucket `vps-backups` exists in WEUR, Standard, and is **empty**
  — 0 objects and 0 bytes, so this leg has never run. `vps-tfstate` is likewise empty because the
  Terraform backend is still `local`. The node can already reach R2's S3 API (a TLS-valid
  `InvalidArgument / Authorization` for an unsigned request). Long-lived R2 keys cannot be minted via
  the API — only `temp-access-credentials` exists — so the remaining step is one dashboard action.
- **Cost impact:** none.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, image-updater registry credential + docker/ archive)

### ~13:40 IST — S11: removed the dead registry credential, so image-updater tracks the public majority again
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `477dd57`.
- **Trigger:** the open S11 item — `argocd-image-updater` was failing on *every* image on every cycle
  (`Could not get tags from registry: … denied: denied`, `images_considered=17 errors=17`).
- **Finding that changed the fix approach:** a *rejected* credential is worse than no credential,
  because it is sent on every request and makes even **public** packages fail. So this did not need to
  wait for an owner token: measured anonymously per package
  (`GET ghcr.io/token?scope=repository:<pkg>:pull&service=ghcr.io`), **18 of the 23 configured images
  are publicly readable**. Only 5 are genuinely private: `upayanmazumder/bitvault/bitvaultd`,
  `vitscopesoftwareteam/vcap-staging-backend/{api,auth,worker}`,
  `vitscopesoftwareteam/vcap-staging-frontend/web`.
- **Change:** `k8s/platform/argocd-image-updater/values.yaml` — removed
  `credentials: pullsecret:argocd/ghcr-creds` from the `ghcr` registry entry (registry stays
  `default: true`, so anonymous pulls are used), with the reasoning written into the file itself.
- **Verification:** - **Verification:** live, after a hard refresh of the `argocd-image-updater` Application plus a
  `rollout restart` (a ConfigMap change alone does not restart the pod — the first refresh also
  stayed pinned to the previous revision until the annotation cleared, worth knowing for next time):
  - Cycle summary went `images_updated=0 errors=17` (every cycle, previously) to
    **`images_updated=10 errors=6`**, then `images_updated=4 errors=6`. It is genuinely working again,
    not just quieter.
  - It is now writing digest pins back to git: six commits on `main` authored by
    `argocd-image-updater <image-updater@upayan.dev>` within a minute of the fix
    (`build: automatic update of {bandit,cheatsheet,kodesphere,learning-react,status-page,upayan-web}`),
    each writing/updating the app's `.argocd-source-<app>.yaml` — e.g. `status-page` api+app
    `latest` → `sha256:7fb858bf…` / `sha256:10cdf429…`. This is the intended `git` write-back.
  - `level=error` since the restart names **only** the four private packages predicted:
    `upayanmazumder/bitvault/bitvaultd`, `vitscopesoftwareteam/vcap-staging-backend/{api,auth}`,
    `vitscopesoftwareteam/vcap-staging-frontend/web`.
  - Live `ConfigMap/argocd-image-updater-config` now renders `registries` with no `credentials` key;
    Application `Synced`/`Healthy`.
- **Side effect worth noting:** because the write-back method is `git:secret:argocd/git-creds` for 16
  apps, unblocking the registry reads switched on automated digest commits to `main`. Pull before
  pushing; a direct `git push` from a stale local `main` will be rejected.
- **Still owner-gated:** the 5 private packages cannot be tracked until a PAT with `read:packages`
  (or per-package access) is supplied. Nothing is functionally broken by that — they are digest-pinned
  and pulled via their own `pull-secret`s (`vcap-dev/pull-secret`, `vcap-staging/pull-secret`).
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~13:20 IST — archived the dead `docker/` stack, and made the rotation runbook executable
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit `b47f4a2`.
- **Change:** `git mv docker archived/docker` (13 files) plus `archived/docker/README.md`;
  `AGENTS.md` corrected — it described `docker/` as a *live* second (non-Kubernetes) deployment path;
  `docs/runbooks/rotate-secrets.md` rewritten from a stale generic stub into an executable
  per-credential procedure (where each credential lives, what consumes it, the rotate step, and the
  verification that must pass before the old value is revoked).
- **Evidence it is dead, rather than assumption:** `docker/Caddyfile` serves the *same* hostnames as
  the Kubernetes Ingresses (`ks.upayan.dev`, `api.upayan.dev`, `api.react.dj.upayan.dev`,
  `api.smart-home-system.upayan.dev`, …), and Traefik holds 80/443 on the node and answers those hosts
  today — so a Caddy from that compose file cannot be bound. This is an inference from who holds the
  port, not an SSH check: the node's SSH is Tailscale-only and no key was available this session.
- **Finding recorded, deliberately not fixed:** `archived/docker/*.env` (8 files) and
  `kodesphere.private-key.pem` are a **second plaintext copy** of credentials belonging to apps still
  running in Kubernetes (`mochi`, `kodesphere`, `cas-api`). Removing the files does not retire them
  (they remain in git history) — rotation is the fix.
- **Also documented:** *why* the vcap Helm values cannot be `ksops`-encrypted — the chart is sourced
  from git (`path: k8s/chart`), and `kustomize --enable-helm` only consumes HTTP Helm repos, so that
  gap would require publishing the chart to a Helm repo or vendoring it into this repo. Written into
  the runbook so it is not re-attempted blind.
- **Cost impact:** none.

### ~13:05 IST — consolidation side effect: in-cluster digest pins were dropped by the re-home
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** changelog only.
- **What changed, beyond the rename:** the old Applications had `argocd-image-updater` digest pins applied *in-cluster* (the `argocd` write-back method, which is why `meghmitra`/`mochi`/`rankstack`/`upayan-v5` omitted `write-back-method`). The successor Applications do not carry those pins, so the workloads went back to their git tags (`:edge`, `:latest`) and the kubelet resolved them to whatever build those tags point at now.
- **Observed effect:** `meghmitra`'s api/web pods now run `ghcr.io/xarhaanshx/meghmitra/{api,web}:edge`; that is a newer build than the digest the old Application had pinned, and it is the most likely explanation for the `/rules` response serialization change noted above (its `migrate` initContainer ran the newer image's migrations; all 51 table row counts are unchanged).
- **Consequence, and why it is acceptable:** image-updater re-establishes digest pins on its next successful pass — it is already tracking the renamed apps correctly (`application=meghmitra`, `image_tag=edge` in its logs). It cannot complete a pass until `argocd/ghcr-creds` holds a working token, which is still the open owner-supplied item.
- **Note for anyone reading the images later:** for the 11 ApplicationSet apps the default write-back is now explicitly `git`, so their digest pins will land as commits in `kustomization.yaml` (a change from the previous in-cluster overrides, and the reason `write-back-method` is set in `apps.yaml`).
- **Cost impact:** none.

Origin-direct probes for bandit/status-page/grafana/api.upayan.dev still return the wildcard (`SAN: *.upayan.dev, vps.upayan.dev`). Traefik TLS errors: **0** in a 60 s window.

Cloudflare's Always-Use-HTTPS already returns **301 for every host** (verified on five), so the observable outcome is already achieved. Traefik runs a single replica, so the config change costs a brief cluster-wide ingress blip, and the k3s-packaged Traefik chart's value key for this cannot be validated locally (`additionalArguments` vs `ports.web.redirectTo`) — so it would be a blind change to the ingress path for no measurable gain. Left as an explicit owner decision rather than done for symmetry.
