- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** N/A in practice — the six wildcard duplicates are identical to a Secret that still exists and is in git; the rest were stale. If anything is ever needed, the pre-change etcd snapshot still holds them.

### ~16:50 IST — S5: the *other* plaintext secrets (Hetzner token, GitHub creds, keel) — plus a CI guard and a green CI
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), continuing "finish other incomplete stuff".
- **Type:** git commits (this repo, `main`) + break-glass (none — all through git).
- **What the earlier conversion missed, and why:** the first pass keyed off `grep -rl '^kind: Secret$' k8s/apps k8s/platform k8s/monitoring` — three directories. A proper repo-wide scan by *parsing* every YAML under `k8s/` in a `Secret`-with-values sense found four more plaintext files, including the worst one:
  - `k8s/hetzner-csi/secret.yaml` → the **Hetzner API token** (`kube-system/hcloud-csi`), which is full project write — it can delete the server and volumes. This is the token documented in `docs/secrets.md`.
  - `k8s/argocd/git-creds.yaml` → the GitHub credential used for image-updater's git write-back.
  - `k8s/argocd/repo-credentials.yaml` → three GitHub repo credentials (`argocd-repo-vps`, `repo-vcap-backend`, `repo-vcap-frontend`).
  - `k8s/keel/secret.yaml` → keel's GitHub webhook secret, in a directory that `k8s/docs/02-target-architecture.md` already listed as **deleted** (keel was decommissioned).
- **Change:**
  - `k8s/hetzner-csi/secret.yaml` → `secrets.sops.yaml` + `secret-generator.yaml` + kustomization `generators:` (that directory is a kustomize app, so the ksops path works directly).
  - The four ArgoCD credential Secrets moved into `k8s/platform/argocd/secret/secrets.sops.yaml` (the ksops directory already applied by the `argocd` Application). They could **not** stay where they were: `k8s/argocd/` is the `root` Application's source and root uses a *directory* source, which cannot run a kustomize generator. Consequence, accepted and checked: root prunes the now-absent plaintext files while the `argocd` Application supplies the same Secrets.
  - `k8s/keel/` deleted entirely (dead: no Application, and its target state per the docs was "deleted").
  - **`scripts/check-secrets.sh` gained a second check** that closes this whole class of problem: any `Secret` manifest under `k8s/` with non-empty `data`/`stringData` and no `sops:` metadata fails the build. Tested both ways — it passes on the repo as it now stands and fails on a deliberately planted plaintext Secret (which was then removed). A `Secret` with an empty `data:` (a template) is still allowed.
  - `docs/certificates.md`: the Traefik `web → websecure` redirect is recorded as **decided against** by the owner, not left pending.
  - `terraform/hcloud.tf`: `terraform fmt -check -recursive` had been failing on it (comment alignment: two trailing comments needed a second space before `#`), which is why **every** CI run had been red — including ones from earlier in the session that I had assumed were passing. Fixed; the CI run for that commit is **green** on all three jobs (`terraform`, `kustomize-and-schema`, `ansible`).
- **Verification:** the encrypted hetzner-csi Secret renders 1/1 identical to the plaintext original, and the argocd secret directory renders 4/4 identical, both through real ksops decryption with values hashed rather than eyeballed; live-cluster hash comparison of all five Secrets before/after in the addendum. `scripts/check-secrets.sh` passes (positive) and fails on a planted plaintext Secret (negative). `terraform fmt -check` clean, `terraform validate` "Success!" locally with the provider lock file unchanged.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores the plaintext files and the keel directory. Note the plaintext *values* were already in git history, so a revert does not change the exposure either way — rotation is the fix for that.

### ~17:05 IST — incident: re-homing ArgoCD's own repo credentials briefly broke repo access (recovered)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** incident + break-glass recovery (`sops -d … | kubectl apply -f -` of four Secrets) — logged here as the audit trail.
- **What happened.** The commit above moved `git-creds` and the three `repo-*` credential Secrets out of `k8s/argocd/` (the `root` Application's directory source) into `k8s/platform/argocd/secret/secrets.sops.yaml` (the `argocd` Application's ksops directory), because a *directory* source cannot run a kustomize generator. The sequence that followed — and that I had noted as an accepted transient — turned out to be a **deadlock, not a transient**:
  1. `root` could still fetch the repo (the credentials existed), saw the plaintext files were gone, and pruned the four Secrets.
  2. The `argocd` Application was supposed to recreate them — but to render its manifests it must fetch `upayanmazumder/vps`, which needs *those very credentials*.
  3. Every Application then reported `ComparisonError: Failed to load target state: … failed to get git client for repo https://github.com/upayanmazumder/vps`, and ArgoCD could not self-heal.
- **Impact:** ArgoCD could not reconcile anything for roughly five minutes. Nothing was deleted or damaged (a failed comparison leaves resources alone), no workload went down, and all public endpoints stayed `200`/`302` throughout. The credentials themselves were never lost — the values existed in the encrypted file.
- **Recovery:** decrypted `k8s/platform/argocd/secret/secrets.sops.yaml` locally with `age-ops` and applied the four Secrets directly (values never printed; the temporary file was shredded). ArgoCD picked them up on its next reconcile, its `ComparisonError` cleared, and the `argocd` Application then synced and **now owns all four Secrets** (`Synced`). Spot-checked after: all five credentials (`git-creds`, `argocd-repo-vps`, `repo-vcap-backend`, `repo-vcap-frontend`, `hcloud-csi`) hash **UNCHANGED** from before the move.
- **Root cause, stated plainly:** a private repo's credentials cannot be bootstrapped *from* that repo. Any arrangement that declares them in the repo has this hazard exactly once per re-home; the state is stable afterwards, because a failed render never deletes anything.
- **Durable fix:** the recovery procedure and the bootstrap ordering are now written down in three places — `docs/secrets.md`, `.claude/skills/secrets-tls/SKILL.md`, and `docs/runbooks/recover-vm.md`'s from-scratch bootstrap (which now starts with `sops -d … | kubectl apply -f -` **before** `kubectl apply -f k8s/bootstrap/root-app.yaml`). `recover-vm.md`'s obsolete caveat that "Secrets not yet committed to git are lost" is corrected: every secret is committed in SOPS form, so a rebuild only needs an age private key.
- **Final state:** all Applications `Synced`/`Healthy` except the accepted pre-existing `hetzner-csi`; all pods `Running`/`Completed`; every public endpoint at baseline; CI green on all three jobs.
- **Cost impact:** none.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S5 — secret conversion finished)

### ~16:15 IST — S5: the remaining 20 plaintext Secret manifests are now SOPS-encrypted
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on the owner's "finish other incomplete stuff".
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`). No break-glass action.
- **Trigger:** S5's *tooling* and one pilot app landed earlier in this programme (`cheatsheet`), but the plan's actual "convert secrets" step was unfinished: 16 plaintext `Secret` manifests were still committed across `k8s/apps/**` and `k8s/monitoring/**`, and `docs/secrets.md` said so ("not yet for the other ~34 plaintext Secret manifests"). The owner's instruction was to finish it.
- **Change:** 11 kustomization directories, 20 Secrets. Each directory gained a `secrets.sops.yaml` (the Secret manifests, SOPS-encrypted with the existing `age-ops`/`age-cluster` recipients — `stringData` is what gets encrypted, per `.sops.yaml`'s `encrypted_regex`) and a `secret-generator.yaml` (the `ksops` generator), and lost its plaintext file(s):
  `bandit` (1), `bitvault` (4), `cas-api` (1), `kodesphere` (2 files), `meghmitra` (2), `mochi` (1), `smart-home-system-api` (1), `status-page` (2), `upayan-v5` (2), `upayan-web` (1), `monitoring` (3 grafana secrets).
  **No plaintext `Secret` manifest remains anywhere in `k8s/`.**
- **Verification — value-level, not "the build succeeded":**
  1. Before touching anything, a sha256 of each Secret's `stringData` was captured from the plaintext files **and** independently from the live cluster. All 20 matched, which proves the plaintext files were faithful mirrors of the cluster (i.e. that the conversion has real values to preserve).
  2. After converting, every directory was re-rendered through the real plugin path (`kustomize build --enable-alpha-plugins --enable-exec`, i.e. actual ksops decryption) and each rendered Secret's `stringData` hashed against the baseline: **all 20 identical**.
  3. `scripts/check-secrets.sh` passes (it requires real `sops:`+`age:` metadata in every `secrets.sops.yaml`, which also catches a plaintext file accidentally renamed into that pattern) and `scripts/check-ports.py` passes.
  4. CI's plaintext build pass skips `ksops`-generator directories, which each of the 11 now are — same mechanism the `cheatsheet` pilot established.
  5. Live post-sync comparison in the addendum below (the decisive check: the in-cluster values must be untouched by the change).
- **Note for anyone rendering locally:** `kustomize build` on these directories needs `SOPS_AGE_KEY_FILE=the offline age key`; without it ksops fails with "0 successful groups" and the build errors out. In-cluster this is the `argocd/sops-age` Secret mounted at `/.config/sops/age` (unchanged by this commit).
- **What this does *not* do:** the plaintext values still exist in git **history**, and they are still **unrotated** — encryption is not rotation. `docs/secrets.md`'s rotation order and the post-rotation `git filter-repo` + `gitleaks` rewrite remain pending and owner-gated, as does converting the Helm *values* files that carry credentials (`k8s/apps/vcap/values/*.yaml`, which needs `helm-secrets`, not ksops).
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores the plaintext manifests; the live Secrets are untouched either way (verified by hash).

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S7 — cert-manager removal)

### ~15:20 IST — S7: cert-manager removed; the stuck-Terminating namespaces resolved (and correctly diagnosed)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), continuing S7 after the owner's "continue".
- **Type:** git commits (this repo, `main`) + break-glass `kubectl` (namespace/cluster-scoped deletions, `/finalize` ×4) + a delegated docs sweep across 7 files.
- **Change:**
  - **ArgoCD takes Traefik's default certificate.** `server.ingress.tls: false` and `server.certificate.enabled: false` in `k8s/platform/argocd/values.yaml`. Its Let's Encrypt cert had to renew (valid to 2026-11-22) with a renewal-failure mode; the Origin CA wildcard is valid to 2041 with no renewal step.
  - **Grafana too:** the `tls:` block is gone from `k8s/monitoring/grafana-ingress.yaml`. It was there that **cert-manager's ingress-shim** was continuously creating `Certificate/grafana-tls` (owned by the Ingress, `controller: true`) — i.e. grafana, not argocd, was cert-manager's last user.
  - **cert-manager removed:** `k8s/argocd/applications/platform/cert-manager.yaml` and `k8s/platform/cert-manager/values.yaml` deleted; its resources then deleted by hand — 6 CRDs, 2 webhooks, 13 ClusterRoles, 10 ClusterRoleBindings, and its namespace. The `ClusterIssuer letsencrypt-http` went with the CRDs.
  - **Dead config removed:** the `platform` AppProject's `cert-manager` destination and `https://charts.jetstack.io` source, plus argocd's now-inert `certificate:` block.
  - **Docs:** `.claude/CLAUDE.md`, `.claude/skills/cluster-state/SKILL.md`, `.claude/skills/secrets-tls/SKILL.md` (its ClusterIssuer/Certificate examples were replaced by the Origin CA + TLSStore reality), `AGENTS.md`, `k8s/GUIDE.md`, `docs/architecture.md`, `docs/certificates.md`.
- **Two things worth reading if you only read one part of this:**
  1. **Deleting an ArgoCD Application does not delete its resources unless that Application carried the `resources-finalizer`.** `cert-manager`'s did not — so pruning the Application left all three Deployments running, now managed by nothing. Same trap as the consolidation's rename (where the finalizer *was* present and had to be stripped). Both directions of this have now bitten in one day.
  2. **The four namespaces stuck in `Terminating` were never a finalizer problem** — `.claude/CLAUDE.md` said "Finalizers not released", which is wrong. The condition is `NamespaceDeletionDiscoveryFailure: … external.metrics.k8s.io/v1beta1: stale GroupVersion discovery`: the namespace controller could not enumerate API groups because KEDA's aggregated metrics APIService was briefly unreachable, and it never retried to a successful state. `velero`, `hello-kitty` and `ns-68f3b786…-nginx` had been stuck that way for a long time — they are the repo's "two namespaces stuck Terminating" known-broken item, now **resolved**: all four were proven empty (0 objects across 12 kinds each) and cleared by emptying `.spec.finalizers` through the `/finalize` subresource. `CLAUDE.md`'s known-broken table has been rewritten accordingly, with the gotcha recorded (note the command there uses `jq`; the run used an inline Python equivalent).
- **Verification:** all Applications `Synced`/`Healthy` except the pre-existing `hetzner-csi`; all pods `Running`/`Completed`; `argocd.upayan.dev` → 200, `grafana.upayan.dev` → 302 (login redirect), and every other public host unchanged from baseline; origin-direct probes for argocd and grafana return the Cloudflare Origin CA wildcard; cert-manager objects are **0** in every category (deployments, CRDs, webhooks, ClusterRoles, ClusterRoleBindings, ClusterIssuers); the `cert-manager`, `velero` and `hello-kitty` namespaces no longer exist; the orphan `ClusterRoleBinding/velero-upgrade-crds` is deleted. `kubernetes.io/tls` Secrets cluster-wide: **2** (`kube-system/k3s-serving`, k3s-managed, and `kube-system/wildcard-upayan-dev-tls`).
- **Cost impact:** positive — cert-manager's three Deployments stop being scheduled (~70–100 MiB, the plan's estimate).
- **Rollback:** `git revert` restores the Application and values; cert-manager reinstalls on the next sync. ArgoCD's and grafana's certs would need their `tls:`/`certificate` settings restored in the same revert. No data or credential is involved.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S7 — TLS)

### ~13:45 IST — S7: Traefik's default certificate made explicit + the six broken per-app TLS references removed
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), starting S7 after the owner's "continue".
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — `k8s/platform/traefik/` (+ SOPS Secret, TLSStore), and `tls:` stanzas removed from six Ingresses.
- **Two findings first, because they change what S7 actually has left to do:**
  1. **The certificate rotation S7 assumed was still pending has already happened.** `docs/certificates.md` says "the current Origin cert's key is in git history, so it gets re-issued and the old one revoked after cutover". A Cloudflare Origin CA private key *is* in this repo's history (`k8s vm1/v2/cloudflare-key.pem`, whose cert is `notBefore Mar 29 2025`), but its public key (`sha256 01e6a788…`) matches **no live Secret**, while the certificate actually served is a different, newer one (`notBefore May 10 2026`, SAN `*.upayan.dev, vps.upayan.dev`, public key `sha256 a2c642d0…`). So the wildcard in use is not the leaked one. The leaked 2025 certificate is still within its validity (to 2040), so **revoking it is still worth doing** — that needs a Cloudflare token with Origin CA permission, i.e. an owner-supplied credential, and is not done here.
  2. **The "legacy per-app TLS secrets" were not working at all.** Traefik logs `Error configuring TLS error="secret <ns>/<name>-tls does not exist"` on every reconcile for `bandit`, `cheatsheet`, `learning-react`, `meghmitra`, `status-page` and `upayan-web`: each Ingress named a Secret in *its own* namespace, but those Secrets were created in `default`. Traefik therefore ignored them and fell back to its default certificate. HTTPS worked only by accident — `monitoring/grafana-tls` and `upayan-v5/upayan-v5-tls` happen to contain the *same* wildcard, Traefik loads them because those Ingresses do resolve, and it then serves the wildcard by SNI match for every `*.upayan.dev` host.
- **Change:**
  - **`k8s/platform/traefik/tlsstore.yaml`**: a `TLSStore` named `default` in `kube-system` pointing at `wildcard-upayan-dev-tls`. This is the supported way to state Traefik's default certificate, and it removes the dependency on two unrelated Secrets.
  - **`k8s/platform/traefik/secrets.sops.yaml`**: that Secret, SOPS-encrypted with the existing age recipients (the certificate+key are currently in the cluster but managed by nothing). Copied byte-for-byte from the live Secret — `tls.crt` and `tls.key` both verified `IDENTICAL` by sha256 — so nothing about what is served changes.
  - **Removed the `tls:` stanza** from the six Ingresses above, each with a comment recording why. All six keep `traefik.ingress.kubernetes.io/router.entrypoints: websecure` and Traefik runs with `--entryPoints.websecure.http.tls=true`, so TLS still terminates on the websecure entrypoint exactly as before.
- **Verification:** local `kustomize build --enable-alpha-plugins --enable-exec` renders the Secret, HelmChartConfig and TLSStore; `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` reports `secret/wildcard-upayan-dev-tls unchanged` (i.e. the git version equals the live one) and `tlsstore.traefik.io/default created`; `TLSStore` is a real `traefik.io/v1alpha1` resource. All six Ingresses re-parse, still carry their rules, and pass server dry-run; `kustomize build` is clean for their app dirs. Post-sync host checks (through Cloudflare and origin-direct) in the addendum.
- **Deliberately not done:** revoking the leaked 2025 Origin certificate (needs an owner Cloudflare token); removing `monitoring/grafana-tls` / `upayan-v5/upayan-v5-tls` and the stale `default/*-tls` Secrets — those are ⚠️ deletions and, now that the default is explicit, they can go in a follow-up pass once the TLSStore is proven in production.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores the chart-managed… *nothing* — restoring the Ingresses' `tls:` stanzas returns them to referencing Secrets that do not exist; deleting the TLSStore returns Traefik to the previous accidental default. Either is safe.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S11 — argocd-secret in SOPS)

### ~13:20 IST — S11: `argocd-secret` moved into SOPS (admin password unchanged)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), closing the last §S11 item that was not owner-credential-gated. Owner chose "keep your current password, manage the hash via SOPS".
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — new `k8s/platform/argocd/secret/` (ksops generator + SOPS-encrypted Secret), `k8s/platform/argocd/values.yaml`, `k8s/argocd/applications/platform/argocd.yaml`.
- **Change:** `argocd-secret` — holding `admin.password` (bcrypt), `admin.passwordMtime` and `server.secretkey` — was generated by the argo-cd chart and existed **only in-cluster**, i.e. recoverable solely from an etcd snapshot. It is now a SOPS-encrypted `Secret` in git (`k8s/platform/argocd/secret/secrets.sops.yaml`, encrypted with the existing `age-ops`/`age-cluster` recipients per `.sops.yaml`), applied through a `ksops` generator over a fourth source on the `argocd` Application, and the chart's own creation is disabled (`configs.secret.createSecret: false`) so there is exactly one owner.
- **Why this ordering matters:** the ksops source declares the *same object name*, so ArgoCD's desired set never lacks `argocd-secret` — with `prune: true` on that Application, disabling chart creation without simultaneously providing the object would have pruned the live Secret, and ArgoCD would have regenerated a *different* one (resetting the admin password and every session).
- **Verification — the point of the approach is that the password cannot change, and that was checked, not assumed:**
  - The plaintext was copied from the live Secret and the encrypted file round-trips (`sops -d`) to **identical fingerprints**: `admin.password` sha256 `c6a6be4c659226b5`, `admin.passwordMtime` `25086849518458f9`, `server.secretkey` `7a88ceb8ca5eff42`.
  - Local `kustomize build --enable-alpha-plugins --enable-exec` of the new directory renders the same Secret with the same fingerprints.
  - `helm template argo-cd 7.9.1` with the new values renders **no** `argocd-secret` at all, confirming the chart has handed over ownership.
  - After sync: the live Secret's three fingerprints are **still identical** (no password change, no session invalidation), the `argocd` Application is `Synced`/`Healthy` and now lists `Secret/argocd-secret` as `Synced` (so it is chart-independent), `argocd-server` is `1/1` with **0 restarts** (it never needed to restart — the value is unchanged), and `argocd.upayan.dev` → `200`.
  - `scripts/check-secrets.sh` passes (it validates sops+age metadata for this new file).
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores chart-managed creation; the live Secret is untouched either way. Note the encrypted file is useless without an age private key — `the offline age key` (owner's workstation) or the in-cluster `argocd/sops-age` Secret.

## 2026-09-28 (continued — de/sg consolidation verification)

### ~12:55 IST — consolidation cutover: verified, plus the one thing that nearly went wrong
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** changelog only (no further change).
- **Outcome:** the cutover completed. `kubectl -n argocd get appproject` → `apps`, `default`, `platform`, `vcap` (the `de`/`sg` projects are gone); `get applicationset` → `apps` only; **30/30 Applications `Healthy`**, the only `OutOfSync` being the accepted pre-existing `hetzner-csi`; zero non-`Running`/`Completed` pods.
- **Verification, by identity rather than by status:**
  - **All 12 PVCs have byte-identical UIDs to the pre-cutover snapshot** (`/tmp/before-pvc.json`) — i.e. every PVC was adopted, not recreated. This was the whole risk: a recreated `bitvault` PVC would have come up on a fresh empty volume, and a recreated `meghmitra` PVC could not have bound to its static PV at all (`claimRef` pins the old UID).
  - **All 13 public endpoints match the pre-change baseline exactly** — `200`: bandit, meghmitra, api-bitvault/healthz, ks, status-page, api-status-page, api.upayan.dev, api-vcap/ping, vcap, argocd; `307`: sayantani; `404` (serving): api-meghmitra, api-rankstack.
  - **meghmitra's data is provably intact**: its API returns real rows, and every one of the **51 tables' row counts matches the S8 post-migration baseline exactly** (`/tmp/s8-migration/meghmitra/row-counts-after.txt`, restored from the S8 dump directory). *Observation, not a discrepancy:* `GET /rules` now returns 113,244 bytes where it returned 115,350 before, while `/documents` (15,080) and `/geo/states` (5,142) are byte-identical and every table count is unchanged — so no rows moved, and the difference is response serialization (the new pod ran the `migrate` initContainer against the schema), not data.
  - 59 of 63 pods survived with identical UIDs.
- **What nearly went wrong, recorded because it is the useful part:** the plan was to strip `resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` from the 12 generated Applications so that deleting them would orphan their resources. That strip **did not hold** — by the time the controllers got to the Applications they had the finalizer back *and* a `deletionTimestamp`, i.e. ArgoCD had already begun the resource-deletion pass. `applicationsSync: create-only` stopped the sets from pruning their Applications, but did not stop the finalizer being (re-)added. I cleared the finalizer on all 12 immediately; the PVC check above was run in the same minute and came back 12/12 identical.
  - **Actual damage:** 7 pods were recreated — `meghmitra` web + api (their Deployments rolled, presumably because the successor Application synced git's `edge` tag over the digest the old one had pinned in-cluster), `rankstack`'s `mongo-rs-init` Job (re-run, idempotent by design since S13), `cas-api` and `upayan-v5-app` (KEDA scale-to-zero churn), and the two `watchdog` cron pods (normal cron churn). No PVC, PV, Secret or Service was lost. So: brief pod-level churn, no data loss.
  - **Lesson for the next rename:** the resources-finalizer is re-added by the controller, so "strip then delete later" is not a safe pattern here — either delete the Application in the same operation as the strip, or accept the cascade. Recording it so the next person does not trust `applicationsSync: create-only` for this.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** not needed; the consolidation is live and verified. `git revert 6d80263` would restore the old layout (resources are untouched by it).

## 2026-09-28 (continued — de/sg consolidation)

### ~12:40 IST — consolidate the `de`/`sg` split into a single `apps` grouping
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), on explicit owner instruction: "consolidate de and sg into [a] common [one] — those were meant to be for different nodes earlier but it's just one rn and will be so for the forseeable future."
- **Type:** git commits (this repo, `main`) + break-glass kubectl (12 finalizer strips). This supersedes the separate `de`/`sg` halves of the S11 image-pinning decision: the owner chose the ApplicationSet list-generator mechanism, and the merged set now carries the image annotations for every app it manages.
- **Change:**
  - **Folders flattened**: `k8s/apps/de/<app>/` and `k8s/apps/sg/<app>/` → `k8s/apps/<app>/` (16 apps moved). `k8s/apps/de/vcap/` → `k8s/apps/vcap/` (values overlay only).
  - **One AppProject** `apps` replaces `de` + `sg` (`k8s/argocd/projects/{de,sg}.yaml` → `apps.yaml`); destinations are the 16 namespaces, cluster kinds stay `Namespace` + `PersistentVolume`.
  - **One ApplicationSet** `apps` replaces `apps-de` + `apps-sg`. It uses a **list generator** — because each Application now carries its own `argocd-image-updater` `image-list`, and a git *directory* generator exposes only `path` (a folder name cannot be turned into an image name: `dj-html-nodejs-web` → `devjourney/html-nodejs/web`, `upayan-web` → `upayan.dev`).
  - **16 Applications renamed** to drop the now-meaningless region prefix (`de-bandit` → `bandit`, `sg-cheatsheet` → `cheatsheet`, `de-meghmitra` → `meghmitra`, …). The three `vcap-*` Applications keep their names; only their manifests moved to `k8s/argocd/applications/vcap/`.
  - **`status-page` became an explicit Application** (`k8s/argocd/applications/apps/status-page.yaml`): it ships two images, and a single-image template cannot express that without a `{{- if }}` line — which is *not valid YAML* inside an ApplicationSet manifest. Same reason `meghmitra`, `upayan-v5`, `mochi` (explicit for its own reasons) stay explicit.
  - **Deleted the 12 per-app `.argocd/image-updater.yaml` files.** `AGENTS.md` already documented them as non-functional ("ApplicationSet-templated Applications cannot receive annotations from it … inline documentation only"); with the image list now inline in `apps.yaml` they were duplicated, drift-prone data.
  - **Docs updated** across 27 files (`.claude/**`, `AGENTS.md`, `k8s/GUIDE.md`, `k8s/docs/**`, `docs/**`), including `app-onboarding` (adding an app is now: create the folder, add a list element, add the namespace to the project) and `06-image-automation.md` (keeps the explanation of why the merge generator was never implementable).
- **Reason:** `de` and `sg` existed because they were meant to be different nodes. There is one node, and mixing region prefixes into app names, project names, ApplicationSet names and folder paths added overhead with no benefit.
- **The dangerous part, and how it was neutralised.** Renaming an Application deletes it, and every Application here carries `resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` — ArgoCD would then delete every resource it tracks. That would have destroyed `bitvault`'s `local-path` PVC contents (S0 made the PVs `Retain`, so the new PVC would have come up empty on a fresh volume) and **unbound `meghmitra`'s static PV** (its `claimRef` pins a specific PVC UID, so a recreated PVC would not bind and Postgres would not start). `preserveResourcesOnDeletion` was the intended lever and is **not in this ArgoCD's CRD schema** — root's comparison failed with ".spec.syncPolicy.preserveResourcesOnDeletion: field not declared in schema" for both the ApplicationSet template and a plain Application (v2.14.11). Instead:
  1. `applicationsSync: create-only` on both old ApplicationSets (their own `spec.syncPolicy` — that field IS valid). This stops the controller updating its Applications, so it cannot re-add a stripped finalizer, and it stops the set pruning them once its folder glob stops matching.
  2. Dropped the `finalizers:` declaration from the two explicit manifests that had one (`meghmitra`, `rankstack`), so nothing re-adds it from git.
  3. Then, break-glass, stripped `resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` from the **12 live generated Applications** (`de-bandit`, `de-bitvault`, `de-cas-api`, `de-dj-html-nodejs-web`, `de-learning-docker`, `de-learning-qwik`, `de-learning-react`, `de-smart-home-system-api`, `sg-cheatsheet`, `sg-kodesphere`, `sg-status-page`, `sg-upayan-web`) via `kubectl -n argocd patch app … -p '{"metadata":{"finalizers":null}}'`. This is the log entry for those 12 writes.
  With the finalizers gone, the old Applications are deleted by the controllers (old sets removed, manifests re-homed) and their resources are **orphaned in place**, for the successor Applications to adopt untouched.
- **Pre-flight verification (before pushing):** the new ApplicationSet's 11 list elements were rendered with the template and diffed against each predecessor's live Application — namespace, `source.path`, project, `ignoreDifferences` and `syncPolicy` all match exactly (so adoption is a no-op, and no element is missing — a missing element would stop the set managing that app). All 12 manifest files pass `kubectl apply --dry-run=server`; `kustomize build` is clean for every app dir; `check-ports.py` and `check-secrets.sh` pass.
- **Post-cutover verification (post-cutover check in the addendum below):** the decisive test is identity, not status — the 12 PVCs' UIDs (captured before, in `/tmp/before-pvc.json`) and the PV names must be **unchanged** after the cutover, proving the resources were adopted rather than recreated.
- **Also recorded:** commit `911494d` added the rejected `preserveResourcesOnDeletion` fields; they were reverted in the following commit, and no live Application was ever patched with them (root failed comparison, so it never synced). Commit `eb73a12`'s message is missing one clause ("`applicationsSync: create-only`") because backticks inside a double-quoted `-m` were command-substituted by the shell — cosmetic only, the subject line and the change itself are intact; later commits in this series use a quoted heredoc.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert` restores the old folder layout, names, sets and project; the resources would be re-adopted the same way (they are untouched by this change). The 12 finalizer strips are not restored by a revert — ArgoCD re-adds a finalizer when the Application is next created.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S11 — control-plane netpols & vcap labels)

### ~12:05 IST — S11: adopt ArgoCD's untracked NetworkPolicies into git, drop the dead dex one; vcap namespace labels verified
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — `k8s/argocd/applications/platform/argocd.yaml`, new `k8s/platform/argocd/network-policies/manifests.yaml`, `docs/architecture.md`, changelog. Plus one break-glass `kubectl -n argocd delete netpol argocd-dex-server-network-policy`.
- **Found:** removing dex left one object behind — `argocd-dex-server-network-policy` — and looking at it revealed the `argocd` Application was reporting **26 orphaned resources**. 29 resources in the `argocd` namespace are not tracked by that Application; most are legitimately owned by other apps (`argocd-image-updater`) or are hand-made Secrets (`ghcr-creds`, `git-creds`, `sops-age`, `repo-vcap-*`, `argocd-server-tls`), but **seven `NetworkPolicy` objects were live, enforced, and managed by nothing**: `argocd-{application-controller,applicationset-controller,dex-server,notifications-controller,redis,repo-server,server}-network-policy`, created 2026-06-14 by an older argo-cd chart version that shipped NetworkPolicy templates under those names (chart 7.9.1's templates render under different names).
- **Why they mattered:** they are not decorative. k3s's kube-router controller enforces them, and `k8s/monitoring/prometheus-configmap.yaml` has a static `job_name: argocd` scraping `argocd-metrics:8082`, `argocd-server-metrics:8083` and `argocd-repo-server:8084` — the `from: namespaceSelector: {}` rules in the controller and repo-server policies are exactly what permit that cross-namespace scrape. `argocd-redis-network-policy` genuinely restricts Redis to its three legitimate clients.
- **Change:** copied the six live policies **verbatim** into `k8s/platform/argocd/network-policies/manifests.yaml` (same object names, so ArgoCD adopts the existing objects rather than creating duplicates) and applied them via a third `sources` entry on the `argocd` Application. Deleted `argocd-dex-server-network-policy` — dex is disabled now, so it selected zero pods.
- **Deliberately not used as the fix:** the chart's own `global.networkPolicy.create` (note: it is `global.networkPolicy.create`, not the top-level `networkPolicy.create` that `helm show values` appears to suggest — the first render attempt silently produced nothing). Rendering it produces *differently named* objects (`argocd-repo-server`, not `argocd-repo-server-network-policy`) and its repo-server policy allows only the named port `repo-server` (8081) from the other ArgoCD components — it would have **cut Prometheus off from 8084**. Verified by comparing the rendered specs against the live ones before choosing, rather than assuming the chart's current templates were equivalent.
- **vcap namespace labels verified:** after triggering sync operations (a refresh alone does not apply `managedNamespaceMetadata` — only a sync operation does), `vcap-dev` and `vcap-staging` both report `pod-security.kubernetes.io/warn=restricted`, and `vcap-backend-dev`, `vcap-backend-staging` and `vcap-frontend-staging` are all `Synced`/`Healthy`. `argocd.upayan.dev` → `200` and every `argocd` component `1/1` after the dex removal; `argocd` Application `Synced`/`Healthy`.
- **Docs:** `docs/architecture.md` gained a "Hardening posture (S11)" section covering PSA, the AppProject scopes and the remaining `default`-project gap.
- **Verification:** all six vendored policies `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` `OK` and diffed **IDENTICAL** to the live specs (spec-level, name by name) before committing, so adoption is a no-op behaviourally; post-sync `Synced`/`Healthy` sweep in the addendum.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` (the policies stay live but become untracked orphan objects again, as they were); the deleted dex policy is dead weight and needs no restore.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S11 — AppProject scopes)

### ~11:59 IST — S11: AppProject scopes made explicit — enumerated destinations, explicit cluster kinds
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), closing the last §S11 item that needed no owner input.
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — `k8s/argocd/projects/{de,sg,vcap,platform}.yaml`, `.claude/skills/app-onboarding/SKILL.md`, `.claude/skills/cluster-state/SKILL.md`.
- **Trigger:** plan §S11 — "`platform` whitelist explicit kinds; destinations enumerate namespaces".
- **Change:**
  - `de`, `sg`, `vcap`, `platform`: `destinations` now enumerate the exact namespaces their apps target instead of `namespace: "*"` — `de` 12, `sg` 4, `vcap` 2, `platform` 7 (incl. `namespace: ""` for `priority-classes`, which is cluster-scoped only).
  - `platform`: `clusterResourceWhitelist` changed from `group: "*" / kind: "*"` to the 11 cluster-scoped kinds its apps actually manage, taken from their **live** `status.resources` inventories: `CustomResourceDefinition`, `APIService`, `MutatingWebhookConfiguration`, `ValidatingWebhookConfiguration`, `ClusterRole`, `ClusterRoleBinding`, `StorageClass`, `CSIDriver`, `PriorityClass`, `Namespace`, `PersistentVolume`.
  - `namespaceResourceWhitelist` deliberately left at `group: "*" / kind: "*"` in all four — the namespaces are now the boundary, so restricting kinds *inside* an already-enumerated namespace buys little and would break every chart upgrade.
  - `CLAUDE.md`'s task-level consequence: adding an app now also means adding its namespace to `k8s/argocd/projects/<region>.yaml` — written into `.claude/skills/app-onboarding/SKILL.md` as a new required step 6 (and step 7's `git add` updated), plus a note in `.claude/skills/cluster-state/SKILL.md`.
- **Reason:** two real bugs in this session came from AppProject scope (`de` missing `PersistentVolume` broke every `de-meghmitra` sync; the permissive `*` was the thing hiding it). Enumerating makes the scope reviewable, and any mismatch now fails loudly (`namespace <x> is not permitted in project <y>` / `resource :<Kind> is not permitted in project <p>`) instead of silently mis-syncing.
- **Verification (done before committing):** all four files `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` `OK`; then two cross-checks against the live cluster — (1) every Application's `destination.namespace` is permitted by its project, (2) every cluster-scoped resource in every Application's `status.resources` is covered by its project's whitelist. Both report **no** violations. Post-sync `Synced`/`Healthy` sweep recorded in an addendum.
- **Deliberately not done — the built-in `default` project.** The `root` app (which manages the AppProjects and Applications themselves) runs in `default`, which is still `namespace: "*"`, `server: "*"`, cluster kinds `*`. Moving `root` into a restricted project is self-referential — it is the app that would have to apply the restriction — so it needs its own deliberate change, not a ride-along. Noted in `.claude/skills/cluster-state/SKILL.md` as the remaining AppProject gap.
- **Also not done (per the plan's own wording):** the ArgoCD v2.14 → v3.x upgrade is "its own tracked change" in §S11, and the admin-password-into-SOPS item is an authentication change that needs the owner's call on which password to set.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores `namespace: "*"` and the `*` cluster whitelist.

### ~11:58 IST — S11: ArgoCD — drop the unused dex, fix the placeholder `url`, and log that image-updater is dead cluster-wide
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), continuing the S11 hardening pass.
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — `k8s/platform/argocd/values.yaml`, `docs/upgrade-policy.md`; investigation of the image-updater failure (no live change: the credential it needs is owner-supplied).
- **Change:**
  - `dex.enabled: false` in the `argo-cd` chart values (and the now-dead `dex.resources` override removed).
  - `configs.cm.url: https://argocd.upayan.dev`.
  - `docs/upgrade-policy.md`: replaced the "S11, not yet implemented" framing with the verified current state.
- **Reason — dex:** the live `argocd-cm` has **no `dex.config` at all**, i.e. dex runs with zero connectors, so SSO cannot work by construction; login is the local `admin` account (`admin.enabled: true`). It was a whole Deployment + Service + ServiceAccount + NetworkPolicy listening on 5556 for nothing.
- **Reason — url:** `argocd-cm` had `url: https://argocd.example.com` — the argo-cd chart's placeholder default, since our values never overrode it. That feeds links in the UI and CLI and would be the OAuth redirect base for any future SSO.
- **Safety check before touching self-managed ArgoCD:** rendered `argo-cd 7.9.1` with the real values (`helm template`) — render clean, no `argocd-dex-server` Deployment/Service/SA/NetworkPolicy left, `url` present — and confirmed every remaining dex reference is `optional: true` (`argocd-repo-server`'s `argocd-dex-server-tls` volume, and `argocd-server`'s `ARGOCD_SERVER_DEX_SERVER*` env `configMapKeyRef`s), so no pod can fail to start on a missing key or secret.
- **Incident found (no fix applied — needs an owner-supplied credential):** `argocd-image-updater` is failing on **every** cycle for **every** app it is configured for — `Could not get tags from registry: … denied: denied` for all nine images (`vcap-*` ×3, `meghmitra` ×2, `rankstack`, `mochi`, `upayan-v5`), 9 errors per run, 0 updates. Root-caused to `configs.registries → credentials: pullsecret:argocd/ghcr-creds`: that PAT is dead — `GET https://api.github.com/user` with it returns `401 Bad credentials`, and `ghcr.io/token` returns `403`. Worse than no credential: because a rejected credential is sent on every request, even **publicly** pullable packages fail (verified by hand: an anonymous manifest fetch for `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/mochi:latest` returns `200`, `cas-api`/`cheatsheet` likewise). So the "image-updater keeps digests for app images" half of §S11 is currently 0% effective. **Fix requires a new token from the owner** (classic PAT with `read:packages`, or fine-grained / per-repo deploy keys per the S5 plan) written into `argocd/ghcr-creds`; `argocd/image-updater` needs no config change once that secret holds a working value. `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/bitvault/bitvaultd` is additionally private (anonymous `403`), so it stays digest-pinned in git either way.
- **§S11 AppProject item resolved by evidence (no change needed):** "`vcap` loses ClusterRole/ClusterRoleBinding whitelist unless verified needed". **Verified needed** — the vcap chart declares them (`k8s/chart/templates/rbac.yaml`), and the live `ClusterRoleBinding`s bind ServiceAccounts `vcap-dev/vcap-backend-dev`, `vcap-staging/vcap-backend-staging`, `vcap-staging/vcap-staging-backend-sa`, `vcap-dev/vcap-dev-backend-sa`. Dropping the whitelist would break those apps' syncs; note that `clusterResourceWhitelist` is kind-level only, so the plan's "then restrict names" alternative is not expressible in an AppProject.
- **Verification:** `helm template` render of the full chart with the new values (clean, no dex objects, `url` set, all dex refs `optional: true`); live post-sync check recorded in an addendum if anything differs. `docs/upgrade-policy.md` chart table verified against the live Application specs (`argo-cd 7.9.1`, `argocd-image-updater 0.14.0`, `cert-manager v1.21.2`, `keda 2.21.0`, `keda-add-ons-http 0.16.0`).
- **Cost impact:** small positive — dex's requests (25m CPU / 32Mi, limits 150m/96Mi) stop being scheduled on a single-node cluster.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores dex and the placeholder URL.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S11c)

### ~11:55 IST — S11: Pod Security Admission — `enforce=baseline` on the repo-owned app namespaces
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), continuing the S11 hardening pass after the NetworkPolicy work.
- **Timestamp note:** entries in this file written earlier today are labelled ~14:00–15:45 IST while their git commits are timestamped 06:52 IST, i.e. ~+8h apart; the "addendum" entry below is labelled ~12:15 but was committed at 11:49. From this entry on, timestamps are the real system/commit time (IST), as `.claude/skills/changelog/SKILL.md` specifies. Earlier labels are left alone (append-only).
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — 15 `namespace.yaml` files, `k8s/monitoring/namespace.yaml`, 3 `k8s/argocd/applications/de/vcap-*.yaml`; plus PR `upayanmazumder/rankstack#94` (chart-owned namespace).
- **Trigger:** plan §S11 — "PSA: label app namespaces `pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce=baseline`, `warn=restricted`; `monitoring`, `kube-system` privileged. Roll out warn-only first, then enforce." The warn-only half landed in an earlier S11 pass; this is the enforce half.
- **Pre-flight verification (done before flipping anything):** evaluated every workload template in the app namespaces (46 Deployment/StatefulSet/DaemonSet/Job/CronJob templates) **and** the 33 live pods against the full Baseline control list from the upstream "Pod Security Standards" page — host namespaces, privileged, capabilities added beyond the Baseline set (`AUDIT_WRITE`, `CHOWN`, `DAC_OVERRIDE`, `FOWNER`, `FSETID`, `KILL`, `MKNOD`, `NET_BIND_SERVICE`, `SETFCAP`, `SETGID`, `SETPCAP`, `SETUID`, `SYS_CHROOT`), hostPath volumes, **hostPort**, the v1.34 **host-probe/lifecycle-hook `host`** control (this cluster is `v1.34.4+k3s1`, so it applies), AppArmor, SELinux, `/proc` mount type, seccomp `Unconfined`, and unsafe sysctls. **Zero violations.** Templates were checked rather than just live pods so that KEDA-scaled-to-zero workloads and not-currently-running Jobs were covered too.
- **Change:**
  - `enforce: baseline` added to the 15 repo-owned app namespaces (`bandit`, `bitvault`, `cas-api`, `cheatsheet`, `dj-html-nodejs-web`, `kodesphere`, `learning-docker`, `learning-qwik`, `learning-react`, `meghmitra`, `mochi`, `smart-home-system-api`, `status-page`, `upayan-v5`, `upayan-web`); each keeps `warn: restricted`, with the reason recorded inline.
  - `k8s/monitoring/namespace.yaml`: added explicit `enforce: privileged` + `warn: privileged`. Prometheus/Loki/Grafana use hostPath for their TSDBs and Prometheus uses host networking — all Baseline violations, so this is documentation of intent, not a behaviour change (an unlabelled namespace is equally unrestricted).
  - `vcap-dev`/`vcap-staging` (`vcap-backend-dev`, `vcap-backend-staging`, `vcap-frontend-staging`): `managedNamespaceMetadata.labels` with **`warn: restricted` only**.
  - `rankstack`: labels belong in its chart, which owns that Namespace — PR `upayanmazumder/rankstack#94` adds `enforce: baseline` + `warn: restricted` to `k8s/chart/templates/namespace.yaml`. **Not live until the owner merges** (that repo's `main` is branch-protected).
- **Reason:** the app namespaces were warn-only, so nothing actually prevented a privileged/hostPath/host-network pod from being scheduled into them; now Baseline is enforced where it is provably safe.
- **Deliberate decisions (each with its evidence):**
  - **No `enforce` on the vcap namespaces, only `warn`.** §S6 plans `postgres.hostPort` so that `pg_hba` sees the real client IP — and `hostPort` is a Baseline violation ("HostPorts should be disallowed entirely … Allowed Values: Undefined/nil, 0"), which the built-in PSA controller cannot express as a known-list exception. Enforcing Baseline there would block the S6 change rather than protect it. `warn` gives the visibility without the deadlock.
  - **vcap namespaces get no `Namespace` object in this repo.** They are created by ArgoCD's `CreateNamespace=true` and owned by no manifest; declaring the Namespace here would make the app its owner, which — with `resources-finalizer` + `prune: true` on those Applications — hands an app the ability to delete the namespace (and with it `vcap-staging`'s PVCs, i.e. the class-A student data, whose static PVs would survive only because they are `Retain`). `managedNamespaceMetadata` sets the labels without that ownership.
  - **`kube-system` left unlabelled.** It is k3s-owned, not tracked by any manifest here, and `privileged` is exactly what an unlabelled namespace already behaves as — so labelling it would mean a break-glass, untracked mutation whose only effect is cosmetic. Recorded instead of done.
- **Verification:** `kubectl kustomize` clean for every app dir plus `k8s/monitoring`; every changed file `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` against the live cluster (`OK` / `configured (server dry run)`); `scripts/check-ports.py` passes; rankstack change `helm lint`-clean and dry-run-validated. The live post-sync state (labels actually applied) and the confirmation that no running pod was disturbed are recorded in the addendum appended immediately after this entry.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` — removes the `enforce` labels (namespaces revert to warn-only, which is the pre-change state). PSA labels only affect admission of *new* pods, so no running workload is disturbed either way. rankstack: close PR #94.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S11)

### ~11:50 IST — S11: NetworkPolicies for every app namespace — per-namespace default-deny, allow Traefik + same namespace, DB pods same-namespace only
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), owner said "yes" to S11 NetworkPolicies after every app's real live traffic path was traced first.
- **Type:** git commits — this repo `198c4b7` (KEDA-fronted apps) and `39f6d46` (all remaining namespaces); `vitscopesoftwareteam/VCAP-STAGING-BACKEND` commits `293fbd6` (vcap chart) and `fbfdafd` (the same-day fix below). Also break-glass kubectl: short-lived probe pods (`default/netpol-probe`, `vcap-dev/netpol-probe-allowed`, `vcap-staging/netpol-probe-frontend`) created and deleted purely to prove enforcement — nothing else was mutated by hand; every policy went in through ArgoCD.
- **Trigger:** architecture review §S11: "per app namespace default-deny ingress + allow from `kube-system` (Traefik) and same namespace; DB pods only from same namespace, except vcap Postgres".
- **Change:** wrote NetworkPolicies covering 17 files / 34 policies across every app namespace. Two shapes, per the plan:
  - **KEDA-HTTP-fronted** (`cas-api`, `dj-html-nodejs-web`, `learning-docker`, `learning-qwik`, `smart-home-system-api`, `upayan-v5` api+app): public traffic arrives via the interceptor proxy in the `keda` namespace (`k8s/platform/keda-add-ons-http-routes/`), never from Traefik — so these allow `namespaceSelector: keda` + `podSelector: app.kubernetes.io/component=interceptor` only.
  - **Direct-Traefik + the rest** (`bandit`, `learning-react`, `cheatsheet`, `kodesphere`, `status-page`, `upayan-web`, `meghmitra` web/api/postgres, `bitvault` bitvaultd/minio/postgres, `rankstack` api/mongo/redis, vcap frontend web): a namespace-wide `podSelector: {}` default-deny, plus per-workload allow rules for Traefik (`kube-system`, `app.kubernetes.io/name=traefik`) and same-namespace pods.
  - All new intra-namespace "from" rules use an **empty `podSelector` (all pods in the namespace)**, not name/instance label matching. The namespace is the trust boundary here, and label matching within it already proved fragile twice in one session (see the fix below).
  - vcap chart (`vitscopesoftwareteam/VCAP-STAGING-BACKEND`): added `api.networkpolicy.yaml`, `redis.networkpolicy.yaml`, `minio.networkpolicy.yaml` — only `auth` had a NetworkPolicy before (that pre-existing one is left untouched).
  - `rankstack` and the vcap frontend chart have no NetworkPolicy templates of their own, so theirs live in this repo (`k8s/apps/de/rankstack/manifests/`, `k8s/apps/de/vcap/frontend-manifests/`) and are applied via a **third source** added to those two explicit Applications.
- **Reason:** the cluster had essentially no pod-level network policy — any compromised or misbehaving workload could reach every other pod's ports cluster-wide. This makes each app namespace deny-by-default on ingress, with only the two legitimate external paths (Traefik, or the KEDA interceptor) and same-namespace traffic allowed through.
- **Verification:**
  - **Enforcement is real, and proven negatively.** A probe pod in `default` got `nc` refused against `vcap-backend-dev-redis:6379` (policy-4h57m old) while a probe pod *inside* `vcap-dev` carrying the release labels got `open`; `vcap-backend-dev-postgres:5432` (unpoliced, intentionally) stayed `open` from `default`. So the block is policy-specific, not a broken Service or missing tool.
  - **The Traefik path works.** `api-vcap.upayan.dev/ping` → `200` and `vcap.upayan.dev/` → `200` *after* the vcap policies were live; Traefik is confirmed **not** `hostNetwork` (`spec.hostNetwork` unset on `traefik-659fc7c6fb-hr967`), which is what makes the `podSelector` rule match at all.
  - **The KEDA path works.** `api-cas.upayan.dev` → `200`, `bots.upayan.dev` → `200` after their policies applied.
  - Every file: `helm lint` (vcap chart) / `kubectl kustomize` for all app dirs / YAML+shape check / `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` against the live API — all clean. `scripts/check-ports.py` and `scripts/check-secrets.sh` pass.
  - Pre-change HTTP baseline captured for all 16 affected hosts, to diff against after sync.
- **Regression I introduced and fixed within the hour (`fbfdafd`):** my first vcap `api` policy matched same-release pods by `app.kubernetes.io/name=vcap-backend` + `instance=vcap-backend-staging`. That is exactly how `values/frontend-staging.yaml` calls it — `apiSvcHost: vcap-backend-staging-api`, i.e. the web UI reaches the API by **in-cluster Service name** — and the frontend chart's pods are labelled `name=vcap-frontend` / `instance=vcap-frontend-staging`, so the web UI's server-side API calls would have been dropped. Reproduced with a probe pod carrying the frontend's labels (`nc vcap-backend-staging-api:8000` → refused) vs the release labels (→ open), fixed, re-verified: with the fix live the frontend-labelled probe reaches `api:8000`, `auth:8080`, `redis:6379` and `minio:9000` all `open`, and the frontend pod's real labels include `part-of=vcap` so the pre-existing `auth` policy still admits it. This is the second time this chart's labels misled a selector (the `part-of: vcap` inconsistency found while writing the policies is the first), which is why every new intra-namespace rule is now an empty `podSelector`.
- **Real bug found and fixed in the same pass (`k8s/argocd/projects/de.yaml`):** `de-meghmitra` would not apply the new policies at all, and the cause was not the policies. Its sync operation was stuck `Running` / "one or more synchronization tasks are not valid", with `PersistentVolume/meghmitra-postgres-pvc-vps :: resource :PersistentVolume is not permitted in project de` — the `de` AppProject's `clusterResourceWhitelist` listed only `Namespace`, so the static PV that S8 deliberately put **in git** (plan §S8 step 4: "in git for repo-owned apps") made **every** sync of that app fail. It was previously recorded as a harmless `OutOfSync` tracking quirk; it was in fact a permanently dead GitOps path for that app, and the live state only looked right because the S8 migration had been applied out-of-band. Added `PersistentVolume` to the project's whitelist. (`platform` already allows `*` and covers every other cluster-scoped object in the repo — monitoring PVs, `keel` RBAC, `PriorityClass`, the Traefik `HelmChartConfig`.)
- **Pre-existing findings surfaced while working (none caused by, or fixed by, this change):**
  - **`de-smart-home-system-api` is broken, latently.** It looked `Healthy` only because KEDA keeps it scaled to 0; the first request my traffic check sent scaled it up and the pod stuck in `ContainerCreating` — `FailedAttachVolume ... volume "pvc-6d634268-…" : volume not found`. The Hetzner API confirms volume `106353532` no longer exists (only `vps-data`, plus the two retained vcap volumes, remain). This is **known and owner-confirmed** (`docs/inventory.md`, `docs/storage.md`, review §"smart-home-system-api lost volume 106353532 | Deliberate; data not needed") and its PV/PVC cleanup is scheduled by the plan's own **S8 step 5**, gated on ≥14 days after migration and ⚠️-marked — so it is deliberately left alone here. `docs/storage.md`'s "clean up (S10)" label is corrected to name S8 step 5.
  - **Two-level hostnames fail at the Cloudflare edge** — `qwik.dj.upayan.dev` and `react.dj.upayan.dev` / `api.react.dj.upayan.dev` return a TLS handshake failure (`curl -sv` → `error:0A000410:SSL routines::ssl/tls alert handshake failure`), the same limitation already logged for `api.ks.upayan.dev`: Cloudflare's Universal SSL covers `upayan.dev` + one wildcard level, so `*.dj.upayan.dev` and `*.ks.upayan.dev` have no SAN. Recorded in `docs/certificates.md`; the fix is S7's Origin-CA wildcard.
  - **`api-bandit.upayan.dev` → `502`** — re-confirmed as the already-documented `changelog/2026-08.md` finding, now with the exact error: the pod's `pm2`-managed backend never binds `:5000` because `MongooseServerSelectionError: Could not connect to any servers in your MongoDB Atlas cluster` (Atlas allowlist). Only `:3000` (frontend) listens. MongoDB Atlas is explicitly out of scope in `docs/storage.md`; the frontend host stays `200`.
- **Deliberately not done:**
  - **vcap Postgres gets no NetworkPolicy.** It is still reachable from the internet via the unmanaged NodePort Services (`vcap-backend-{dev,staging}-postgres-external`, 30533/30532) plus `IngressRouteTCP` objects, per the owner's S6 decision. That traffic does not originate from Traefik, so the §S11 allow rule would silently break direct DB access — the plan itself pairs the Postgres policy with S6's `hostPort`/TLS/tailnet redesign. It stays unrestricted until S6.
  - **No namespace-wide default-deny in `vcap-dev` / `vcap-staging`** for the same reason (the chart's `worker`/`postgres`/Job pods have no allow rules, and a blanket deny written from this repo would cut the path above).
  - **`mochi`** has no Ingress at all (Discord gateway bot, outbound only — `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL`), so it gets default-deny with **zero** allow rules.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert 39f6d46 198c4b7`; in the vcap repo `git revert fbfdafd 293fbd6` — NetworkPolicies are additive objects, so reverting removes them and restores the previous (unrestricted ingress) posture. ArgoCD prunes them on the next sync.

### ~15:45 IST — S3: k3s config cutover applied — systemd drop-in replaced with config.yaml, kubelet image-GC thresholds now live
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), owner said "2nd" for the maintenance-window question (downtime acceptable, no time-of-day constraint) — this was the last deferred item from the earlier S3 Ansible pass.
- **Type:** break-glass kubectl/ssh (etcd snapshot + k3s restart) — permitted under `.claude/CLAUDE.md` rule 2 analogues (host-level maintenance, not a `k8s/` git change since k3s's own config isn't cluster state).
- **Change:** `ssh vps`: `k3s etcd-snapshot save --name pre-k3s-config-cutover` (27 MB, copied off-VM, sha256 recorded) → moved `/etc/rancher/k3s/config.yaml.pending` → `/etc/rancher/k3s/config.yaml` → removed `/etc/systemd/system/k3s.service.d/tailscale.conf` (the flags it set — `--tls-san vps.upayan.dev --node-ip=138.201.157.147 --node-external-ip=100.96.250.81 --flannel-iface=tailscale0` — are now in the config file instead) → `systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl restart k3s`. The config file also adds `kubelet-arg: image-gc-high-threshold=75/low=60` (previously unset, i.e. kubelet defaults), matching the S8 design's disk-hygiene target.
- **Reason:** moves k3s's real running config into a git-reviewable file (`ansible/roles/k3s/templates/config.yaml.j2`) instead of a systemd drop-in that could drift unnoticed; adds the kubelet image-GC threshold safety net.
- **Verification:** node stayed `Ready` throughout (didn't even flap `NotReady` during the ~10s restart); all 28 ArgoCD Applications' Sync/Health status **byte-identical** before/after (`diff` of a pre/post `kubectl get applications` snapshot); zero CrashLoopBackOff/ImagePullBackOff anywhere; `ps aux` confirms `k3s server` running with no `--tls-san`/etc. flags (all moved into the config file, `cat /etc/rancher/k3s/config.yaml` shows them); `kubectl get --raw /api/v1/nodes/vps/proxy/configz` confirms `imageGCHighThresholdPercent: 75` / `imageGCLowThresholdPercent: 60` actually took effect; `tailscale0` interface/IP (`100.96.250.81`) unchanged; `https://argocd.upayan.dev/` → `200`.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** restore `/etc/systemd/system/k3s.service.d/tailscale.conf`, remove `/etc/rancher/k3s/config.yaml`, `systemctl daemon-reload && systemctl restart k3s`; if that doesn't recover, restore the etcd snapshot (`/root/pre-k3s-config-cutover/` on the node, mirrored to the workstation) per `docs/runbooks/recover-k3s.md`.

- **Actor:** agent (omp session), owner said "2nd" (onboard `default/kodesphere` as canonical) for the S10 conflict flagged earlier this session (the architecture review's "retire the sg-kodesphere duplicate" framing had it backwards — `default/kodesphere` was the only copy actually serving traffic).
- **Type:** git commit (`k8s/apps/sg/kodesphere/`) + break-glass kubectl (export-then-delete the old unmanaged `default/kodesphere` objects, same pattern as the mochi cutover).
- **Change:** added `services.yaml` (`kodesphere:3000`, `kodesphere-api:5000`) and `ingress.yaml` (`ks.upayan.dev`, `api.ks.upayan.dev`) to the existing `sg-kodesphere` app — neither existed before, under the now-outdated "internal tool, no Service/Ingress by design" assumption. Removed `cronscaledobject.yaml` (KEDA cron scale-to-zero, 8am–11pm IST) since this is now an always-on public service. `kodesphere-env.yaml`/`github-private-key.yaml` secrets needed **no changes** — diffed field-by-field against the live `default/kodesphere` Secrets first (without printing any values) and found them already identical except one dead/overridden key.
- **Cutover sequence**: hard-refreshed `sg-kodesphere` → new pod came up healthy → verified internally (`kubectl exec` + `wget` to both ports, real HTML/Express responses) before touching public traffic → exported `default/kodesphere`'s Deployment/Services/Ingress to `archived/kodesphere-default-dup/live-export-2026-09.yaml` → deleted them → confirmed `https://ks.upayan.dev/` → `200` through the new `sg-kodesphere` path.

Confirmed the backend itself is fine (`kubectl exec` direct requests to `:5000` return correct Express responses); this would have equally broken the old `default/kodesphere` setup since it's the identical hostname/zone-cert limitation — not a regression. **Not fixed** — renaming the hostname needs a matching GitHub OAuth App callback-URL update, an external, owner-facing change out of scope for this cutover. Documented in `archived/kodesphere-default-dup/README.md`.
- **Verification:** `sg-kodesphere` Application `Synced`/`Healthy`; public `https://ks.upayan.dev/` → `200`; `python3 scripts/check-ports.py` and `kubectl kustomize k8s/apps/sg/kodesphere` both pass.
- **Reason:** resolves the S10 finding correctly — the previously-unmanaged, actually-live app is now under GitOps; the genuinely-idle duplicate config assumption is corrected.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` restores the pre-cutover `sg-kodesphere` manifests (cron scaler, no Service/Ingress); `default/kodesphere` would need to be recreated from `archived/kodesphere-default-dup/live-export-2026-09.yaml` plus its Secrets (not exported, per this repo's no-plaintext-secret-export convention for archived objects — though as established elsewhere this repo does commit real secret values for actively-managed apps, this is specifically about the *archived, retired* copy).

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S13b)

### ~15:00 IST — S13: fixed vcap MinIO bucket-init (mc unpullable, no cache) + a real ArgoCD sync-retry-storm bug it exposed
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), the last S13 finding: "vcap MinIO (both envs) | server runs from cached image only; minio-init hook's mc image unpullable | Plan B: ... vcap-repo PR replacing the mc hook with a boto3 bucket-create".
- **Type:** git commit in `vitscopesoftwareteam/VCAP-STAGING-BACKEND` (chart fix) + git commit in this repo (ArgoCD Application `ignoreDifferences`, unrelated bug found while verifying the chart fix).
- **Root cause confirmed live**: `quay.io/minio/mc:RELEASE.2025-04-16T18-13-26Z` (and every other tag/registry combination tried: `docker.io/minio/mc:latest`, `bitnami/minio-client:latest`) returns 401/403/not-found — unlike the MinIO **server** image, no working copy of `mc` was ever cached on the node before the upstream change, so there was no "pin to cached digest" fallback available here.
- **Chart fix**: `templates/minio.yaml`'s `minio-init` PostSync hook Job now uses `public.ecr.aws/aws-cli/aws-cli` (pinned by digest, confirmed pullable) running `aws --endpoint-url http://<release>-minio:9000 s3api create-bucket` against MinIO's S3-compatible API instead of `mc mb` — MinIO doesn't care which S3-compatible client talks to it. `values.storage.s3.minio.mcImage` renamed to `bucketInitImage`. Verified locally with `helm lint`/`helm template`, then **smoke-tested live**: applied a throwaway pod in `vcap-dev` with the exact same image/script/env against the real MinIO instance — `aws s3 ls` listed the real `vcap-artifacts`/`vcap-workspace` buckets, `aws s3api create-bucket` succeeded and is idempotent; test bucket cleaned up before pushing.
- **Real bug found while verifying the fix**: hard-refreshing `vcap-backend-dev`/`vcap-backend-staging` kept recreating and immediately killing the `minio-init` Job before it could finish (`hook-delete-policy: before-hook-creation` tears it down on every sync attempt) — traced to the S8 migration's static-PV PVCs: `volumeName`/`storageClassName` are immutable once a PVC is Bound, so ArgoCD could never resolve the diff it kept detecting between the chart's rendered PVC (which can't know the out-of-band static PV's name) and the live object, causing **every** reconcile loop to re-trigger a full sync including PostSync hooks — a real, actively-harmful bug (perpetual hook churn), not just cosmetic `OutOfSync` noise as first assumed in the S8 entries above. Fixed with `ignoreDifferences` on the affected PVC's two fields in `k8s/argocd/applications/de/{vcap-backend-dev,vcap-backend-staging,meghmitra}.yaml` (meghmitra also needed one for its PV's auto-added `claimRef`, since unlike vcap its static PV is a git-tracked resource of that Application).
- **Self-inflicted, transient side effect while debugging**: the many rapid manual hard-refreshes/sync-patches used to diagnose the above briefly overloaded the ArgoCD repo-server (concurrent `helm template`/`pull` calls taking 8–23s each vs. its 1s liveness-probe timeout) — `argocd-repo-server` pod restarted 4 times over ~30 minutes (`root`/`apps-sg` ApplicationSet briefly showed `Degraded`, "connection refused" to the repo-server mid-restart). Self-resolved within ~2 minutes of stopping manual interference; no config change needed, no data at risk (repo-server is stateless). Noting here since it looked alarming in the live cluster status during the session.
- **Verification**: `vcap-backend-dev`'s `minio-init` Job ran to completion end-to-end through the real GitOps path (`kubectl get events` shows `Completed`, not just my manual smoke test) after the `ignoreDifferences` fix landed; `vcap-backend-staging` confirmed on the identical fixed chart+values revision with its buckets intact. Both `vcap-backend-dev`/`vcap-backend-staging` now `Synced`/`Healthy` (no more perpetual `OutOfSync`). ### ~14:20 IST — S13: fixed bitvault crash-loop — minio+bitvaultd pinned to already-cached digests, verified live
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), S13 item from `docs/plans/2026-09-27-architecture-review.md` ("`quay.io/minio/minio:latest` → 401 ... `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/bitvault/bitvaultd:edge` → 403").
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — ArgoCD auto-syncs.
- **Root cause confirmed live**: both images were unpullable from their upstream registries (quay.io no longer serves public `:latest` pulls for MinIO; the private `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/bitvault/bitvaultd` package's pull token is revoked/expired, `403`) — but `crictl images` on the node showed a **working cached copy of each** from before the registries changed (`quay.io/minio/minio@sha256:14cea493d9...`, `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/bitvault/bitvaultd@sha256:79cd714b7d...`). The bitvaultd main container also had `imagePullPolicy: Always` explicitly set, which ignores the local cache and hits the network on every restart regardless — the actual reason it kept crash-looping instead of just running from cache.
- **Change:** `k8s/apps/de/bitvault/minio.yaml` — pinned `image:` to the cached digest, added `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent`. `k8s/apps/de/bitvault/kustomization.yaml` — replaced `newTag: edge` with `digest: sha256:79cd714b7d...` for `bitvaultd` (applies to both the `migrate` initContainer and the main container). `k8s/apps/de/bitvault/bitvaultd.yaml` — added explicit `imagePullPolicy: IfNotPresent` to the `migrate` initContainer, changed the main container's from `Always` to `IfNotPresent`.
- **Verification:** hard-refreshed `de-bitvault` — `bitvault-minio` pod went straight to `1/1 Running` (0 restarts); `bitvaultd` briefly hit one transient `CrashLoopBackOff` from a real but unrelated startup race (`dial tcp ... connect: connection refused` to `bitvault-minio:9000` — MinIO's own pod was still starting at that exact moment since both pods restarted together from the same sync), self-resolved on the next retry once MinIO was ready — now `1/1 Running`, log shows `"bitvaultd lite started"`. Public endpoint `https://api-bitvault.upayan.dev/healthz` → `200`. `de-bitvault` Application: `Progressing` → `Synced`/`Healthy`.
- **Reason:** closes one of the four S13 "broken apps" findings — restores a fully working app using zero new credentials, by using what was already sitting on the node.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` — reverts to the previous (broken, crash-looping) state; not planned.
- **Not done — the underlying credential problem**: `argocd-image-updater` still can't reach `ghcr.io` for this private package (403), so it can't automatically track new `bitvaultd` builds until a working pull/read credential exists — that's the S5 rotation's "replace the reused `gho_` token with per-repo deploy keys" item, not fixed here. Until then, any *new* `bitvaultd` release requires manually updating the pinned digest in `kustomization.yaml`.

## 2026-09-28 (continued, S8)

### ~13:00 IST — S8: vcap-dev data migrated from Hetzner CSI volume to vps-data (live, verified)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), owner said "do" for the full S8 data migration — starting with vcap-dev (class C) as the lowest-risk proof of the exact mechanical pattern before touching vcap-staging (class A, real student PII) or meghmitra (class A).
- **Type:** real production data migration. Fresh `pg_dump` backup + row-count baseline before touching anything; break-glass kubectl (`scale --replicas=0`, cronjob suspend, `exec` for a one-off in-cluster copy pod, `delete pvc`) — all reversible, all logged here since kubectl bypasses git; git commit (chart fix in `vitscopesoftwareteam/VCAP-STAGING-BACKEND`, values override in this repo).
- **Root cause of extra complexity discovered mid-migration**: the vcap Helm chart's PVC template only rendered `storageClassName` when the value was **truthy** — an empty string (needed to force static PV binding instead of dynamic Hetzner-CSI provisioning) is falsy in Go templates, so there was no way to actually request static binding at all. Fixed upstream: `vitscopesoftwareteam/VCAP-STAGING-BACKEND` commit `b5938ee` (pushed directly, no branch protection on that repo) changes the guard to `hasKey .Values.storage "storageClassName"` in `pvc.yaml`/`postgres.yaml`/`minio.yaml` — preserves identical behavior for every other deployment of this chart that doesn't set the key, verified via `helm template` both ways before pushing.
- **Change:**
  1. Fresh `pg_dump` of vcap-dev's `vcap` database + a full row-count snapshot of all 57 tables (baseline for post-migration verification) — saved to a local workstation temp dir, not committed (real, if low-stakes, application data).
  2. Suspended `vcap-backend-dev-orphan-sweep`/`watchdog` CronJobs, scaled `api`/`minio`/`postgres` Deployments to 0 (stops all writes to the shared PVC).
  3. Ran a one-off in-cluster Alpine pod mounting both the old PVC and the new `hostPath /srv/data/vcap-dev` simultaneously, `rsync -a` copied all 1,649 files / ~72 MB (matches the pre-migration `df` reading exactly) — avoids needing raw node-device access, since the CSI driver detaches the volume from the node as soon as no pod mounts it (confirmed: `lsblk` on the node showed the device gone within seconds of scaling to 0).
  4. Deleted the old PVC `vcap-backend-dev-data` (Retain policy from S0 confirmed via `kubectl get pv` before deleting — PV went `Bound → Released`, the underlying Hetzner CSI volume `106574316` is untouched and still attached, kept for 14 days per the plan before any real deletion).
  5. Created a static `PersistentVolume` (`vcap-backend-dev-data-vps`, hostPath `/srv/data/vcap-dev`, `Retain`, node-affinity pinned to `vps`) + a new PVC of the same name with `storageClassName: ""` and an explicit `volumeName` pointing at the static PV — the explicit `volumeName` turned out to be load-bearing: ArgoCD's own chart-rendered PVC (which has no way to know the specific static PV's name) kept getting re-applied without it and never converged to `Bound` within ArgoCD's sync loop; applying the PVC directly with `volumeName` set (a field ArgoCD's server-side-apply doesn't own and therefore never touches) is what let it bind and stay bound.
  6. `k8s/apps/de/vcap/values/backend-dev.yaml`: added `storage.storageClassName: ""`.
  7. Scaled `postgres`/`minio`/`api` back to 1, re-enabled the two CronJobs.
- **Verification:** row counts for all 57 tables identical before/after (`diff` clean); MinIO's `vcap-artifacts`/`vcap-workspace` buckets present with original file timestamps (Aug 9/10, unchanged); `vcap-backend-dev-api` pod reached `1/1 Running` with a clean `Application startup complete` log and no errors; `vcap-backend-dev` Application `Healthy` (the one `OutOfSync` resource left is the PVC itself — its `volumeName`/`storageClassName` fields are immutable and ArgoCD's rendered manifest doesn't specify them the same way, so this will show `OutOfSync` indefinitely, same accepted pattern as the pre-existing `hetzner-csi` app — harmless, does not block sync/health).
- **Reason:** S8 — moves class-C-but-still-real vcap-dev data onto the durable, Retain-by-default `vps-data` volume and proves the exact migration mechanism (in-cluster copy pod + static PV/PVC + chart fix) before repeating it for class-A data.
- **Cost impact:** none new (vps-data volume already billed; Hetzner CSI volume `106574316` stays attached for the 14-day retention window, same cost as before).
- **Rollback:** the old CSI PV (`Released`, Retain) still has the original data untouched — re-pointing the PVC's `claimRef`/creating a PVC bound to it and scaling back up would restore the pre-migration state; the fresh `pg_dump` is an independent restore path if needed.
- **Not done yet**: vcap-staging (class A, real student PII — proceeding next with the same now-proven pattern, extra care) and meghmitra-postgres (class A, currently on root-disk `local-path`, not CSI) — the two higher-stakes migrations this pattern was proven for.

## 2026-09-28 (continued)

### ~11:10 IST — S13 rankstack: fixed Mongo replica-set self-heal loop, live crash stopped (fix in a PR, not yet merged)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), completing the S13 rankstack investigation flagged as "not viable to drop --replSet" in the previous session (app uses MongoDB transactions extensively — `start_session()`/`start_transaction()` for submissions and team/problem updates, confirmed via grep of `~/dev/upayanmazumder/rankstack/api/services.py`).
- **Type:** break-glass kubectl (`exec` into the live `rankstack-mongo` pod to run `mongosh`, permitted under rule 2 as a targeted fix for a pod stuck in a broken, non-serving state — not a config change to a manifest) + a chart fix pushed to a branch/PR in the separate `upayanmazumder/rankstack` repo (this repo's `de-rankstack` `Application` tracks that repo's `main`, currently protected — cannot push directly, needs owner merge).
- **Root cause, confirmed live:** `rankstack-mongo`'s single-member `rs0` was in a state where `rs.status()` **threw** ("invalid replica set config or not a member") even though `rs.conf()` returned a config that exactly matched the running mongod (`_id: 0, host: "rankstack-mongo:27017"`). The existing `mongo-rs-init-job.yaml` script only checked whether `rs.status()` threw to decide "not yet initiated" — in this state it would have called `rs.initiate()` again, which fails ("already initialized") since `local.system.replset` already exists, and the job never actually recovers the node. This is why `rankstack-api` was crash-looping (464 restarts, all failing `init_db()`'s transaction-dependent Mongo calls) while the init Job showed `Completed` from days ago.
- **Live fix applied directly:** `rs.reconfig(rs.conf() with version+1, {force: true})` via `kubectl exec` — this immediately flipped the node from throwing to `rs.status().myState === 1` (`PRIMARY`). `rankstack-api` stopped crash-looping within seconds; watched 45+ seconds of continuous `/health` → `200 OK` with zero new restarts.
- **Chart fix (not yet merged):** `mongo-rs-init-job.yaml`'s script now checks `status.myState` explicitly — throws → still `rs.initiate()`; exists but not PRIMARY(1)/SECONDARY(2) → forces the same reconfig-with-bumped-version that fixed the live node; otherwise no-op. Committed to `fix/mongo-rs-init-self-heal`, pushed, opened as `upayanmazumder/rankstack#93` — `main` on that repo is branch-protected ("changes must be made through a pull request"), so it needs a merge (by the owner or a reviewer) before ArgoCD picks it up. Without this chart fix, the live fix is a one-time manual repair — if the pod restarts and lands in the same broken state again, it will need the same manual `rs.reconfig` until the PR merges.
- **Verification:** `kubectl exec -n rankstack deploy/rankstack-mongo -- mongosh --eval 'rs.status()'` → `myState: 1, stateStr: PRIMARY`; `rankstack-api` pod logs show continuous `GET /health → 200 OK`, no new restarts after the fix (last restart timestamp is from before the fix); `kubectl get application de-rankstack -n argocd` → `Synced`/`Healthy`; `helm template` used to render the new job YAML and confirm valid Helm/Go-template syntax before pushing.
- **Reason:** completes the S13 rankstack remediation using the surgical self-heal fix (chosen over the plan's originally-suggested StatefulSet conversion) — avoids any PVC/data migration risk since the existing Deployment+Service topology and its `rankstack-mongo-data` PVC are untouched.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** the live `rs.reconfig` is not destructive (same single-member membership list, only the config version incremented) — no data was touched. If PR #93 is rejected, `git revert`/close it; the underlying job script reverts to its previous (non-self-healing) behavior, and any future recurrence of this exact broken state would again need a manual `kubectl exec` repair.
