### ~09:20 IST — S2 applied: Hetzner firewall/backups/protection live, R2 buckets created — plus a self-inflicted state-loss mistake, recovered
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), owner explicitly confirmed "Yes, apply now" after reviewing the plan summary from the previous entry.
- **Type:** `terraform apply` (twice, see mistake below) — first real infra-changing Terraform run in this repo.
- **Change (intended, successful)**: `Apply complete! Resources: 5 imported, 5 added, 3 changed, 0 destroyed.` — Hetzner server `122385524` now has `backups=true` (window `14-18`), `delete_protection=true`, `rebuild_protection=true`; both primary IPs now `auto_delete=false` + `delete_protection=true`; a permissive `hcloud_firewall` (mirrors today's de-facto-open state, changes no traffic) created and attached; R2 buckets `vps-backups`/`vps-tfstate` created; `cloudflare_zone_settings_override` created importing the zone's real current settings (`ssl=strict`, `always_use_https=on`, `min_tls_version=1.0` — see the previous entry's finding, `automatic_https_rewrites=on`).
- **⚠️ Mistake made and corrected in the same sitting**: immediately after the successful apply, ran the routine `rm -rf .terraform terraform.tfstate*` cleanup (the same command used after every prior plan-only run this session) — except this time an actual `apply` had just run, so this **deleted the only copy of Terraform's state** for the 5 imported + 5 newly-created resources, before the planned R2 state-backend migration had happened. The actual Hetzner/Cloudflare resources were never at risk (verified immediately via direct API calls: backups/protection/firewall-attachment/R2-buckets all confirmed live and correct) — this was purely a loss of Terraform's own bookkeeping, not infrastructure or data loss.
- **Recovery**: re-ran `terraform init` + `terraform import` for each of the 5 newly-created resources against their real IDs (`hcloud_firewall.vps` → `11692068`, `hcloud_firewall_attachment.vps` → `11692068`, `cloudflare_r2_bucket.{vps_backups,vps_tfstate}` → `<account-id>/<bucket-name>`). One resource — `cloudflare_zone_settings_override` — **does not support `terraform import` at all** in this provider version; since it's a settings-overlay (not a discrete created object), re-running `apply` for it is a safe, idempotent re-PUT of the same already-live values, not a duplicate-creation risk. Final reconciling `terraform apply`: `Resources: 5 imported, 1 added, 0 changed, 0 destroyed` — full state now correctly tracks all 9 real resources (`terraform state list` confirmed). This time the local `terraform.tfstate` was **not** deleted — it stays local (already `.gitignore`d) until the R2 backend migration below happens.
- **Verification (post-recovery)**: SSH to the node works; `https://argocd.upayan.dev` returns `200`; `kubectl get nodes`/`kubectl get applications` both work normally; ArgoCD health baseline unchanged (27 Synced/Healthy, same 4 pre-existing issues) — the firewall attach caused zero live disruption, confirming the "permissive, mirrors current state" design worked as intended.
- **Still pending — R2 backend migration NOT done**: `terraform/backend.tf`'s commented S3-compatible backend block needs an **R2 API token** (S3-compatible access key/secret pair, generated separately in the Cloudflare dashboard under R2 → "Manage R2 API Tokens") — this is a different credential from the Cloudflare API token already supplied, which doesn't grant S3-compatible access on its own. Until that's supplied, `terraform.tfstate` stays local only, backed up to `/tmp/tfstate-backup.json` on the workstation for now (not committed, not in the repo).
- **Cost impact**: Hetzner server backups (~20% of server cost, confirm current billing), two new small R2 buckets (free tier likely covers this — see `docs/backups.md`), no new compute.
- **Rollback**: firewall — detach via `terraform apply` with the attachment resource removed, or Hetzner console "remove from server" (never `terraform destroy`, all imported resources carry `prevent_destroy`). Backups/protection flags — can be toggled back via config + apply if ever needed, though there's no reason to. R2 buckets — empty, safe to delete via console if truly unwanted (not recommended, they're the target backup destination).

### ~07:50 IST — break-glass + git commit: completed the mochi cutover — `default/mochi` retired, `mochi/mochi` live and confirmed working
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), completing the S10 mochi onboarding from the previous entry.
- **Type:** break-glass kubectl (`scale`, `patch app`, `delete deployment/service/secret`) + git commit (syncPolicy update to match).
- **Trigger:** the previous entry deliberately left `de-mochi` on manual sync to avoid running two live bot instances against the same Discord token simultaneously. Proceeding to full cutover now: zero data at risk (stateless bot, no PVC), the action is trivially reversible until the old Deployment is actually deleted (`kubectl scale --replicas=1` would have undone it), and the task's own standing principle is explicit — "Downtime is acceptable. Data loss is not." This is exactly the "brief downtime while the default copy is removed" the plan itself anticipated for this item.
- **Change, in order:**
  1. `kubectl scale deployment mochi -n default --replicas=0` — old pod terminated, freeing the Discord token for the new instance. **Rollback point**: if the new instance had failed to come up, `--replicas=1` would have restored the old one within seconds; nothing else had been touched yet.
  2. `kubectl patch app de-mochi -n argocd --type merge -p '{"spec":{"syncPolicy":{"automated":{"prune":true,"selfHeal":true}}}}'` + hard refresh — `de-mochi` synced immediately: new `mochi` namespace, `Deployment/mochi` `1/1 Running`, 0 restarts.
  3. **Verified actual bot functionality**, not just pod health — `kubectl logs deploy/mochi -n mochi` shows `logging in using static token` → `Shard ID None has connected to Gateway` → `Logged in as Mochi#5595` → a webhook POST to Discord returning `204`. This is real, confirmed end-to-end bot operation, not an assumption.
  4. Only after that confirmation: `kubectl delete deployment/mochi service/mochi secret/mochi-env -n default` — the old unmanaged copy and its now-redundant secret (values already safely committed to git as `k8s/apps/de/mochi/secret.yaml`) are gone. `kubectl get all -n default -l app=mochi` → `No resources found`.
  5. Committed `k8s/argocd/applications/de/mochi.yaml`'s `syncPolicy` (now `automated: {prune: true, selfHeal: true}`) to git so the live-patched state matches the committed desired state and stays self-healing going forward — the same "align git to what's actually live and correct" pattern as the Grafana password fix, not a live/git drift left dangling.
- **Reason:** completes the mochi item of S10; `mochi` is now the second app (after `ns-68f3b786edf06be2b1f011a4-nginx`) counted down toward keel's retirement — keel itself is not yet removed (the nginx orphan is still its only remaining consumer, untouched in this entry).
- **Verification:** see step 3 (Discord login + webhook success) and step 4 (`No resources found` in `default`); `kubectl get application de-mochi -n argocd` → `Synced`/`Healthy`; overall cluster health unchanged otherwise.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** the old Deployment/Service/Secret are gone (not just scaled down) — a full rollback would mean re-creating them from git history (`git show <old-sha>:...` doesn't exist since they were never git-managed; would need to reconstruct from this changelog's description or `kubectl` history) — treat `mochi/mochi` as the only copy going forward. Its manifests are now in git (`k8s/apps/de/mochi/`), which the old unmanaged copy never had — this is a net durability improvement, not a new risk.

### ~07:35 IST — git commit: S10 — onboard `mochi` into GitOps (additive only — old `default/mochi` + keel left untouched)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), the additive half of S10's "onboard mochi" item in `docs/plans/2026-09-27-architecture-review.md` §17 S10 ("Onboard `mochi` as `k8s/apps/de/mochi/` plus an explicit Application with argocd-image-updater annotations... check its volumes/env first... New namespace `mochi` → brief downtime while the `default` copy is removed").
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`). The `Application` object itself will sync (ArgoCD always applies its own CRs), but its `syncPolicy` deliberately has **no `automated:` block** — the `Application` exists and is visible in the ArgoCD UI/CLI, but nothing inside `k8s/apps/de/mochi/` (the actual Deployment) deploys until someone runs a manual sync. Does **not** touch the existing unmanaged `default/mochi` Deployment or its keel annotations.
- **Trigger:** continuing S10 with the one sub-item that's purely additive (create new, don't touch/delete old) rather than the retirement items, which all involve removing a live object.
- **Investigation before writing anything** (per the plan's explicit instruction to check env/volumes first): read the live `default/mochi` Deployment (`ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/mochi:latest`, port 3000, `envFrom: mochi-env`, resources `100m/128Mi` request → `250m/256Mi` limit, no `imagePullSecrets` — the ghcr package is public) and Service (`ClusterIP`, `3000:3000`); confirmed **no Ingress exists** for mochi at all (it's a Discord-bot-style internal service, not web-facing) — so no `ingress.yaml`/DNS record needed, unlike a typical onboarding. Compared the live `Secret/mochi-env` key names against `docker/mochi.env`: both have exactly `BOT_TOKEN`, `DISCORD_WEBHOOK_URL` — no drift.
- **Change:**
  - `k8s/apps/de/mochi/{namespace.yaml,deployments.yaml,services.yaml,secret.yaml,kustomization.yaml}` — `namespace.yaml`/`services.yaml`/`deployments.yaml` reproduce the live spec exactly (image, port, resource requests/limits, node affinity) with one deliberate addition: `priorityClassName: low-priority` (matches this repo's convention for best-effort workloads like this one, per `AGENTS.md`) and the S11 `pod-security.kubernetes.io/warn=restricted` label (matching the other 14 app namespaces from the earlier S11 commit). `secret.yaml` was populated by reading the live `Secret/mochi-env` values directly via `kubectl get secret -o json` into the file — **the values never appeared in any tool output or this changelog entry**, only the two key names above.
  - `k8s/argocd/applications/de/mochi.yaml` — explicit `Application` (not ApplicationSet-discovered, same reason as `meghmitra`/`rankstack`: ApplicationSet-templated Applications can't receive image-updater annotations, per `AGENTS.md`'s documented gotcha) with `argocd-image-updater.argoproj.io/image-list: app=ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/mochi:latest` and `app.update-strategy: digest` — tracks the mutable `:latest` tag by digest, no `write-back-method` (defaults to in-cluster, zero git-write credential needed), replacing keel's `keel.sh/policy: force`/`trigger: poll` for this app once it's confirmed working and the old copy is retired.
  - `k8s/argocd/applicationsets/apps-de.yaml` — added the matching `exclude: true` generator entry for `k8s/apps/de/mochi` (required alongside the explicit Application, per `AGENTS.md`'s "Excluding a path" pattern, or the ApplicationSet would double-generate).
- **Reason:** gets `mochi` under GitOps management (git-visible desired state, ArgoCD `selfHeal`, image-updater digest tracking) without any downtime or risk to the currently-working `default/mochi` — the two can coexist (different namespaces, same image, independent Discord bot instances would both be live briefly, which needs a moment's thought before cutover — see "Not done" below).
- **Verification:** `kubectl kustomize k8s/apps/de/mochi` builds clean; every file individually YAML-parses; `python3 scripts/check-ports.py` still passes (mochi has no ports registry entry — it's ClusterIP-only, ingress-free, ephemeral, matching the registry's scope of externally-reachable ports only). `kubectl apply --dry-run=server` on the full kustomize output hit the well-known dry-run limitation where a namespace created in the same dry-run pass isn't visible to subsequent resources in that pass (confirmed this is a dry-run-only artifact, not a real manifest defect, by testing that even pre-dry-run-creating the namespace in a separate command doesn't help — dry-run never persists anything across invocations); ArgoCD's real sync order with `CreateNamespace=true` doesn't have this limitation.
- **⚠️ Not done (needs a moment's thought, not a credential)**: two live `mochi` bot instances (`default/mochi` via keel, `mochi/mochi` via ArgoCD) both connecting to the same Discord bot token **simultaneously** could cause duplicate message handling or gateway-connection conflicts depending on how the bot is written. **This is why `de-mochi`'s `syncPolicy` has no `automated:` block** — the Application won't deploy anything until a manual sync is run, which should only happen after `default/mochi` is scaled to 0 first (brief, controlled downtime, exactly what the plan anticipated) or the bot framework is confirmed to tolerate two gateway connections on one token. Recommended sequence: scale `default/mochi` to 0 → `kubectl -n argocd patch app de-mochi --type merge -p '{"spec":{"syncPolicy":{"automated":{"prune":true,"selfHeal":true}}}}'` (or `argocd app sync de-mochi`) → verify the bot responds in Discord → **then** delete the old `default/mochi` Deployment/Service/keel annotations and add the `automated:` block back to `mochi.yaml` in git so it stays self-healing going forward.
- **Cost impact:** none (same image, same resource footprint, one extra namespace).
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` before syncing; if already synced, `kubectl delete -n mochi deployment/mochi service/mochi` (or `argocd app delete de-mochi` with cascade) — `default/mochi` is completely untouched either way.

### ~07:10 IST — break-glass (owner-approved): reset Grafana admin password to the git-committed value, unblocking the stuck `monitoring` sync
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), **explicit owner confirmation obtained via `ask` before acting** — owner chose "Reset Grafana to the git password" over the two safer alternatives offered (align git to the live password, or leave it stuck until S5). Owner was told plainly this repeats the same action that silently overwrote a human-set password twice before (2026-08-20, 2026-08-29) and confirmed anyway.
- **Type:** break-glass kubectl (`exec ... grafana-cli admin reset-admin-password`, `rollout restart`, `patch job ... suspend`). No git change.
- **Trigger:** the `monitoring` Application had been stuck (`status.operationState.message: "waiting for completion of hook batch/Job/grafana-create-dev-user"`) since the previous S9 alert-rule commit — confirmed this blocks **every** future `k8s/monitoring/` change from reaching the live cluster, not just that one commit (the live `grafana` Deployment was still mounting a 22-day-old ConfigMap).
- **What happened, in order:**
  1. Read the git-committed value from `k8s/monitoring/grafana-secret.yaml` (`grafana-admin-credentials`, `admin`/`admin` — a long-known, non-sensitive placeholder, not a real secret being protected).
  2. `kubectl exec deploy/grafana -- grafana-cli admin reset-admin-password <value-from-live-secret>` — reported success, but a follow-up `curl -u <user:pass> still returned 401.
  3. Misdiagnosed initially as "reset didn't stick"; retried the reset and did a `rollout restart` of the Grafana Deployment — still 401.
  4. Checked `kubectl logs deploy/grafana` and found the real cause: **Grafana's brute-force login protection**, not a wrong password — `"too many consecutive incorrect login attempts for user - login for user temporarily blocked"`. The hook Job had been retrying every ~15-20s against the *old* (pre-reset) password for the ~20+ minutes it was stuck, and my own diagnostic `curl` attempts added more failed attempts, tripping the lockout.
  5. `kubectl patch job grafana-create-dev-user -n monitoring -p '{"spec":{"suspend":true}}'` — stopped the hook from continuing to hammer the login endpoint (and re-triggering the lockout) while it cleared.
  6. Waited ~60s, tested once (`curl -u <user:pass> → `200`, `{"id":1,"name":"Main Org."...}`) — confirmed the reset had actually succeeded all along; the repeated 401s were the lockout, not a bad password.
  7. Un-suspended the Job (`suspend: false`) — it completed cleanly on its very next run: `dev: ready (HTTP 412)`, `staging: ready (HTTP 412)`, all three public dashboard URLs confirmed present (`vcap-dev`, `vcap-staging`, `hello-kitty`).
- **Result:** `kubectl get application monitoring` → `Synced`/`Healthy` (was stuck `Running`/`OutOfSync` for the prior ~25 minutes). The alert ConfigMap from the previous two commits (`grafana-alerting-config-8gmffdf7c6`, containing all 14 rules including the 4 new ones) is now actually mounted by the live `grafana` Deployment — confirmed via `kubectl get deploy grafana -o yaml` and via Grafana's own `/api/v1/provisioning/alert-rules` API showing all 4 new rule UIDs present and loaded.
- **Reason:** owner-directed; unblocks not just this commit but the entire `monitoring` app's ability to receive future changes.
- **Verification:** see steps 6-7 above; `kubectl get application monitoring -n argocd -o jsonpath='{.status.sync.status} {.status.health.status}'` → `Synced Healthy`.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** N/A for the password value itself (it now matches git, which is the intended state going forward — "change it in git, never in the UI" per `docs/monitoring.md`'s target design, effective immediately even though the initContainer automation for this isn't built until S9's full fix). If a human needs to set a *different* live password again before S9 lands, expect this exact drift to recur — that's the underlying bug, not yet fixed, only unstuck this once.

  - `argocd-image-updater`: `0.*` → `0.14.0`

### ~06:00 IST — git commit: S11 partial — Pod Security Admission `warn=restricted` on all 14 app namespaces (warn-only, nothing enforced)
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), the safe first half of S11's PSA item in `docs/plans/2026-09-27-architecture-review.md` §17 S11 ("label app namespaces `pod-security.kubernetes.io/enforce=baseline`, `warn=restricted`; ... Roll out warn-only first, then enforce").
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — ArgoCD auto-syncs each `k8s/apps/*/namespace.yaml`.
- **Trigger:** `warn` mode is non-blocking by design — the API server only emits a `Warning:` message to whoever applies a violating pod spec (kubectl output, or ArgoCD's own apply logs), it never rejects or restarts anything. This is a genuinely safe increment distinct from `enforce`, which is **not** applied here — enforcing `restricted` (which requires `runAsNonRoot`, seccomp, dropped capabilities, no privilege escalation, etc.) against manifests that don't already set those fields would reject new pod creation, which none of these plain Deployments are hardened for yet. That step needs a per-namespace review first, not a blind rollout.
- **Change:** added `labels: {pod-security.kubernetes.io/warn: restricted}` to the 14 raw `namespace.yaml` files this repo owns directly: `bandit`, `bitvault`, `cas-api`, `dj-html-nodejs-web`, `learning-docker`, `learning-qwik`, `learning-react`, `meghmitra`, `smart-home-system-api`, `upayan-v5`, `cheatsheet`, `kodesphere`, `status-page`, `upayan-web`. **Not touched**: `monitoring`/`kube-system`/`argocd`/`cert-manager`/`keda` (platform namespaces, review explicitly calls out `monitoring`/`kube-system` as privileged — need separate consideration, not a blanket label), and `vcap-dev`/`vcap-staging`/`rankstack`/`hello-kitty` (namespace created by their external Helm charts via ArgoCD's `CreateNamespace=true`, not a `namespace.yaml` this repo owns — would need `syncPolicy.managedNamespaceMetadata` on the Application instead, a different, riskier mechanism not attempted here).
- **Reason:** surfaces exactly which pods would fail a future `enforce=restricted` rollout (as warnings, harmlessly) before that rollout happens — matches the review's own explicitly-staged approach.
- **Verification:** `kubectl kustomize` on all 14 app dirs + the full `k8s/` tree builds clean; `kubeconform -strict` passes all 143 rendered resources (134 valid, 9 CRDs skipped, 0 errors — same baseline count as every prior validation pass this session, confirming nothing else changed); `python3 scripts/check-ports.py` still passes; diff is exactly 14 files, 2 lines each, no other content touched.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` — removing a `warn`-only label has zero live effect either way (it was never enforcing anything).

### ~03:45 IST — git commits: S9 partial — Loki retention enforcement, Prometheus digest pin + size cap, node-exporter public-exposure fix
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), the safe subset of S9 (`docs/plans/2026-09-27-architecture-review.md`) — deliberately excludes the Grafana admin-password fix, which requires the owner to be present to verify the new login path first (see the "not done" note below; that specific change has already caused two documented incidents when applied without a human confirming the new credential works, per §13's root-cause analysis).
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — ArgoCD auto-syncs `k8s/monitoring/`.
- **Trigger:** three independently-actionable, credential-free, git-only S9 items with no relationship to the Grafana password-reset risk:
  1. **Loki retention not enforced** (`k8s/monitoring/loki-configmap.yaml`): the config only set `table_manager.retention_period: 168h`, which does not enforce deletion for `boltdb-shipper`+`filesystem` storage on Loki 2.9.10 — retention enforcement for that backend requires `compactor.retention_enabled: true`. Matches the review's "Loki chunks 5.2 GB, retention not enforced" finding.
  2. **`prom/prometheus:latest` floating tag** (`k8s/monitoring/prometheus-deployment.yaml`): pinned to the exact digest already running live (`sha256:efd719c99…`, read via `kubectl get pod -o jsonpath='{.status.containerStatuses[0].imageID}'` immediately before the edit) — zero behavior change today, closes the "unreviewed image reaches prod via selfHeal" gap for this component (S11 principle, applied opportunistically). Also added `--storage.tsdb.retention.size=4GB` as a size-based safety net alongside the existing 7-day time-based retention.
  3. **node-exporter publicly exposed, no auth** (`k8s/monitoring/node-exporter-daemonset.yaml`): `hostNetwork: true` + `hostPort: 9100` bound the metrics endpoint to the node's public IP (confirmed live via `ss -Hltnp` showing `*:9100` in the S0-era audit, and the pod's own IP showing as `138.201.157.147` today). Removed both — node-exporter only ever needed the `/proc`/`/sys`/`/` hostPath mounts (already present) to read host metrics, never the host network namespace. The Service (`node-exporter-service.yaml`) is headless (`clusterIP: None`), so Prometheus's static scrape target resolves straight to the pod's own IP regardless of network mode — internal scraping is unaffected, only the public exposure is removed.
- **Reason:** all three are additive/corrective config changes with no secrets, no credentials, and no relationship to the risky Grafana rollout — reversible via `git revert`, matching the "low-risk, reversible, make the call" bar.
- **Verification:** `kubectl kustomize k8s/monitoring` builds clean; `kubeconform -strict` passes all 40 rendered resources; grepped the rendered output to confirm `retention_enabled: true` (×2, compactor config), `--storage.tsdb.retention.size=4GB` present, and `hostNetwork` count = 0. Pushed and hard-refreshed; will confirm post-sync: Loki pod restarts cleanly and its logs show no config-parse errors; Prometheus pod restarts on the new pinned digest and `/api/v1/targets` still shows `node-exporter` `up`; node-exporter's new pod gets a normal pod-CIDR IP (not the node's public IP) and `ss -Hltnp` on the node no longer shows `*:9100`.
- **Cost impact:** none.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>` for any of the three independently.
- **Not done (deliberately deferred, needs the owner present):** the Grafana admin-password initContainer fix and the PostSync-hook→plain-Job conversion — applying either without you available to verify the new login path first repeats the exact incident pattern documented in `docs/monitoring.md` (2026-08-20, 2026-08-29: `grafana-cli admin reset-admin-password` silently overwrote a human-set password). Loki's compactor retention also touches only *new* deletion behavior going forward — existing chunks already older than 7 days will be deleted on the next compaction cycle (~10 min), which is expected but noting it here since it's a one-way data-loss action on Loki's own disposable (class C, no-backup) log data.

### ~02:45 IST — git commit: S13 follow-up — learning-react memory plateaued at the first fix's ceiling, raised further with real margin
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), immediate follow-up to the previous entry after observing the live pod post-fix.
- **Type:** git commit (this repo, `main`) — ArgoCD auto-syncs.
- **Trigger:** after the previous fix (limit 128Mi→256Mi) synced, `kubectl top pod` showed the pod settling at **255Mi/256Mi and 201m/200m** — plateaued (not still climbing, so likely not a leak) but with essentially zero headroom against the new limit, which risked repeating the exact same OOM loop just at a higher ceiling. Watched for ~4 minutes (3m42s uptime, 0 restarts, memory flat at 255Mi) before concluding the working set is genuinely ~250Mi, not a leak trending toward infinity.
- **Change:** `resources.requests` 50m/64Mi → 100m/192Mi, `resources.limits` 200m/256Mi → 300m/384Mi (≈50% headroom over the observed ~255Mi/201m plateau, matching the review's "peak + 30%" guidance with extra margin since the first attempt's 30%-over-the-*old*-limit undershot the real working set).
- **Reason:** a limit that sits exactly at the observed working set isn't a fix, it's the same crash loop deferred — real margin is required.
- **Verification:** pushed; will re-check `kubectl top pod -n learning-react` and restart count after sync to confirm memory now sits comfortably under the new 384Mi ceiling.
- **Cost impact:** negligible, single-node cluster, low-priority class.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <sha>`.

Mirrored the node's cached `quay.io/minio/minio:RELEASE.2025-04-22T22-12-26Z` (only version still pullable — upstream now 401s on both `docker.io` and `quay.io` for this tag and `:latest`) via `k3s ctr images export` → scp → `skopeo copy docker-archive:… docker://ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/mirror/minio:RELEASE.2025-04-22T22-12-26Z`. **Push failed**: `denied: permission_denied: The token provided does not match expected scopes` — the `gh` CLI token used for registry auth lacks `write:packages`. Tarball (64 MB, sha256 `43a4e677…`) is safe in the backup bundle on both the node and the workstation meanwhile; vcap/bitvault MinIO pods must not be restarted/rescheduled until the ghcr push succeeds (S13 dependency).
- **Reason:** every later stage (S6 network lockdown, S8 storage migration, S10 orphan retirement) can destroy data if interrupted; this stage guarantees a Retain-protected PV plus an independent off-cluster, off-VM copy exists first, per the brief's non-negotiable data-safety rules.
- **Verification:** `kubectl get pv -o custom-columns=…` shows all 9 targeted PVs `Retain`; `pg_restore --list` succeeded on all 4 `.dump` files; `sha256sum -c` clean on the synced bundle; `skopeo inspect` of the mirror still 404s (push not yet done — tracked as open item, see below).
- **Cost impact:** none (no new billed resources; ghcr package once pushed is free/private).
- **Rollback:** N/A — additive/non-destructive only. `Retain` can be reverted to `Delete` per-PV if ever needed (`kubectl patch pv <name> -p '{"spec":{"persistentVolumeReclaimPolicy":"Delete"}}'`), but there is no reason to.
- **Not done in this pass (blocked, owner input needed):**
  - `rankstack-mongo` dump failed (`NotPrimaryOrSecondary`, both plain and `directConnection=true`) — matches the known `InvalidReplicaSetConfig` bug (S13). Per the brief, stopping the pod to tar its data directory needs explicit confirmation before running — not done.
  - ghcr mirror push blocked on a token with `write:packages` (or `packages:write` fine-grained) scope — needs owner-side `gh auth refresh -s write:packages` (interactive device-code browser approval) or a new PAT.
  - Hetzner-side owner actions from S0 item 2 (enable server backups, delete/rebuild protection on server + both volumes + both primary IPs, `auto_delete=false`, manual snapshot `pre-architecture-2026-09`) — requires the Hetzner API token (currently only known to live in-cluster as a Secret, `k8s/hetzner-csi/secret.yaml`) or console access; not run without explicit owner go-ahead per the brief's confirmation gate.

### ~00:50 IST — git commits (rankstack repo) `94d9a56` + (this repo) `15abd9c` + break-glass: moved rankstack's k8s manifests into a Helm chart in the app repo, fixed a stale field-ownership bug found live
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), user follow-up after the previous entry's raw-kustomize
  onboarding: "shouldnt manifests live in rankstack repo with helm properly n argo maintain it?"
  -> agent recommended against it (single environment, no benefit over the raw-manifest pattern
  23/24 other apps use; VCAP is the only precedent and only needed Helm for its 3-environment
  split) -> user: "i wanna showcase ik kubernetes so pls do" (explicit override, understood as a
  portfolio/demonstration goal, not a correctness fix — proceeded as directed).
- **Type:** 2 git commits (`upayanmazumder/rankstack` PR #51 squash-merged as `94d9a56`; this
  repo `15abd9c`) + break-glass kubectl (stale field-ownership patch, described below) + a
  temporary repo-ruleset edit on `upayanmazumder/rankstack` (also described below).
- **Change:**
  - New `upayanmazumder/rankstack` `k8s/chart/`: a proper Helm chart (`Chart.yaml`, `values.yaml`
    with safe non-secret defaults, `templates/` for namespace/secret/mongo/mongo-rs-init-job
    (Sync hook, wave 1)/redis/api (wave 2)/ingress) — a direct templated port of the raw
    manifests from the previous entry, same sync-wave fix, same tcpSocket probes, same resource
    sizing (including the live 128Mi/256Mi rs-init bump that was never committed to git before —
    caught and carried forward correctly while porting).
  - This repo: deleted the raw `k8s/apps/de/rankstack/{namespace,secret,mongo,
    mongo-rs-init-job,redis,api,ingress,kustomization}.yaml`; added
    `k8s/apps/de/rankstack/values/prod.yaml` (the one real override, `CORS_ORIGINS`) — same
    chart-in-app-repo / values-in-platform-repo split as `k8s/apps/de/vcap/values/
    backend-staging.yaml`. Rewrote `k8s/argocd/applications/de/rankstack.yaml` as a multi-source
    Application (`sources: [{repoURL: rankstack, path: k8s/chart, helm.valueFiles: [$values/...]},
    {repoURL: vps, ref: values}]`), image-updater annotations switched from kustomize-style
    (`image-list` only) to Helm-style (added `api.helm.image-name: image.repository`,
    `api.helm.image-tag: image.tag`) so the same zero-write-back digest-tracking on `:edge`
    still works against a Helm parameter override instead of a kustomize `images:` override.
    Added `https://github.com/upayanmazumder/rankstack` to the `de` `AppProject`'s `sourceRepos`
    (required — Applications can only source from repos their project allows). Kept the
    `apps-de.yaml` ApplicationSet exclude entry for `k8s/apps/de/rankstack` (directory still
    exists — now holds only the values overlay, but an un-excluded ApplicationSet would still
    try, and fail, to render it as a bare Kustomize app) — comment updated to explain why.
  - **Merge process, `upayanmazumder/rankstack`:** discovered mid-session that repo now has a
    branch ruleset (`required_deployments: [Production]`, `current_user_can_bypass: never`,
    `created_at` timestamp matches exactly when `vercel link` was run earlier in this session —
    root-caused to Vercel's GitHub integration auto-creating it) blocking direct pushes to `main`
    and blocking PR merges without a Production-environment deployment check on the exact PR
    commit (which a chart-only PR never triggers, since Vercel only builds `app/`). Asked the
    user how to proceed rather than silently working around a security control on someone else's
    repo; user chose "temporarily disable that one rule, merge, re-enable it." Backed up the
    full ruleset JSON (`gh api repos/upayanmazumder/rankstack/rulesets/23891256`) before touching
    it, PUT a version with only the `required_deployments` rule removed (kept `deletion`,
    `non_fast_forward`, `pull_request`), merged PR #51, then PUT the exact original backup back
    immediately. Verified restored: `rules: [deletion, non_fast_forward, pull_request,
    required_deployments]` — matches the pre-edit list exactly.
  - **Break-glass, stale field-ownership bug found live:** after pushing both repos and syncing,
    `argocd app sync de-rankstack` failed: `` `kustomize edit set image ghcr.io/.../api:edge@sha256:...`
    failed ... Missing kustomization file `` — even though the Application's git-committed spec
    now used `sources:` (Helm), not the old `source:` (Kustomize). Root cause: the previous
    entry's `argocd-image-updater` in-cluster write-back (zero-git-write-back "argocd" method)
    had patched `spec.source.kustomize.images` directly onto the **live** Application object
    under its own field-manager identity; when root's server-side-apply tried to replace
    `source:` with the new `sources:` field, SSA left the old `spec.source` in place because
    another field-manager (image-updater) still owned part of it — classic SSA multi-manager
    field-removal gap, not something a normal `kubectl apply`/git push can clear on its own.
    **Fix:** `kubectl -n argocd patch application de-rankstack --type=json
    -p='[{"op":"remove","path":"/spec/source"}]'` force-cleared it, then `argocd app sync root`
    to push the correct `sources:` field immediately (confirmed via `kubectl get application
    de-rankstack -o jsonpath='{.spec.sources}'` showing the expected two-entry array, and
    `{.spec.source}` empty) before retrying the rankstack sync, which then succeeded cleanly.
- **Reason:** explicit user request to demonstrate Helm + multi-source Argo CD patterns despite
  the added complexity not being strictly needed for rankstack's single-environment shape (agent
  recommendation stands unchanged for any *future* single-env app — this was a deliberate,
  informed exception, not a new default).
- **Verification (full live state, same checks as the previous entry, re-run against the new
  Helm-sourced deployment):**
  - `helm lint k8s/chart` — 0 failed. `helm template` + `kubectl apply --dry-run=client` — 11/11
    resources schema-valid, both against chart defaults alone and against chart+this repo's real
    `prod.yaml` values file (simulating exactly what Argo CD's multi-source render produces).
  - Structural diff (Python, normalized by `(kind, name)`) between the old raw-kustomize render
    and the new chart render: identical resource set, only 2 of 11 resources differing at all,
    and only in expected ways (`CORS_ORIGINS` default vs. the values-overlay-supplied prod value;
    Job field key ordering, semantically identical).
  - `kubectl -n argocd exec deploy/argocd-server -- argocd app sync de-rankstack` completed
    without the earlier error; `kubectl get application de-rankstack` -> `Synced` / `Healthy`.
  - `kubectl -n rankstack get pods` — `rankstack-api` rolled to a fresh pod (`1/1 Running`, image
    from the new Helm source) with **zero downtime** (old pod stayed `1/1 Running` throughout the
    rollout); `rankstack-mongo-rs-init` `Completed` (sync-wave 1 fired correctly again, this time
    from the chart's rendered Job); `rankstack-mongo`/`rankstack-redis` unaffected, still
    `1/1 Running` from before (no restart needed — chart output for those was byte-identical).
  - `mongosh rs.status()` -> `PRIMARY` (still initiated, unaffected by the source-type switch).
  - `curl https://api-rankstack.upayan.dev/health` -> `200 {"status":"ok"}`.
  - `curl -X POST .../users` through the public HTTPS endpoint -> real document created (Mongo
    `_id` returned), immediately deleted via `mongosh db.users.deleteOne(...)` (test artifact).
  - `curl -X OPTIONS .../users -H "Origin: https://rankstack.upayan.dev"` ->
    `access-control-allow-origin: https://rankstack.upayan.dev` present — CORS still correct
    after the source-type switch.
  - `docker pull ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/rankstack/api:edge` (anonymous, `docker logout` first) and
    `:sha-66c59a2` both succeed, same digest (`sha256:2966767f...`) — package confirmed public
    (user: "its public"), image-updater's registry reads will no longer `denied`.
- **Cost impact:** none (same live workloads, same resource requests as the previous entry — this
  was a source-of-truth relocation, not a resize).
- **Rollback:** `git revert 94d9a56` in `upayanmazumder/rankstack` removes `k8s/chart/`;
  `git revert 15abd9c` in this repo restores the raw kustomize manifests and the old
  single-source `Application`. Both must land together (a reverted Application spec pointing at

## 2026-09-23

### ~22:50 IST — git commits `f204bb4`..`4cdda9c` + break-glass: onboarded `rankstack` (upayanmazumder/rankstack) to DE region, found and fixed 3 live deployment bugs
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), user request: "refer to ~/dev/upayanmazumder/vps to understand
  how to deploy. keep manifests on repo with k8s argocd correctly" (backend already had a GHCR
  CI workflow from an earlier turn in the same session; frontend deploys to Vercel separately,
  not this cluster) -> "dont use atlas" (in-cluster MongoDB, not Atlas, unlike every other
  Mongo-using app in this cluster).
- **Type:** 4 git commits (this repo) + break-glass kubectl (ArgoCD/ApplicationSet naming
  collision recovery, stuck-operation unblock, manual verification) — see Change/Verification.
- **Trigger:** none of the prior work in this repo touched `rankstack`; onboarding from scratch,
  first Mongo-in-cluster app on this VPS (every existing Mongo-using app — `bandit`, `cheatsheet`,
  `kodesphere`, `status-page` — uses Atlas `mongodb+srv://`, per explicit user instruction not
  followed here).
- **Change:**
  1. `f204bb4` — new `k8s/apps/de/rankstack/`: `namespace.yaml`; `secret.yaml`
     (`rankstack-api-env`, plaintext per repo policy — no passwords needed, Mongo/Redis run
     without auth, ClusterIP-only, matching the app's own `docker-compose.yml` exactly);
     `mongo.yaml` (`mongo:7 --replSet rs0`, 2Gi `local-path` PVC, `wiredTigerCacheSizeGB 0.25`
     capped explicitly for this shared node); `redis.yaml` (no PVC — app treats it as
     rebuildable cache, `maxmemory 100mb --allkeys-lru` bounds it); `mongo-rs-init-job.yaml`
     (idempotent `rs.initiate()`); `api.yaml` (`wait-for-mongo`/`wait-for-redis` initContainers,
     `init_db()` runs on every boot via the app's own FastAPI startup hook — no separate migrate
     initContainer needed); `ingress.yaml` (`api-rankstack.upayan.dev` only — no `web.yaml`, the
     frontend is Vercel, not this cluster, unlike every other multi-service app here).
     `k8s/argocd/applications/de/rankstack.yaml`: explicit Application (`de-meghmitra` pattern)
     for `argocd-image-updater` digest tracking on `ghcr.io/upayanmazumder/rankstack/api:edge`
     (public image, zero write-back). `apps-de.yaml`: excluded `k8s/apps/de/rankstack` from
     ApplicationSet auto-discovery (same reason as meghmitra). `GUIDE.md`: added inventory row.
  2. `45bf519`, `5e3421a` — **bug found live**: mongo's `mongosh`-exec liveness/readiness probes
     killed the container repeatedly (`command timed out: "mongosh ..." timed out after 1s`,
     4 restarts) — identical class of bug to meghmitra's `changelog/2026-08.md` ~13:00 IST entry
     (no `timeoutSeconds`, too tight for this shared 4-CPU node). `timeoutSeconds: 5` alone
     (`45bf519`) still wasn't enough (mongosh's own Node.js process spawn was the slow part, not
     the ping); switched both probes to `tcpSocket: 27017` (`5e3421a`) — lighter, suffices for
     what the initContainers/rs-init Job actually need (mongod accepting connections).
  3. `4cdda9c` — **second bug found live, a real deadlock**: the rs-init Job was a `PostSync`
     hook, which ArgoCD only runs once the *whole* Application is Healthy — but `rankstack-api`'s
     own readiness needs `init_db()` to succeed, which needs the replica set already initiated.
     Uvicorn/FastAPI blocks the ASGI app (including `/health`) from serving anything until
     `on_startup` handlers finish, so the api pod hung forever at "Waiting for application
     startup", the Deployment never went Healthy, and PostSync (which would have fixed the
     replica set) never fired — self-inflicted circular wait. Fixed by switching to
     `argocd.argoproj.io/hook: Sync` + `sync-wave: "1"` on the Job and pinning
     `sync-wave: "2"` on the api Deployment: wave 0 (mongo/redis, default) only needs to be
     Healthy for wave 1 (rs-init) to run; wave 2 (api) isn't even *applied* until wave 1
     succeeds, so api's own health can never gate the fix it depends on.
  4. **Break-glass, ArgoCD/ApplicationSet naming collision** (same bug meghmitra hit, per
     `changelog/2026-08.md` ~16:40 IST entry): my explicit Application and the ApplicationSet's
     auto-generated name (`de-{{basename}}` for path `k8s/apps/de/rankstack`) are identical by
     design (`de-rankstack`) — even though the exclude entry landed in the same commit
     (`f204bb4`), the `apps-de` ApplicationSet controller's own generator briefly raced ahead of
     that exclude, took ownership of my `de-rankstack` object (`ownerReferences` confirmed:
     `ApplicationSet/apps-de`), then on its next reconcile (now correctly excluding the path)
     issued a delete against the object it thought it owned — `metadata.deletionTimestamp` set,
     stuck behind `resources-finalizer.argocd.argoproj.io` with the finalizer never actually
     processed (application-controller logs kept showing normal re-syncs of the same Application,
     not finalizer-cascade-delete logic — root cause not fully understood, workaround applied).
     **Data-loss check before any action:** `kubectl -n rankstack get all,pvc,secret` — resources
     existed but were <1 min old (fresh empty PVC, no seeded data, no real traffic yet) — zero
     risk. **Fix:** `kubectl -n argocd rollout restart deploy/argocd-applicationset-controller`
     and `rollout restart statefulset/argocd-application-controller` (force both off stale
     state) did not clear it alone; `argocd app delete de-rankstack --cascade --yes` was also a
     no-op (object already had a deletionTimestamp, API treats re-delete as idempotent);
     `kubectl -n argocd patch application de-rankstack --type=json -p='[{"op":"remove","path":"/metadata/finalizers"}]'`
     force-cleared it — root's next reconcile (selfHeal) recreated `de-rankstack` cleanly within
     ~2s with **zero** `ownerReferences` this time (confirmed via `kubectl get application
     de-rankstack -o jsonpath='{.metadata.ownerReferences}'` — empty).
  5. **Break-glass, stuck sync operation + Job OOM (live iteration on fixes 2-3 above)**: while
     landing the probe/sync-wave fixes, `argocd app sync`/`terminate-op` CLI calls repeatedly hit
     `another operation is already in progress` — automated `selfHeal` kept re-triggering a new
     operation faster than my manual terminate+retry could win the race. Broke the loop with
     `kubectl apply --server-side --force-conflicts -f <(kubectl kustomize k8s/apps/de/rankstack)`
     directly (bypasses ArgoCD's operation queue; selfHeal sees the live state already matches
     desired and stops fighting). Separately, the rs-init Job itself OOMKilled 3x
     (`kubectl -n rankstack get pods -l job-name=... ` showed 3 `OOMKilled` pods) at its original
     64Mi memory limit — `mongosh`'s Node.js runtime needs more; bumped to 128Mi request / 256Mi
     limit (folded into `4cdda9c`'s working tree, applied live before commit via the same
     server-side-apply path, confirmed fixed, then committed). After the Job finally completed
     (`rs.initiate()` succeeded), the *already-hung* api pods (stuck since before fix #3 landed)
     needed a manual `kubectl -n rankstack rollout restart deployment/rankstack-api` — a hang
     doesn't self-heal from a config fix alone, only a fresh pod does.
- **Reason:** in-cluster Mongo/Redis per explicit user instruction (no Atlas); explicit
  Application (not ApplicationSet) is the only way to get `argocd-image-updater` digest tracking
  on this cluster (documented constraint, `apps-de.yaml` comment + `GUIDE.md` "Image Automation"
  section); sync-wave over PostSync is the correct fix for any future app with the same
  "app's own health depends on a one-shot init step" shape — worth remembering for meghmitra-style
  onboardings that need a migrate/init Job.
- **Verification (full live state, all commands re-run after the final fix, not just
  manifest validation):**
  - `kubectl kustomize k8s/apps/de/rankstack` + `kubectl apply --dry-run=client` — 11/11
    resources schema-valid, before every commit in this entry.
  - `kubectl -n rankstack get pods` — `rankstack-api` `1/1 Running`, `rankstack-mongo` `1/1
    Running`, `rankstack-redis` `1/1 Running`, `rankstack-mongo-rs-init` `0/1 Completed`. Zero
    unexpected restarts on the final pod generation.
  - `kubectl -n rankstack exec deploy/rankstack-mongo -- mongosh --eval 'rs.status()...'` ->
    `[{name: 'rankstack-mongo:27017', state: 'PRIMARY'}]`.
  - `curl https://api-rankstack.upayan.dev/health` (via `--resolve` to the node IP, DNS not
    required for this check) -> `200 {"status":"ok"}`.
  - `curl -X POST https://api-rankstack.upayan.dev/users -d '{...}'` -> `201`-equivalent, real
    document with a Mongo `_id` returned; immediately deleted via `mongosh
    db.users.deleteOne(...)` (test artifact, not real data).
  - `curl -X OPTIONS .../users -H "Origin: https://rankstack.upayan.dev"` ->
    `access-control-allow-origin: https://rankstack.upayan.dev` present (CORS working against
    the actual Vercel frontend origin, added to `api/main.py` in the app repo in an earlier turn
    of this same session).
  - `db.getCollectionNames()` -> all 5 (`users`, `teams`, `contests`, `problems`, `submissions`);
    `db.getCollectionInfos()...hasValidator` -> `true` on all 5; `db.users.getIndexes()` ->
    `uniq_email` present; `db.submissions.getIndexes()` -> `by_contest_and_submitter`,
    `by_problem` present — `init_db()` ran correctly against the now-initiated replica set.
- **Cost impact:** none (existing Hetzner VM). Summed requests: mongo 50m cpu/192Mi mem, redis
  10m/32Mi, api 50m/128Mi ≈ 110m cpu / 352Mi mem added to a node previously at 60%/65%
  requests-vs-allocatable (4 CPU / 7.9Gi) — negligible headroom impact. New PVC: 2Gi `local-path`.
- **Rollback:** `git revert 4cdda9c 5e3421a 45bf519 f204bb4` (in that order) removes the
  Application, the ApplicationSet exclude, and the `k8s/apps/de/rankstack/` manifests; ArgoCD
  prune then removes the namespace/Deployments/Services/Job/Ingress/Secret. `rankstack-mongo-pvc`
  is **not** cascade-deleted by prune (same PVC caveat as bitvault/meghmitra) — `kubectl delete
  pvc rankstack-mongo-pvc -n rankstack` needed to fully reclaim the 2Gi volume. No production data
  exists yet (only the one test user created and deleted during this entry's verification), so

## 2026-09-05

### ~18:15 IST — monitoring: added Grafana alert for failed Jobs in vcap-staging
- **Actor:** agent (omp session), part of a broader VCAP watchdog CronJob fix (see
  `VCAP-STAGING-BACKEND` commit in the "fix: resolve watchdog CronJob ModuleNotFoundError,
  add orphan-sweep CronJob" PR, and `VCAP-STAGING-FRONTEND`'s companion chunk-load-recovery PR).
- **Type:** git commit (this commit — see `git log --oneline -1 -- k8s/monitoring/grafana-alerting-rules.yaml`).
- **Trigger:** user reported `vcap-backend-staging-watchdog` CronJob pods in `Error` status
  (`kubectl get pods -n vcap-staging`). Root-caused to a stale `/opt/capstone` sys.path insert
  in `cron/watchdog.py` (pre-container VM path; container root is `/app`) — the CronJob had
  failed every 5-minute run for ~20h with zero alerting, discovered only because the user
  happened to run `kubectl get pods -w` for an unrelated frontend issue.
- **Change:** appended a new rule `vcap-staging-job-failed` to the existing `cluster-health`
  alert group in `k8s/monitoring/grafana-alerting-rules.yaml`:
  `max by (namespace, job_name) (kube_job_status_failed{namespace="vcap-staging"}) == 1`,
  `for: 0m`, `severity: critical`. Same query/reduce/threshold structure as the existing
  `pod-oomkilled`/`keda-lowpriority-scaling` rules in the same file. No new dashboard, no new
  contact point — reuses the existing provisioned alerting pipeline
  (`grafana-alerting-config` ConfigMap, mounted at `/etc/grafana/provisioning/alerting`).
- **Reason:** a CronJob repeatedly failing in this namespace had no observable signal outside
  manually running `kubectl get pods`. `kube_job_status_failed` is keyed by `job_name` (unique
  per scheduled run, not per CronJob), so a persistently-broken schedule will re-fire roughly
  once per cycle rather than staying in one continuous alert state — accepted as consistent
  with this file's existing per-resource-identity convention (e.g. `pod-crashloopbackoff` keys
  by pod name too), flagged as a follow-up if it proves noisy in practice.
- **Verification:** `kubectl kustomize k8s/monitoring` — rendered `grafana-alerting-config`
  ConfigMap content hash changed (`grafana-alerting-config-d8d564b4kg`), confirming Kustomize
  picks up the new rule and Grafana will re-provision on next sync (`kustomization.yaml`
  hashes this file's content into the ConfigMap name specifically so Grafana restarts and
  re-provisions whenever it changes). Not yet confirmed the alert actually fires against a
  live failing Job in-cluster — pending the ArgoCD sync of this commit and a live re-trigger of
  the (now-fixed) watchdog CronJob to confirm end-to-end.
- **Cost impact:** none — reuses existing Grafana/Prometheus resources, no new PVC/dashboard.
- **Rollback:** `git revert <this commit's sha>`.

### ~13:50 IST — vcap-staging: seeded passwords for 5 QA test accounts (`<faculty-test-account>*`) in Postgres
- **Actor:** agent (omp session).
- **Type:** break-glass kubectl (`kubectl exec` into `vcap-backend-staging-postgres`, direct
  `INSERT ... ON CONFLICT DO UPDATE` / `UPDATE` against `auth_password_credentials`). No git
  change — this is application data, not cluster config.
- **Trigger:** user asked to verify whether 6 named QA test accounts exist in the vcap-staging
  DB and can log in, referencing a prior dev's report of `403 password setup is required` for
  privileged roles and `401 invalid credentials` for a "reversed" faculty/student domain
  mapping.
- **Investigation:** confirmed via `psql` against `vcap-backend-staging-postgres` (db `vcap`)
  that all 6 identity rows exist (emails stored lowercase — the dev's mixed-case lookups were
  fine, Postgres querying was case-sensitive in his ad-hoc checks but the app's own queries use
  `lower(email)`). `auth_security_policies` shows `ADMIN`/`HOD`/`PROJECT_COORDINATOR`/
  `SUPERADMIN` all require `password_enabled=true` + `otp_required=true`; `FACULTY` requires
  password, OTP optional; `STUDENT` has `password_enabled=false` (Google-OAuth-only by design,
  matches `password authentication is disabled` response observed live). None of the 4
  admin-role principals had any row in `auth_password_credentials` at all (not just
  `password_setup_required=true` — no row), so the prior dev's "database is seeded correctly"
  claim was wrong; the identity rows existed but no one had ever completed password setup for
  them. Faculty (`<faculty-test-account>`) already had a working password credential (unknown
  plaintext, reset below so QA had a working credential).
- **Second bug found live-testing:** `HOD`/`PROJECT_COORDINATOR` admin rows
  (`ADM-C72E2144C43C6980D1812A6827335A4D`, `ADM-F1C99096610E249F88557AB64EDAACD8`) are linked
  to their own `faculty_id` row. Per `services/auth/internal/auth/store.go` `loadUsers`, when a
  linked faculty row exists the admin principal is merged/consumed and the effective principal
  for password-credential lookup becomes the **faculty** principal
  (`FAC-C72E2144C43C6980D1812A6827335A4D` / `FAC-F1C99096610E249F88557AB64EDAACD8`), not the
  admin one. Seeding under the admin principal alone left both accounts still returning
  `403 password setup is required` live even after the row existed — root-caused by reading
  `store.go` and confirmed by seeding the faculty-principal row instead, which fixed it.
- **Change:** upserted `auth_password_credentials` rows (Argon2id PHC hashes generated locally
  with the exact same params as `services/auth/internal/auth/password.go`:
  `m=65536,t=3,p=1`, verified live) for:
  - `admin` / `ADM-B00D3F477E2BAA53C9709F98927FAA76` (<admin-test-account>, admin)
  - `admin` / `ADM-DE4798EA7B3E3F096931C56F15DAEA60` (<superadmin-test-account>, superadmin)
  - `faculty` / `FAC-C72E2144C43C6980D1812A6827335A4D` (<hod-test-account>, hod — linked
    faculty principal, see above)
  - `faculty` / `FAC-F1C99096610E249F88557AB64EDAACD8` (<coordinator-test-account>,
    project_coordinator — linked faculty principal, see above)
  - `faculty` / `<faculty-test-account>` (<faculty-test-account>, professor — password reset for QA testing)
  All rows: `password_enabled=true`, `password_setup_required=false`. Left the now-unused
  `admin`-principal rows for the HOD/coordinator ids in place (harmless — never read once a
  linked faculty row exists). Did **not** seed a password for `<faculty-test-account>`
  (student) — `STUDENT` security policy has `password_enabled=false`, so a password row would
  never be read; that account only ever authenticates via Google OAuth by design.
- **Reason:** unblock QA login testing for all 6 roles without waiting on a real email-OTP round
  trip for initial password setup, and close out the specific 403/401 the prior dev hit.
- **Verification:** live `POST /api/auth/password/login` against
  `http://vcap-backend-staging-auth:8080` from an in-namespace pod (labelled to satisfy the
  `vcap-backend-staging-auth` NetworkPolicy, which only allows ingress from
  `app.kubernetes.io/part-of=vcap` pods or the `monitoring` namespace):
  - `<admin-test-account>` / role `ADMIN` → `200`, `requires_otp: true`, `factor: EMAIL_OTP`
    (correct — policy requires OTP after password).
  - `<hod-test-account>` / role `HOD` → `200`, `requires_otp: true`, `factor: EMAIL_OTP`,
    resolved principal `faculty`/`FAC-C72E2144...` in the response (confirms the merge
    behavior above).
  - `<faculty-test-account>` / role `FACULTY` → `200`, full session issued directly (no OTP step —
    matches `FACULTY` policy `otp_required=false`).
  - `<superadmin-test-account>` (SUPERADMIN) and `<coordinator-test-account>` (PROJECT_COORDINATOR) hit
    transient connection resets during this same window because the single `auth` replica was
    mid-`OOMKilled` restart (see ~14:25 entry above) — not re-confirmed with a clean 200 in
    this session; re-test after the memory bump syncs.
  - Confirmed the very first round of "401 invalid credentials" results for all 5 accounts was
    my own test-request bug (omitted the required `role` field, defaulting to `STUDENT` and
    failing role selection), not an application defect — re-tested with `role` set and got the
    results above.
- **Cost impact:** none (data-only change on existing PVC-backed Postgres).
- **Rollback:** `UPDATE auth_password_credentials SET password_hash='', password_enabled=false,
  password_setup_required=true WHERE (principal_type,principal_id) IN (('admin',
  'ADM-B00D3F477E2BAA53C9709F98927FAA76'), ('admin','ADM-DE4798EA7B3E3F096931C56F15DAEA60'),
  ('faculty','FAC-C72E2144C43C6980D1812A6827335A4D'),
  ('faculty','FAC-F1C99096610E249F88557AB64EDAACD8'), ('faculty','<faculty-test-account>'));` — no git
  state to revert since this is application data, not cluster config.
